What is this name of Christ which is said to be written on the stone of the Gospel? “In the beginning was the Word, and the Word was with God, and the Word was God” (Jn. 1:1); and again: “I and the Father are one” (Jn. 10:30); and the same evangelist says in another place: “Because he not only broke the sabbath, but also called God his Father, making himself equal to God” (Jn. 5:18). If Christ made himself equal to God, why does Arius say that the Son is less than the Father? This, then, is the name written on the stone, which no one knows except the one who receives it; because no one is able to know the omnipotence, divinity, and equality of the Son with the Father, except the one to whom the teaching of the Gospel has been given. But there is something else that troubles us; for a little above he said: “To the one who overcomes I will give hidden manna, and I will give him a white stone, and on the stone your name, which no one knows except the one who receives it.” If, therefore, only to those who overcome the Devil are given both the manna and the stone and the knowledge of the name of Christ, then the wicked who do not overcome the Devil but are overcome by him, although they seem to have received baptism and the faith of Christ, remain strangers to this gift. But someone says: How is it that each learned person, educated in the divine Scriptures, does not know the mystery of the name of Christ, even though he loves the world and lives in pleasure? To such a one I will set in opposition the likeness of two men who possess a great quantity of honey, of whom one was accustomed to eat from it, while the other, compelled by some necessity, abstained from eating it in every way. Which of these seems to you to have known the taste of honey more fully? You will indeed answer me that it is the one who was accustomed to enjoy its sweetness. And yet if you had asked each of them about the honey, of what taste it was, each would have admitted that it is most sweet; but that man would say what he had heard, this one what he had experienced: the tongue of the former would speak what his throat did not know, but that of the latter what delight had taught it. The man, then, who possessed honey not for use but to increase his profits represents those who from the knowledge of the divine Scriptures pursue temporal glory more than eternal; the man, however, who possessed honey in order continually to enjoy its sweetness represents those who strive to learn the commandments of God not for temporal glory but for the love of God, so that through their practice they may merit to attain eternal glory. To the former the commandments of God, although they preach them, are not sweet, because they love not the sweetness of the heavenly homeland but the earthly sweetness that produces bitterness; but to the latter the hope of the heavenly homeland makes the commandments of God sweet, as the Psalmist says: “How sweet are your words to my throat, Lord, more than honey and the honeycomb to my mouth” (Ps. 118:103)! Therefore only those know the name of God who keep the commandments of God; but those who love the world, who cast themselves down into certain vices, who fulfill the desires of their flesh, do not know the name of God, because they cast out from their mind his fear and his love. In another sense: by the stone and the name written on it can be signified the blessedness of the heavenly homeland; so that by the stone the heavenly homeland is figured, and by the name written on it the blessedness of the heavenly homeland. This name, therefore, no one knows except the one who receives the stone; because how great that heavenly blessedness is no one can know except the one who has merited to reach it, as Isaiah the prophet says: “Eye has not seen, O God, besides you, what you have prepared for those who wait for you” (Isa. 64:4).

Revelation 2:18 And to the angel of the Church in Thyatira write: These things says the Son of God, who has eyes like a flame of fire, and whose feet are like fine brass.

What these things mean has been said above. (see Rev. 1:13b-16)

Revelation 2:19 I know your works, and love, and faith, and ministry, and your patience, and your last works, more than the first.

Here he is praised in every respect, but, as the following words show, only the fault of silence is rebuked in him.

Revelation 2:20 But I have this against you, that you allow the woman Jezebel, who calls herself a prophetess, to teach and to seduce my servants to commit fornication and to eat things sacrificed to idols.

This woman, who is compared to the most wicked Jezebel, claimed falsely to be a prophetess so that she could more easily introduce false teaching into the people of God under the name of religion; and just as Jezebel compelled the people of God to abandon the worship of God and to venerate idols, so this woman also tried, through corrupt doctrine, to separate the people of God from the worship of God, that is, from true faith. For the soul commits fornication whenever it departs from God, its Creator. He calls “idol-offering” that which was consecrated to idols. For this woman taught that it did not harm the faithful if they ate meat that had been sacrificed to idols.

Revelation 2:21 And I gave her time to repent, and she does not want to repent of her acts of fornication.

Just as blessed Job says of the wicked man: God gave him a place for repentance, and he abused it in pride (Job 24:23).

Revelation 2:22-23a Behold, I will cast her onto a bed, and those who commit adultery with her, into great tribulation, unless they repent, and I will put her children to death.

Behold, for the woman and her children no place of repentance is promised, because they have despised the time of repentance granted to them by God; but to those who were committing adultery with her, a place for repentance is still promised again. Therefore, those who were committing adultery with her seem to me to signify those who, favoring her depraved proclamations, had not yet been wholly plunged into the pit of her wickedness; but by her children are signified those who, having been completely deceived by her, in no way wished to receive the remedy of correction. By the bed into which the woman is to be cast, hell [infernus] is signified, in which the rich man is buried, of whom the Lord says in the Gospel: “But the rich man died and was buried in hell [inferno]” (Luke 16:22). By the great tribulation, in which those who commit adultery with her are to be placed, unless they repent; and by the death in which her children are to be killed, the same punishment of hell [inferni] is signified.

Revelation 2:23b And all the Churches shall know that I am the one who searches reins and hearts.

By the reins, which are contained within the body and from which desire is said to proceed, hidden sins are signified; by the hearts, hidden thoughts are signified. And the meaning is: And they shall know, namely on the day of judgment, when both the woman herself and all who have been deceived by her will be condemned; all the Churches, that is, all who are counted under the Christian name, not only the elect but also the depraved, because I am the one who searches reins and hearts, that is, not only the sins that are revealed, but also those that are hidden; and I also look upon the thoughts themselves, and on that day I will give to each of you according to your works.

Revelation 2:24 But to the rest of you, who are in Thyatira, whoever do not have this doctrine, who have not known the depths of Satan, as they say, I will not put another burden upon you.

What he says seems to me to refer not only to the woman’s teaching, but also to the false apostles, who were teaching those from among the Gentiles who had believed that they should turn to the Law and to circumcision. And because the Jews were proud of observing the Law, and after the Gospel has been given, the keeping of the Law according to the letter is nothing other than the slavery of the Devil, we can understand by the depths of Satan the Law itself, if it is fulfilled in a carnal way. What, therefore, he says is this: I say to the rest of you who are in Thyatira, that is, who have remained steadfast in the faith: whoever do not have this doctrine, that is, who have not received the teaching of that very wicked woman; who have not known the depths of Satan, as they say, that is, who have not accepted the Law in the way the false apostles say, so as to fulfill it carnally, they have indeed received it so as to fulfill it spiritually; but they have not received it so as to observe it carnally (Acts 15:1). For example: those men were saying that unless you circumcise the flesh of your foreskin, you cannot be saved; but these, understanding circumcision not carnally but spiritually, were cutting away from their hearts and from their flesh all vices. Those were saying that they should keep the sabbath, doing no work on it; but these, taking all the time of their life as a sabbath, made it their concern, as far as they were able, to abstain from every servile work, that is, from sins. I will not put another burden upon you, that is, upon you who have remained steadfast in the faith, who have not received the false apostles. I will not put another burden upon you, except what has been commanded to you by the apostles: that you abstain from foods offered to idols, and from what is strangled, and from blood, and from fornication (Acts 15:29).

Revelation 2:25 Nevertheless, hold fast to what you have until I come.

As if he were saying: Because you are little children and you need milk, not solid food, hold fast to the faith you have learned until I come, so that by the grace of my inspiration I may stir you up to undertake higher works of virtue.

Revelation 2:26–27 And he who overcomes and keeps my works until the end, I will give him power over the nations, and he will rule them with a rod of iron, and they will be broken like a potter's vessel, just as I also received from my Father.

By "end" in this place death is understood: therefore he who overcomes the world and its desires, and perseveres until the end, will be given power over the nations, and he will rule them with a rod of iron. But even if by "end" death is understood, how are the elect said to rule the nations after death, since in that blessed life there is no one who is ruled with a rod of iron, where nothing distorted and nothing crooked is found? Unless perhaps we understand it in that sense, as that: Who made the things that are to be. For all things which are to be done according to God's disposition already exist in his predestination as if made, because it cannot happen that anything is changed from all those things which God has decreed to be done. Similarly also to the saints, whom God foreknew would be righteous and would persevere in good works, before their birth he granted this, that they have power over the nations and rule them with a rod of iron, as he speaks to Jeremiah saying: Before I formed you in the womb, I knew you, and before you came out of the womb, I sanctified you, and I made you a prophet to the nations (Jer. 1:5). In the second Psalm it is written that the Father said to the Son: Ask of me, and I will give you the nations as your inheritance, and your possession the ends of the earth. You shall rule them with a rod of iron; you shall break them like a potter's vessel (Ps. 2:8). The Father gave the Son power, that he might rule the nations with a rod of iron; the Son also gave power to the apostles and the other preachers, that they also might rule the nations with a rod of iron and break them like a potter's vessel. Therefore by the iron rod the Gospel is signified, as also by the wooden rod the Law. For the wooden rod which Moses used represented the law which was given by him; for that rod, as we read in Exodus (Num. 20:11; cf. Exod. 17:6), struck the rock twice, and very abundant waters came forth, so that the people and the beasts drank. Therefore the rod is the Old Testament: the double striking the Law and the prophets; but the rock prefigured Christ. Therefore the rod struck the rock twice, when the Old Testament through the law and the prophets foretold that Christ would come for the redemption of humanity. And because it was impossible that those things which were foretold through the Law and the prophets should not be fulfilled, as the Lord says in the Gospel: It is easier for heaven and earth to pass away than for one iota or one tittle of the Law to pass away without being fulfilled (Luke 16:17). The rod struck the rock twice, when the prophecies of the prophets and the Law in a certain way compelled God to consent to become man. Therefore the rock was struck, and very abundant waters came forth, because Christ, according to what was said, coming, produced from himself the streams of heavenly doctrine, that is, the Gospel. The people and the beasts drank, that is, the Jews and the Gentiles. Also that rod flourished in the tabernacle and produced nuts (Num. 17:8), because in the tabernacle of the Church afterwards the Old Testament brought forth flowers of spiritual senses, and those flowers themselves produced fruits of good works. We have said all this in order to demonstrate that the Law was signified by the wooden rod.

And because the teachings of the New Testament are stricter than those of the Old, the Gospel is not inappropriately foreshadowed by the iron rod, but the Law by the wooden one. In the latter it is said: “You shall eat and be filled; you shall drink and be made drunk; you shall sleep, and there will be none to make you afraid” (Lev. 26:5–6); and again: “You shall eat the oldest of the old, and the old you will cast away when the new comes upon you” (Lev. 26:10); and many other things which refer to bodily pleasures. But here it is said: “See that your hearts are not weighed down with dissipation and drunkenness and the cares of this world”; and again: “Narrow and constricted is the way that leads to life” (Matt. 7:14); and the Apostle Paul says: “In all things let us present ourselves as ministers of God, in much patience, in vigils, in fastings” (2 Cor. 6:4); and countless other things which are found in the New Testament concerning the mortification of the flesh. For the Lord in the Gospel also makes clear how much stricter his teachings are than the precepts of the Law, when he says: “You have heard that it was said to the people of old: You shall not kill; and whoever kills shall be liable to judgment. But I say to you that everyone who is angry with his brother shall be liable to judgment; and whoever says to his brother, ‘Raca,’ shall be liable to the council; and whoever says, ‘Fool,’ shall be liable to the fire of hell” (Matt. 5:21–22); and again: “It was said to the people of old: You shall not commit adultery. But I say to you that whoever looks at a woman in order to lust after her has already committed adultery with her in his heart” (Matt. 5:27–28); and again: “It was said to the people of old: Whoever divorces his wife, let him give her a bill of divorce. But I say to you: Whoever divorces his wife, except on account of fornication, commits adultery; and whoever marries a divorced woman commits adultery” (Matt. 5:31–32); and again: “It was said to the people of old: An eye for an eye, and a tooth for a tooth. But I say to you, do not resist an evildoer; but if anyone strikes you on your right cheek, offer him the other also; and whoever wants to take your tunic, let him have your cloak as well” (Matt. 5:38–40); and again: “It was said to the people of old: You shall love your friend and hate your enemy. But I say to you: Love your enemies, do good to those who hate you, and pray for those who persecute you and slander you, so that you may be sons of your Father who is in heaven” (Matt. 5:43–45). These words of the Lord which we have set forth clearly show that the Gospel is figured by the iron rod, and the Law by the fragile and flexible wooden one. By the potter’s vessels are signified the hearts of the Gentiles, filled with every error of demons, which were shattered by the touch of the iron rod; for when the grace of the Gospel came, every error of the Gentiles was destroyed, and what were formerly earthen vessels were made iron vessels, most strong against all the temptations of demons and against the persecutions of evil men.

Revelation 2:28 And I will give him the morning star:

That is, I will give him myself; for he himself is the morning star, of whom Scripture says: A star shall rise out of Jacob, and a man shall arise from Israel, and he shall strike the leaders of Moab (Num. 24:17). Moab is said to be “from the father”; and the Lord said to the Jews: You are from your father the Devil. Therefore the Lord struck the leaders of Moab when he brought to nothing all the makers of errors.

Chapter 3

Revelation 3:1a And to the angel of the Church in Sardis write: These things says he who has the seven spirits of God and the seven stars.

The seven spirits of God are the seven gifts of the Holy Spirit, which the prophet Isaiah shows to have descended upon the Lord when he says: And the Spirit of the Lord shall rest upon him, the spirit of wisdom and understanding, the spirit of counsel and strength, the spirit of knowledge and piety, and the spirit of the fear of the Lord shall fill him (Isa. 11:2). About these seven spirits, what the Lord has granted, we will speak in what follows; but what the seven stars are has been said above (Rev. 2:1). It continues:

Revelation 3:1b I know your works, that you have a name that you live, and you are dead.

He had a name that he lived, that is, the Christian name; but he was dead, because he did not have the works of faith, which is love.

Revelation 3:2a Be watchful, then, and strengthen the remaining things that were about to die.

One keeps watch who always practices good works; therefore it is commanded to this one to keep watchful, that is, to train himself in works of love, so that the small goods that he still seemed to possess might not perish. For just as a body is dead without the soul, so also whatever good things we may seem to have are dead if love is lacking:

Revelation 3:2b For I have not found your works full before my God.

They were not full, but empty, because they were not filled with love; and therefore the five foolish virgins were driven away from the fellowship of the heavenly Bridegroom, because they were unwilling to have the oil of love in their vessels (Matt. 25:3).

Revelation 3:3a Therefore have in mind how you received and heard, and keep it, and do penance:

That is, remember how you received the faith of Christ in baptism, where you promised that you would renounce the Devil and all his works; remember also what you heard from the teachers of the Christian faith who taught you the faith of Christ, namely how you ought to live. And keep what you heard, and do penance, because you have so long been sluggish in good works.

Revelation 3:3b If therefore you do not keep watch,

That is, if you do not rouse yourself to perform good works:

Revelation 3:3c I will come like a thief, and you will not know at what hour I will come to you.

The Lord comes upon that person like a thief, to remove him from this worldly dwelling through death; that person who, resting in his wickedness, is unwilling either to abstain from vices or to set death before his eyes, just as the Lord says in the Gospel: Because if the master of the house knew at what hour the thief was going to come, he would certainly keep watch and would not allow his house to be broken into (Matt. 24:43). By the master of the house we can understand the mind; for just as the master of the house rules over all who are in the house, so the mind is set over all the actions of the body and over its thoughts. Therefore the master of the house sleeps, and the thief, coming, breaks into his house, when the mind, resting in bodily pleasures, does not foresee death, which then arrives, and breaking into the house of the body, snatches away the soul. But if he keeps watch, that is, perseveres in good works, he will endure death not as a thief, but as a liberator.

Revelation 3:4a But you have a few names in Sardis who have not defiled their garments.

Look, the disciples are set before the negligent pastor, so that at least by imitating them he might make progress. But it is remarkable why he is said to have “a few names” and not “a few people.” This pertains to the very great excellence of a certain saint, when he is said to be known by God by name, just as it is said of Moses, a perfect man: “I know you by name” (Exod. 33:17); and the Lord says in the Gospel: “He calls his own sheep by name and leads them out” (Jn. 10:3). These few, therefore, who deserve to be praised by the voice of God, have not defiled their garments, that is, they have not stained with the filth of vices the purity which they received in baptism.

Revelation 3:4b And they have walked with me in white, because they are worthy.

That is, in good works.

Revelation 3:5a Whoever overcomes will therefore be clothed in white garments;

That is, whoever through repentance and tears has overcome the Devil and driven all vices from himself will also obtain the same purity that a baptized person possesses. He says this in order to stir both the pastor of the church of Sardis and all sinners as well to repentance.

Revelation 3:5b And I will not erase his name from the book of life.

If he calls the book of life “predestination,” how can someone be erased from the book of life, since the Apostle says: “And those whom he predestined, he also called; and those whom he called, he also justified; and those whom he justified, he also glorified” (Rom. 8:30)? Unless perhaps we understand it in this way: there are many who are considered righteous by human beings, but in the sight of God they turn out to be unrighteous. Therefore, the people who regard them as righteous already think that they are written in God’s book; but on the day of judgment, when the deeds of the depraved and the righteous are made known to all, this entry and this false assumption will be erased from their minds, when they see their condemnation for their sinfulness.

Revelation 3:5c And I will confess his name before my Father and before the angels of God.

This confession of the names of the righteous will then take place when, according to the quality of their merits, each person receives his reward.

Revelation 3:7a And to the angel of the Church in Philadelphia write: These things says the holy and true one, who has the key of David.

We read in the Book of Kings that the Lord said to David: And it shall be, when you sleep with your fathers, I will raise up your seed that shall come forth from you, and I will establish his kingdom forever. I will be to him a father, and he shall be to me a son (2 Sam. 7:12–14). That this was said about Christ no one doubts, except the faithless Jew, who hesitates. And into a locked house no one can enter except by a key. Therefore the Son of God has the key of David, because he himself took on a man from the line of David, whom he calls the key of David, through whom all things have been opened, both what God spoke to David and what David spoke about him.

Revelation 3:7b Who opens, and no one shuts; shuts, and no one opens.

The Lord Jesus Christ opened the Old Testament for understanding the divine mysteries that lay hidden in it, which no one will be able to shut; but he shut the unnecessary observance of the literal law, which no one will be able to open. He also opened the hearts of the Gentiles to receive the teaching of faith, which no persecutor has been able to shut; but he shut and destroyed the worship of idols, which no persecutor has been able to open. He also opened the entrance of the heavenly kingdom to his elect, which no power of demons can shut; but he shut that same entrance to the depraved, which none will be able to open for them.

Revelation 3:8 I know your works: behold, I have set before you an open door which no one can shut; because you have little strength, and you have kept my word, and have not denied my name.

It seems to me that this angel of the Church of Philadelphia was not sufficiently educated in the divine Scriptures, and therefore it is said that he had little strength, namely for overcoming the enemies of the truth of the faith. This is the meaning: Because in persecution you were steadfast, and you have kept my word, that is, you did not cease from preaching my word, and you have not denied my name; therefore, because you have little strength, I have set before you an open door in the divine Scriptures, so that you may understand the mysteries of the divine Scriptures: which no one will be able to shut, that is, no one among the enemies will be able to resist the wisdom that will be given to you.

Revelation 3:9 Behold, I will give some from the synagogue of Satan, who say that they are Jews and are not, but lie.

Behold, I will give some from the synagogue of Satan, understood to mean certain people, that is, from among the Jews, who are the synagogue of Satan, who were opposing you, and whose harassment you constantly endured, so that they may believe in me through your words.

Revelation 3:10a Behold, I will make them come and worship at your feet, and they will know that I have loved you; because you have kept the word of my patience:

That is, when they have believed in me through your words, they will worship at your feet, begging that through you they may obtain eternal life.

Revelation 3:10b And I will keep you from the hour of temptation, which is going to come upon the whole world, to tempt all who dwell upon the earth.

The hour of temptation signifies the day of judgment; and he has used temptation in place of revealing, because the causes of each person will then be revealed. And we read in Exodus: God tests you, so that he may know if you love him (Deut. 13:3), that is, so that it may become evident and appear to all whether you love him. According to this sense, therefore, the hour of temptation is signified as the hour of revealing, when the deeds of each person will be revealed to all. From this hour of temptation the Lord will save us and all his elect.

Revelation 3:11 Behold, I am coming quickly: hold fast what you have, so that no one may take your crown.

Hold fast what you have, that is, the good that you have, hold it until the end of your life. So that no one may take your crown, that is, so that the glory which is owed to you may not be possessed by anyone without you.

Revelation 3:12a Whoever overcomes, I will make him a pillar in the temple of my God, and he will no longer go out.

By the temple the Church can be indicated; by the pillars, which support the temple, the preachers. For the Apostle Paul also calls Peter and James pillars of the Church (Gal. 2:9), because the Church has been built upon them, just as the Lord said to Peter: You are Peter, and upon this rock I will build my Church (Matt. 16:18). But it seems that, on account of what follows: And he will no longer go out, this statement belongs not to the present Church but to the heavenly one. For there were many who were called pillars of the Church, but because of their corrupt teaching they departed from it, as was the case with Origen and many others. Therefore, by the temple the heavenly dwelling can be indicated, and by the pillar, each individual elect person. Therefore, whoever conquers all diabolical temptations and all carnal pleasures becomes a pillar in the temple of God, that is, in heavenly blessedness; and just as a pillar, in the place where it is set, continues with the firmest stability, so too each of the elect in heavenly blessedness receives from his Creator such exceedingly firm stability that he is never torn away from it, as the Devil was torn away, who fell from heavenly blessedness through pride.

Revelation 3:12b And I will write upon him the name of my God.

This name of God is eternal; therefore the name of God, which is eternal, is written upon each righteous person when eternity is granted him, to remain with the Lord. And the name of the city of my God,

Revelation 3:12c the new Jerusalem, which comes down from heaven from my God.

That new Jerusalem is the heavenly one, which is built up daily not from stones, as the old one was, but from holy men. Moreover, Jerusalem is interpreted as “vision of peace,” that is, “vision of God.” Therefore the name Jerusalem is written upon each of the elect, when it is granted to each one that he may merit to see God without end. Alternatively: The name Jerusalem is written upon each of the elect, because he himself is Jerusalem, in whom God consents to reign, namely in the fellowship of the angels and all the elect, who are called the heavenly Jerusalem. But it is remarkable that Jerusalem is said to have come down from heaven, since we know that all the elect daily ascend from earth to heaven, and do not slip back from heavenly things to earthly things. But when the Lord descended from heaven for the redemption of humanity, the saints also descended in a manner, because those whom he was coming, in his mercy, to redeem were already joined to him by the grace of predestination. And my new name; namely, that he may be called a son of God, just as I too am the Son of God.

Revelation 3:14a And to the angel of the Church of Laodicea write: These things says the Amen, the faithful and true witness.

“Amen” truly, or “faithfully,” is interpreted: what “amen” is, this is “the faithful and true witness.”

Revelation 3:14b Who is the beginning of the creation of God.

He does not call Himself the beginning of the creation of God because He Himself was created, but because all things have been created by Him, just as, for example, an architect made a house. If you ask what was the beginning of the house, it will be said to you: the craftsman, who first conceived it in his mind, and afterwards built it; because the house could not give itself its own beginning.

Revelation 3:15a I know your works, because you are neither cold nor hot.

He was not cold, because although he did not have works of faith, by faith alone he considered himself righteous; he was not hot, because love was lacking in him.

Revelation 3:15b I wish that you were cold, or hot;

that is, I wish that you were either burning with love, or would regard yourself as a sinner, as you are, so that through fear of eternal fire and through repentance you might be driven to perform good works.

Revelation 3:16 But because you are lukewarm, I will begin to vomit you out of my mouth.

If lukewarm water is drunk, it causes disgust; and so it is easily rejected. Now we enter truly into the mouth of the Lord when, cleansed from all vices through baptism, we become his members. Therefore, if we have grown lukewarm in love and have neglected to practice the faith which works through love, and which we promised in baptism, we are rejected from the mouth of the Lord as something harmful, disgusting, and foul-smelling, just as he himself said of himself to his disciples: I am the vine, and you are the branches; and my Father is the farmer. Every branch in me that does not bear fruit, he cuts off; and every one that bears fruit, he prunes, that it may bear more fruit (Jn. 15:2).

Revelation 3:17a Because you say, “I am rich, and wealthy, and I am in need of nothing.”

One is rightly called rich and wealthy who is full of all virtues and has grown up to the summit of perfection. But this bishop of the Church of Laodicea, because of faith alone, thought himself rich and wealthy in all virtues, and that he needed nothing else to obtain eternal salvation, not considering that which the apostle says: “faith without works is idle” (James 2:20).

Revelation 3:17b And you do not know that you are wretched and pitiable, and poor, and blind, and naked.

We call him wretched who, along with poverty and nakedness, is also oppressed by some infirmity. Likewise, this man is called wretched and pitiable because he was poor in virtues, blind in mind, and naked of good works.

Revelation 3:18a I advise you to buy from me gold that has been tested, refined by fire.

By gold is understood wisdom, as it is written: “A desirable treasure rests in the mouth of the wise” (Prov. 21:20). It is called “tested” because it makes all those whom it possesses tested and perfect. And it is called “refined by fire” for this reason, that it inflames with love of God and neighbor the minds in which it dwells. And because true wisdom increases from the divine Scriptures, the Psalmist makes this clear when he says: “The words of the Lord are pure words, silver tested by fire, proved in the earth, purified sevenfold” (Ps. 11:7). “The words of the Lord are pure words,” because they make minds chaste, free from vices. But since nothing can be purified except what is defiled, we must ask how the words of the Lord can be said to be purified, in which nothing defiled can exist. Therefore, the words of the Lord are said to be like silver tested by fire, because they test the minds of the faithful, sometimes by the fire of repentance, sometimes by the fire of love, cleansing them from all the stains of vices. But when he says “proved in the earth,” in Hebrew it has: “Separated from the earth.” And it is also said to be “purified sevenfold” because of the seven purifications which divine Scripture contains in itself, namely the seven virtues, by which all vices are purified: just as the vice of gluttony is purified by abstinence, pride by humility, lust by chastity; hatred, greed, envy, and many others by love. Likewise, the other vices also have their enemies, by whom they are either conquered or they conquer. And because above the Lord says that gold, that is, wisdom, is to be bought from him, we must ask at what price wisdom is bought, since Job says: “Refined gold shall not be given in exchange for it” (Job 28:15), nor anything else that is considered more precious among men. But at what price it is bought, the blessed Job himself makes clear when he says: “The fear of the Lord, that is wisdom, and to turn away from evil is understanding” (Job 28:28). Not that the one who fears is perfect in wisdom, since the apostle says: “The one who fears is not perfected in love” (1 Jn. 4:18); but that he already begins to possess wisdom who begins to pay the price for it. Now its price is the fear of the Lord, renunciation of vices, repentance, tears, works of mercy. And therefore it is not without reason that it follows:

Revelation 3:18b So that you may become rich.

For without this price no one can attain the wealth of virtues, which is wisdom.

Revelation 3:18c And that you may be clothed in white garments.

White garments are good works.

Revelation 3:18d And that the shame of your nakedness may not appear.

That shame of nakedness is the one of which the Psalmist says: “Behold, I was conceived in iniquities, and in sins my mother bore me” (Ps. 50:7). But we must cover this shame of nakedness with good works; for although this bishop had faith, he is nevertheless said to be naked because he was lacking the works of faith.

Revelation 3:18e And anoint your eyes with eye salve, so that you may see.

By the eye salve that wipes away dimness from the eyes and draws forth tears, repentance and regret of heart can be understood; for it is precisely this that wipes away from the eyes of the mind the darkness of blindness and sharpens them to see what is the reward of the righteous and the punishment of sinners.

Revelation 3:19a I reprove and chastise those whom I love.

As Scripture says: For the Lord corrects the one he loves; he scourges every son whom he receives (Heb. 12:6).

Revelation 3:19b Therefore be zealous, and do penance:

That is, imitate the good, and do penance, because up to now you have been sluggish in good works.

Revelation 3:20 Behold, I stand at the door and knock: if anyone hears my voice and opens the door, I will enter in to him, and will dine with him, and he with me.

The Lord knocks at our door when, through the divine Scripture, he warns us daily to leave behind vices and turn to the doing of good works. We hear his voice and open the door when we accept his commandments with the full attention of our mind. He enters in to us when he plants his love in our hearts. He indeed dines with us when he rejoices over our progress. We dine with him when we exult in the hope of heavenly glory.

Revelation 3:21 Whoever overcomes, I will grant him the power to sit with me on my throne; just as I also overcame and sat with my Father on his throne.

Christ conquered the Devil, and his saints also conquer: but Christ by himself, while his saints not by themselves, but through him. The Father gave the Son authority, inasmuch as he is man, to sit on his throne; and the Son gave authority to his saints, that they too may sit on his throne. But Christ sits at the right hand of the Father, as the Psalmist says: The Lord said to my Lord, Sit at my right hand (Ps. 109:1); because he is equal to the Father. The saints, however, are not said to sit at the right hand of the Son, but on their own thrones, as the Lord said in the Gospel: You also will sit on twelve thrones, judging the twelve tribes of Israel (Matt. 19:28). For they are sons not by nature as Christ is, but by adoption; therefore they are assigned different thrones according to the diversity of their merits. May he of whom we speak grant this to us, that in the distribution of these thrones we wretched ones may be found worthy to have a share, Jesus Christ our Lord, who lives and reigns with the Father and the Holy Spirit for ever and ever, amen.

On the Second Vision

Reason requires that after the seven letters which John addressed to the seven Churches, which signify the one catholic Church, in which the faithful are instructed how they ought to ascend to the height of the virtues, the condition and exaltation of this same Church should be described more clearly.

Chapter 4

Revelation 4:1a After these things I saw a door opened in heaven.

By the door Christ is understood, who says: I am the door; if anyone enters through me, he will be saved (Jn. 10:9). This door is said to be open, because to all who wish to come to him, although they are weighed down by many crimes, he does not disdain to open the door of his mercy, as he himself says in the Gospel: Come to me, all you who labor and are burdened, and I will refresh you (Matt. 11:28). By heaven, however, the Church is signified. But if everyone who enters through the door into the sheepfold, that is, into the holy Church, will be saved, by what entrance have the depraved, whose countless numbers are contained within the holy Church, come in? Let us ask the Lord about this; for he says: He who does not enter through the door into the sheepfold, but climbs up another way, he is a thief and a robber (Jn. 10:1). Whoever therefore does not wish to observe what they promised in baptism has not entered through the door into the sheepfold, but has climbed up another way.

Revelation 4:1b And the first voice which I heard, like a trumpet speaking with me, saying.

We can take the first voice as the Old Testament; but it seems better to me that by the first voice we understand John the Baptist, of whom it is written: And you, child, shall be called the prophet of the Most High; for you will go before the Lord to prepare his ways, to give knowledge of salvation to his people in the forgiveness of their sins (Luke 1:76). But John himself also calls himself a voice, saying: I am the voice of one crying in the desert, make straight the way of the Lord, as Isaiah the prophet said (Jn. 1:23, from Isa. 40:3). And because his preaching spread everywhere around, the voice of his preaching is said to be like a trumpet. But let us hear what this voice says:

Revelation 4:1c Come up here, and I will show you what must be done after these things;

That is, abandon earthly things and lift up your mind to heavenly things. Alternatively: the Evangelist John in this passage plays the role of those who believed in Christ through John the Baptist, and many existed at that time who thought John was the Christ; but John was urging his hearers to lift their minds upward, when he said that he was not the Christ, but that another was to come after him, saying: I am not the Christ; but one stronger than I will come after me, whose sandal strap I am not worthy to loose. He will baptize you in the Holy Spirit and fire (Jn. 1:27); and again: He must increase, but I must decrease (Jn. 3:30). He also warned his disciples to go up higher, when he taught very clearly, with evident reasoning, that he must be abandoned and Christ must be followed. Then it follows:

Revelation 4:1d And I will show you what must be done after these things.

As if he were saying: If you lift up your heart on high, that is, if you follow Christ in faith, you will be able easily to know both what I teach and the things that are to come, with him himself also teaching.

Revelation 4:2a And immediately I was in the spirit;

That is, when I began to believe in Christ, I was changed from carnal to spiritual.

Revelation 4:2b And behold, a throne was set in heaven, and one sitting upon it.

By heaven, as we said above (verse 1), the Church is indicated; but the throne set in heaven signifies the hearts of the saints dwelling in the Church. And the one who was seen sitting upon the throne is God, who dwells and sits in the hearts of his saints, as the Apostle says: For the temple of God is holy, and that temple is you (1 Cor. 3:17).

Revelation 4:3a And the one who sat there had an appearance like a jasper stone and a sardius.

Jasper is green in color, but sardius is red: therefore by the greenness of the jasper the divinity of our Savior is indicated; by the redness of the sardius, however, his humanity is signified. Therefore our Savior contains in himself the greenness of the jasper, because according to his divinity he is in all things equal to the Father; and the redness of the sardius, because according to his humanity he exists as a perfect man, as it is written: Perfect God, perfect man, subsisting from a rational soul and human flesh (Creed under the name of Athanasius).

Revelation 4:3b And there was a rainbow around the throne, similar to the appearance of an emerald.

In Genesis we read that God said to Noah: I will place my bow in the clouds of heaven, and I will remember my everlasting covenant, which was made between God and every living soul (Gen. 9:13). Therefore by the bow, which appears as a sign of God’s mercy to men living upon the earth, the mercy of God is not inappropriately understood. The rainbow, that is, the bow, surrounded the throne of God, that is, the Church; because His mercy, by surrounding us, protects and governs us, and defends us from invisible enemies. For in Him, as the Apostle says, we live and move and are (Acts 17:28). This bow is said to have had the appearance of an emerald, which possesses an exceedingly rich and most pleasing greenness; but jasper, of which we spoke above, is a stone of green color, like the emerald. Yet its greenness is dull; whereas the greenness of the emerald is clear, and its beauty draws the eyes of those who look upon it to admire it. By the greenness of jasper, as we said, the divinity of our Savior is understood; which is obscure and veiled by certain mists, because no mind can comprehend the divinity of our Redeemer as it truly is; and the higher the mind raises itself to investigate it, the thicker a darkness is set before it. But by the greenness of the emerald, which is clear, the mercy of the Lord is rightly signified; for the mercy of the Lord is so evident that no person in possession of a sound mind can fail to know it. For who can be ignorant of the boundless mercy of God, which He has shown toward humanity, and shows every day, even though it cannot be perfectly expressed in words? And this fact also, that the greenness of this stone, if one looks at it, sharpens dim eyes for seeing, can fittingly be applied to the mercy of God; for when we lie in sins and our crimes weigh us down, we have dim eyes of the mind. But when we consider the mercy of God, how He has brought back to salvation, through repentance, prostitutes and robbers and those cast down by every kind of crime, and even His own persecutors, we are restored to the hope of pardon. And since we were already being held in the pit of destruction, when we gaze upon the mercy of the Lord and take confidence from it, we flee for refuge to Him whom we have offended, that by His mercy we may be freed from everlasting death. Gazing upon this mercy of God, and unable to express it in words, the Psalmist called God Himself his mercy, saying: My God, my mercy (Ps. 143:1–2).

Revelation 4:4 And all around the seat were twenty-four seats, and upon the thrones twenty-four elders sitting, clothed all around with white garments, and on their heads golden crowns.

The twenty-four elders in this book sometimes signify the Fathers of the Old Testament, sometimes those of the New, sometimes both together; but in this place they signify the Fathers of the Old Testament, who are said to have sat on the twenty-four seats; for when we sit, we rest from labor. Through the seats, therefore, the twenty-four books of the Old Testament are signified. Therefore, upon the twenty-four thrones, the twenty-four elders were seen sitting, because the Fathers of the Old Testament, from the labors and cares of this age, found a kind of rest in the reading and meditation of the divine books. But we must ask, since the seat signifies the Church, why these seats are said to be arranged around the seat. Surely because whoever is master of a household and desires his house to be safe from robbers and enemies surrounds it either with a wall, or a hedge, or a fence. In the same way the Church, both against heretics and against pagans who want to overthrow the faith of Christ in her, is fortified by the books of the Old Testament; because when she proves that what she venerates was foretold long before the coming of Christ, she drives far away from herself the darts of enemies who wish to destroy the faith of Christ. These elders are said to have been clothed all around with white garments and to have had golden crowns on their heads: by the white garments purity of life is signified, and by the golden crowns good works are signified.

Revelation 4:5a And from the throne (that is, from the Church) proceed lightnings, and voices, and thunders.

By the lightnings are meant the miracles that God works through his saints; by the voices, preaching; by the thunders, the terror of the fire of Gehenna is signified, because the holy preachers, when they cannot draw people back from vices by love of the heavenly homeland, set before them the eternal torments that are prepared for the wicked, so that those whom they cannot restrain by gentleness they may at least curb from vices by terrors.

Revelation 4:5b And seven burning lamps before the throne.

What the seven lamps are, he himself explains when he says:

Revelation 4:5c Which are the seven spirits of God.

By the seven spirits of God we must understand the seven gifts of the Holy Spirit, which the prophet Isaiah shows to have rested upon the Lord, saying: “And the Spirit of the Lord shall rest upon him: the spirit of wisdom and understanding, the spirit of counsel and fortitude, the spirit of knowledge and piety, and the spirit of the fear of the Lord shall fill him” (Isa. 11:2). These seven lamps, that is, the seven gifts of the Holy Spirit, are said to burn before the throne, that is, before the sight of his Church, because without the light of these virtues, placed as we are in the night of this world, we cannot walk along the paths of life to the heavenly homeland. But when he says that Christ received these seven gifts from the Holy Spirit, this belongs to the mystery of his humanity; for according to his divinity he could in no way be increased, since without him nothing could exist.

Yet a question can truly be raised: why, when the prophet said that these seven gifts were going to descend upon Christ, did he begin with the greater ones and, going down, arrive at the lesser? But it will seem not to have been done without reason if it is carefully examined. For since our Lord Jesus Christ, from that heavenly blessedness in which He was in all things equal to the Father, consented to descend to earth and to become man, and chose to be made lower than the angels whom He Himself had created, He fittingly received these seven gifts in a descending order; because by descending and humbling Himself even to death He fulfilled the will of the Father, as the Apostle says: He humbled Himself unto death, even death on a cross (Philip. 2:8). But we, who are raised up from the very pit of destruction to heavenly things, begin from the lesser, so that by growing we may come to the greater.

Let us now briefly say how these seven gifts descended upon Christ, as the Lord has granted. The Spirit of wisdom rested upon the man Christ when the Son of God according to his divinity, who is called the wisdom of the Father, descended into him.

The spirit of understanding rested in him, when he was endowed with all virtues, just as it is written in the Gospel: Jesus advanced in wisdom and age and grace before God and men (Luke 2:52).

After understanding comes counsel. Counsel is so called from “consulting”; for if some danger is hanging over us, we seek counsel from a wise person, so that we may know how to escape the danger. Likewise, when we desire to rise to some dignity of the world, we seek counsel as to how we may be able to attain what we desire. Christ therefore is our counselor, who gives counsel as to how we may escape eternal dangers; and the counsel that he gives, he himself brings to completion, when he frees us from eternal dangers. Likewise, he gives counsel as to how we may be able to reach the inheritance of Paradise, and he himself leads us to those joys. Therefore the spirit of counsel descended upon the man Christ, when from the Holy Spirit he received the teaching of the Gospel, in which the counsel of all who desire to be saved consists.

The Spirit of fortitude also rested upon Christ when he appeared among men as the strong one to wage war against the ancient enemy. Of this fortitude of his he himself said in the Gospel: “When a strong man, fully armed, guards his own courtyard, the things he possesses are in peace; but if someone stronger than he comes upon him and overcomes him, he will take away all his weapons in which he trusted, and will distribute his spoils” (Luke 11:21).

After fortitude comes knowledge. Among men there are many kinds of knowledge; for there are different kinds of knowledge belonging to different arts. There is knowledge in literature, there is in worldly laws, there is in increasing temporal goods, there is also knowledge in anticipating dangers; there are also many who, through sensitivity of perception, foresee certain future things. But these kinds of knowledge are sometimes possessed by the good, sometimes by the evil; whereas the knowledge that descended upon Christ no one except a good person can have. It was so great that no mind could comprehend it: “For in him are hidden all the treasures of wisdom and knowledge” (Col. 2:3). Therefore it seems to me that by the spirit of knowledge we ought not to understand some particular deed of his; for our Savior, foreseeing that, if he avoided the company of sinful men, many wicked people would fall into the pit of despair, willed to conform himself to sinners through the fellowship of the body, namely by eating and drinking with them, and not rejecting even the touch of prostitutes. He also promised paradise to the thief at the very beginning of his confession (Luke 23:43), so that he might give hope of pardon to all sinners who desire to be saved. But the Pharisees, not having the spirit of knowledge, found fault with him because he ate with tax collectors and sinners. Yet he, possessing the spirit of knowledge, said to them: “Those who are well have no need of a physician, but those who are sick; I did not come to call the righteous, but sinners to repentance” (Matt. 9:12 and so on).

But regarding the spirit of piety, there is no need for us to say anything. For what is as pious as he who did not commit sin, and in whose mouth no deceit was found (1 Peter 2:22); and though he could by his own power free himself from death, yet for the salvation of humanity he allowed himself to be seized, bound, spat upon, struck with blows, scourged, and crucified?

But the spirit of the fear of the Lord, which is placed last, refers to the Passion; not because he was compelled by fear to die for the salvation of humanity, but we must understand “fear” here as “obedience,” of which he himself says: “I have not come to do my own will, but the will of him who sent me, the Father” (Jn. 6:38). For we also say that a son who is in all things obedient to his earthly father “fears” his father, although that obedience is not of fear but of love. Of this fear the Psalmist says: “The fear of the Lord is holy, enduring for ever and ever” (Ps. 18:10); yet it is not fear, but the love of God that will remain for ever and ever.

Now it must be said briefly how these seven gifts of the Holy Spirit are fitting for each of the faithful. And since we, who through the guilt of the first transgression lay in the snare of destruction, are by the mercy of God brought back from lower things to those above, that is, we begin with the fear of God, so that by advancing we may grow to higher things, that is, to wisdom. And by the same steps by which Christ, humbling himself, came down to us and brought our salvation to completion by dying, by those same steps we also ought to ascend to him, namely by doing his commandments, which are contained in the seven gifts of the Holy Spirit. Therefore the Spirit of the fear of the Lord rests upon every sinner when, considering his sins and greatly dreading the just judgment of God and the punishments of eternal fire which have been prepared for the wicked, he is turned to repentance and tears, and begins to weep most bitterly for what he performed with delight.

But the spirit of piety rests upon any sinner, when, hearing Scripture which says, “Just as water extinguishes fire, so almsgiving extinguishes sin” (Sirach 3:33), and the Lord saying, “Give alms, and behold, everything is clean for you” (Luke 11:41), he begins to feed the needy, clothe the naked, and to carry out the other works of mercy; so that, by having mercy on others, he may make God favorable to himself, whom he had offended by sinning. Therefore also the prophet Daniel took care to admonish the most wicked king to redeem his sins by almsgiving and by acts of mercy toward the poor.

One ascends from piety to the spirit of knowledge; for although someone may seem to possess knowledge, he is a fool if he lives badly. For what is more foolish than to cast the fear of God out of one’s mind, to be enslaved to vices, and to hurry at full speed toward destruction? Therefore, if it is folly to serve vices, and the best kind of knowledge is to abandon vices and turn with one’s whole mind to God, then whoever, through the spirit of fear, begins to weep for his sins, and through the spirit of piety strives to be merciful toward those in need, so as to make God merciful to himself by having mercy on others, must necessarily possess, through the gift of the Holy Spirit, the spirit of knowledge. For whoever desires to be converted to God must necessarily abandon vices; and whoever wishes to abandon vices must necessarily, through the teaching of the divine Scriptures or through the preaching of holy men, learn what kinds of vices there are, how they lure people, and by what means they can be extinguished; what also are the deceits of demons, and how one must fight against them: this, then, is what it is to possess the spirit of knowledge.

And because Solomon says that whoever adds knowledge also adds labor (Eccles. 1:18), from knowledge one ascends to the spirit of fortitude. For just as any fighter does not battle against someone whom he holds in chains, but rather against one who wishes to resist him, so too the Devil does not fight against the one whom he keeps bound with the chains of sins, but rather against the one who stops sinning and returns to God with his whole mind. Therefore, whoever through penance and works of mercy deserves to be freed from the chains of the Devil and wishes to wage war against him through the spirit of knowledge, must necessarily receive from the Holy Spirit the gift of fortitude. Armed with this, he is able both to extinguish completely his internal enemies, that is, the vices, and to overcome exterior enemies, namely, other people, through patience, and to repel from himself all the darts of demons with the shield of faith.

One ascends from courage to counsel; but the counsel of our salvation lies in the Gospel teachings. Therefore, whoever has been able to extinguish vices within himself through the spirit of courage must necessarily go on to examine the commands of the Gospel and store them up in his heart; there he will find the counsel by which he may be able to ascend to the summit of perfection.

And because the commands of the Gospel cannot be kept without virtues, one ascends from the spirit of counsel to understanding, in which the perfection of the virtues wholly consists. Indeed, the faithful receive the gift of understanding from the Holy Spirit when they deserve to be enriched with all the virtues; for he truly possesses understanding who is enlightened by faith, purified by chastity, adorned with patience, fortified with humility, shining with love, and brought to completion by the other virtues.

And because through the exercise of the virtues one climbs to the summit of perfection, wisdom is placed before understanding, in which all perfection consists. Of this perfection the Lord says in the Gospel: If you wish to be perfect, go, sell all that you have, and give to the poor, and you will have a treasure in heaven; and come, follow me (Matt. 19:21). There are indeed many who try suddenly to ascend to the height of perfection; but because they do not climb by the steps of the virtues, they easily fall. For what does it profit to abandon one’s possessions, if one does not cast away from one’s mind anger, hatred, drunkenness, envy, pride, vain glory, and the other vices that kill the soul? It is no advantage to climb a tree to escape a lion if one carries a serpent in one’s heart, by whose venom one perishes. Nor does the Lord say that he is perfect who has only given up his possessions, but rather he who has given up his possessions and has followed Him Himself. Now we follow Him when we imitate Him. Therefore, he ascends to this summit of perfection by a fitting degree who, having first renounced vices, rises from the active life, which is brought to completion by the virtues, to the contemplative life, in which he is united to Christ alone, who is the wisdom of the Father and the Son of God, and he becomes a citizen of the heavenly Jerusalem.

Now let us briefly show how the ascent of the faithful, through the seven steps of the gifts of the Holy Spirit, corresponds to Christ’s descent. The last step of Christ’s descent is the fear of God, through which we said his passion and death were prefigured, and it is the first step of the ascent of those who turn to God. The death of the Lord, moreover, suits those who, through the spirit of the fear of the Lord, weep for their sins; because in the death of the Lord lies the forgiveness of all sins, nor could there be perfect forgiveness of sins if he had not died for us.

The sixth step of Christ’s descent is mercy, and the second of the penitents’ ascent. Now the mercy of the faithful agrees with the mercy of the Lord; for he bestowed his mercy on those who were wretched in respect of the soul, freeing them from eternal death by his own death, and these (the faithful) bestow mercy on those who are wretched in respect of the body, by giving them the bodily necessities, about which he himself will say in the judgment: “Whatever you did to one of the least of my brothers, you did to me” (Matt. 25:40).

The fifth step of Christ’s descent is knowledge, and the third of the faithful’s ascent. Now, the spirit of knowledge that is given to the faithful agrees with the spirit of knowledge that was bestowed on the man Christ; for he, in order to save sinners, did not disdain to make himself like them, and they, in order to escape sins, flee to him through the spirit of knowledge, who has the power to forgive sins.

The fourth step of Christ’s descent is fortitude, and likewise the fourth of the faithful’s ascent. Moreover, the fortitude of the faithful corresponds to the fortitude of Christ, because they conquer the Devil by that same fortitude by which Christ also conquered, namely, by the fortitude of Christ, not their own; as he himself says: “Without me you can do nothing” (Jn. 15:5).

The third degree of Christ’s descent is the spirit of counsel, and the fifth of the faithful’s ascent. But the counsel of the faithful is joined to the counsel of Christ when they devote themselves to keeping the teachings of the Gospel, in which the perfect counsel of all the saints consists.

The second degree of Christ’s descent is understanding, and the sixth of the faithful’s ascent. And the understanding of the faithful corresponds to the understanding of Christ when the same virtues that he received from his Spirit, the same also his saints merit to receive from him.

The first degree of Christ’s descent is wisdom, and the seventh of the faithful’s ascent; because Christ is the wisdom of the Father, and from him all wisdom arises. Therefore the saints, who are united to the Lord through the practice of virtues, receive from him wisdom, in which the fullness of all goodness consists (Prov. 3:13). To them he himself speaks through Solomon, saying: If anyone loves wisdom, let him turn to me, and he will find it; and when he has found it, he is blessed if he holds fast to it. It follows:

Revelation 4:6a And in front of the throne there was something like a sea of glass, similar to crystal.

In the temple of Solomon we read that there was a bronze sea, in which the priests washed before they approached the altar to offer sacrifices (2 Chron. 4:6); but the brightness of bronze is quickly clouded by a certain blackness. By the bronze sea, therefore, the Law of Moses is signified, which obscures the brightness of spiritual understanding with the blackness of the letter. And just as, if bronze is rubbed with a file, its brightness is brought out, so too the spiritual understanding, which is obscured by the surface of the letter in the Law, if it is carefully examined by faithful men, is revealed through the grace of God. In this sea, then, the priests were washed, because through sacrifices and certain legal observances some sins of the ancient people were wiped away. By the sea of glass and crystal, therefore, the New Testament is not inappropriately understood. Now in this sea we find three things: the sea, the glass, and the crystal. By the sea, then, history is signified; by the glass, moral understanding; and by the crystal, spiritual understanding. By the glass, therefore, from which mirrors are made, moral understanding is not unsuitably taken; because just as a person can detect in a mirror whatever shameful thing he bears in his face, so too moral understanding reveals whatever vice may be in the souls of human beings. By the crystal, however, which is clearer than all gems, and which is accustomed to emit flashes of brightness and often even fire from itself, spiritual understanding is expressed; it clarifies whatever is obscure in the divine Scriptures, it illumines minds with the flames of spiritual meanings, and it kindles some persons into the love of God and the love of the heavenly homeland.

Revelation 4:6b And in the midst of the throne and around the throne, four living creatures full of eyes in front and behind.

This, taken according to the historical sense, cannot stand; for so small a number could not occupy both the whole circuit of the throne and its middle part. Rather, it must be understood spiritually. Therefore, the four living creatures signify not only the four evangelists, but also all the doctors of the Church who follow their teaching. The four living creatures stand around the throne, because the doctors of the Church defend the people entrusted to them, with all the strength they can, from both visible and invisible enemies. They stand in the midst of the throne, because they never cease to admonish each one of them to make progress in virtues and in all good works. Therefore these four living creatures were seen as having eyes in front and behind, because holy preachers look to both future and past things. They have eyes in front, because when they contemplate the day of judgment, the condemnation of the wicked, and the glory of the righteous, they show their hearers what they ought to avoid and what they ought to desire. They have eyes behind, because when they gaze upon the life of the fathers who went before, they themselves make progress by imitating them, and they set before their hearers their life and teaching, so that they too may make progress by imitating them.

Revelation 4:7 And the first living creature was like a lion, and the second living creature like a calf, and the third living creature having a face like a man, and the fourth living creature like a flying eagle.

Not all authors have explained the forms of the four living creatures in the same way10; but it has been handed down to us by the more ancient writers that we should understand Mark through the lion, Luke through the calf, Matthew through the man, and John through the flying eagle; because Mark begins from the wilderness, Luke from the priesthood of the Jews, Matthew from the man, that is, from the genealogy of Christ, but John from the divinity of Christ. Yet some, following another order, think that Matthew was indicated by the lion, Mark by the calf, Luke by the man, and John by the eagle. For they say that Matthew was therefore signified by the lion, which is the king of beasts, because he himself relates that Christ according to His human nature descended from a royal lineage (Matt. 2:2); he also says that at the time of His birth a star appeared, which showed Him to be King of heaven and earth, of angels and of men; he tells how the Magi, as if to a King of the world, were led from the East to worship Him; and he also says that the earthly king, that is, Herod, was troubled at the time of His birth together with all his ministers; for just as any king who has little power trembles and is disturbed if he learns that another king of great power is coming against him to invade his kingdom (Matt. 2:3), so when Christ came in the flesh, who was to be the King of the world, Herod was troubled, and all Jerusalem with him; for the gifts which the Magi are said to have offered to Christ show that He is King, God, and Lord of all creatures. They say that Mark was signified by the calf because of the simplicity of his style; Luke by the man, because he has set forth the human genealogy of Christ more fully than the others. For from him we have learned how He was announced by the angel, how He was born, how He was laid in the manger, how He was circumcised, how He was presented in the temple, and how, by growing, He reached mature age. There is no one who denies that John was signified by the eagle.

Revelation 4:8a And the four living creatures, each of them had six wings, and all around and within they are full of eyes.

By the number six, which is perfect, the perfection of the saints is signified, to which no one can rise except through the teaching of the Gospel: for the Law brought no one to perfection (Heb. 7:19). But if you join together the six wings of the four living creatures, you will find twenty‑four. It seems to me, therefore, that they have the same meaning as the twenty‑four thrones, which we have said signify the twenty‑four books of the Old Testament. Now thrones, since they always cling to the earth, bend somewhat toward the earth those who rest on them; but wings lift up to the heights those to whom they are attached. Therefore, the books of the Old Testament were thrones for the fathers of old, because, understanding them according to the letter, they were in no way able to raise themselves perfectly to their spiritual understanding; but these thrones have been turned into wings for the fathers of the New Testament, because, understanding the books of the Old Testament spiritually, they are daily lifted up to heavenly things through their teaching.

But because this number, that is, twenty‑four, is often found in this book, it has seemed good that we say a few things about the mysteries of this number. For the parts of this number are one, two, three, four, six, eight, twelve. The one, which is placed at the head, signifies the one God; the two that follow indicate the two Testaments; the three pertain to the Trinity. After the three, it is not unfitting that four follow, because through the teaching of the four Gospels the faith of the holy Trinity has been revealed to us. After the four, six follows, which is a perfect number, because without the doctrine of the Gospel no one has been able to ascend to the summit of perfection; for the Law, as the Apostle says, brought no one to perfection (ibid.). After the six, eight follow, because there are eight virtues which fight against the vices, and without the exercise of which no one is able to ascend to the peak of perfection. After the eight, twelve follow, because through the teaching of the apostles we come to the faith, which is the mother of all virtues.

Therefore the four living creatures are said to be full of eyes around and within. By “around” we must understand the whole body; for when Ezekiel spoke about these living creatures, he says that their whole body was full of eyes (Ezek. 10:12). The saints therefore have eyes behind, when, hearing the Lord’s warning, “No one who puts his hand to the plow and looks back is fit for the kingdom of God” (Luke 9:62), they strive with all their strength not to fall back into the evils which they remember having committed at some time in the past; although they do not cease to set those deeds before the eyes of their mind for the safeguarding of their humility. They have eyes before them, when, hearing the Lord’s words, “Blessed is that servant whom his lord, when he comes, shall find watching” (Matt. 24:46), they do not cease to do whatever good they can, so that at the time of their death, when the Lord comes, they may be found prepared. They have eyes on the left, when they repel all the temptations of demons from their minds with the shield of faith; they have eyes on the right, when they carefully guard against being exalted on account of good works. Or, they have eyes on the right and on the left, when neither prosperous things can lift them up nor adverse things cast them down. Below they have eyes, when, by extinguishing carnal desires, they trample them underfoot; above they have eyes, when with the whole desire of their mind they gasp for their heavenly homeland; within they have eyes, while they keep their hearts from filthy thoughts.

Revelation 4:8b And they had no rest day and night, saying: Holy, holy, holy, Lord God almighty, who was, and who is, and who is to come.

For the rest of the wicked is the present life; following them is tribulation that has no end. But the righteous are afflicted in this world, so that they may possess eternal rest, as Paul the Apostle says: Through many tribulations we must enter into the kingdom of God (Acts 14:21). Otherwise: the saints have no rest, because they do not cease from the praises of God and from prayers, as the Lord says: We must always pray and not lose heart (Luke 18:1); and Paul the Apostle speaks, saying: Pray without ceasing; in everything give thanks (1 Thess. 5:17). But we must ask ourselves how we can pray without ceasing. For there are many who, though they are constant in prayers, in psalm-singing, in the celebration of Masses, and give many alms, are nevertheless puffed up with pride, burn with the heat of greed, and gather money by whatever means they can; to such an extent that they even harass the poor themselves, whose misery they ought to have pitied, with certain cons, so as to increase their own wealth from the poor’s small resources, saying that they do this lawfully, though this “law” is not of God but a trick of the Devil. And even in monasteries many are found who are devoted to vigils, psalms, prayers, and the celebration of Masses, and yet are very often and easily inflamed with anger; some indeed are devoted to drunkenness and gluttony; some also carry hatred in their hearts for a long time; some in the good works they appear to do seek human favor and praise; some are weighed down by envy, some by slander, or by other vices. And what more? All the holiness that they lay claim to in words they disfigure and spoil by unworthy conduct.

Therefore, to show the blindness of these people, I will set forth a certain likeness. A man was stuck up to his neck, held fast in the mud; and when he saw someone passing by, he stretched out his hand and cried, saying: Have mercy on me, and pull me out of this mud. When the passerby stretched out his hand to pull him out, the man who had extended his hand sank it down under the mud. And when the one who had come to rescue him wanted to withdraw, the man again begged to be freed. When the rescuer a second time stretched out his hand to pull him out, the man, just as before, plunged his hand under the mud. When he did this repeatedly, the rescuer, angered, said: Why do you ask to be freed, when you choose to remain in your danger? And since you love your own destruction, keep what you have chosen. In the same way, these people constantly beg God to be freed from their vices; and yet they embrace these very vices so firmly that they can in no way be torn away from them. Let us therefore place before our eyes two men: one, learned, constantly praying, chanting psalms, celebrating masses, and yet subject to certain vices; the other, unlearned, ignorant in worldly terms, yet faithful, fearing and loving God, loving his neighbor, patient, humble. Which of these seems to you to have kept the Apostle’s command, where he says: Pray without ceasing (1 Thess. 5:17); the one who prays constantly and is subject to certain vices, or the one who does not know how to pray, and yet is free from vices and devoted to good works? It is clear to all that the innocence and good works of the second man move God to hear him more than the words of the first, whose life is stained by certain vices. Therefore he prays without ceasing who at fitting times entreats God with mind and tongue, and is constant in good works. When he sleeps, or is occupied with any works necessary for human life, his works shine in the sight of God, and they themselves are his intercessors with God, as the angel speaks to Cornelius, saying: Your prayers and your alms have been remembered in the sight of God (Acts 10:4).

But what the four living creatures say without ceasing, let us hear: Holy, holy, holy, Lord God almighty, who was, and who is, and who is to come. By calling God holy three times, they show the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit. But when they say: Lord God almighty, passing from plurality to singularity, they show that the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit are of one dominion, one divinity, one omnipotence. Yet we must ask, if the four living creatures signify not only the four evangelists, but also all the teachers of the Church; why are they said to invoke the holy Trinity without ceasing, when the holy ones of God are sometimes occupied in prayers, sometimes in reading, sometimes in preaching, or in various needs. Therefore, the holy preachers, while they hold in mind and confess with their mouth the faith of the holy Trinity, while they fulfill the works of faith, while they preach that same faith and the works of faith to whomever they can, while also in prayers and in giving thanks they pray and praise Him who is the triune and one God, while, according to the Apostle, whether they eat or drink, they do all things in the name of the Lord Jesus Christ, they invoke the holy Trinity without ceasing (1 Cor. 10:31). But as for what follows: Who was, and who is, and who is to come; this has already been explained above.

Revelation 4:9-10 And when those living creatures gave glory and honor and blessing to the One seated on the throne, who lives for ever and ever, the twenty-four elders came forward before the One seated on the throne and worshiped the One who lives for ever and ever.

In this passage, by the four living creatures the four books of the Gospel are indicated; by the twenty-four elders, the Fathers of the New Testament can be understood. Therefore the four living creatures give glory and honor and blessing to the One seated on the throne, who lives for ever and ever, because the teaching of the Gospel is read and preached throughout the whole world, through which glory and honor and blessing are given to the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit. At the voice of the four living creatures, then, the twenty-four elders fell down before the One seated on the throne and worshiped the One who lives for ever and ever, because the saints of God, established throughout the whole world, when they hear the teaching of the Gospel, humble themselves in the sight of God, so that they may live with Him for ever and ever. And they cast their crowns before the throne, saying. We said above that good works were signified by the crowns; therefore the saints cast their crowns before the throne when they attribute to God, and not to themselves, whatever good they do, according to the command of the Apostle: “Let the one who boasts, boast in the Lord” (2 Cor. 10:17).

But let us hear what they say:

Revelation 4:11a You are worthy, Lord our God, to receive glory and honor and power.

Above, he said that the four living creatures gave glory and honor and blessing to God; but here the twenty‑four elders say that God has received glory and honor and power, exchanging “power” for “blessing.” Yet what indeed can anyone give to God, or what can God receive from anyone, since he himself created all things, and without him nothing can exist, and he says in the Gospel: Without me you can do nothing (Jn. 15:5)? For if the saints can do nothing without God, what can they give him? Therefore, since the nature of human beings is such that they can have no good from themselves unless they receive it from him who is supremely good, God gives to his saints what they may use to repay him. For God has promised the kingdom of heaven to his saints, yet on the condition that they give the price of good works for it; and because the elect do not have this price unless they receive it from him, he himself gives the price with which they may purchase from him eternal life.
