And by the three double-measures of barley we can take the Old Testament, because it is divided by the Hebrews into three parts: the Prophets, the Law, and the Writings. Nor is it unsuitable that the Old Testament is signified by barley, which has a rough husk, on account of the harshness, namely, of the legal guidelines. For just as barley, beneath the roughness of its husk, contains the kernel from which bread is made, so also beneath the letter of the Old Testament there is contained the spiritual understanding, from which the bread of spiritual teaching is prepared by the teachers of the Church, promoting the nourishment of the faithful. For the apostle Paul writes that the Law brought no one to perfection, that is, none of those who understand and practice it in a carnal way; but those who understand and practice it in a spiritual way it undoubtedly raises to the summit of perfection (Heb. 7:19). Therefore the three measures of barley are doubled so that they reach six, which is a perfect number, since the Old Testament is first understood historically and afterwards spiritually by the doctors of the Church, and its spiritual understanding is fulfilled in works. By the denarius, however, eternal life is signified, as the Lord teaches in the Gospel (Matt. 20:2). The Church therefore prays to the Lord and says: “Two measures of wheat for a denarius, and three double-measures of barley for a denarius,” that is, for those who keep the two commandments of love according to the teaching of the Law and the Prophets, namely, the fathers of the Old Testament; and likewise for those who understand and practice the Old Testament spiritually, that is, the faithful of the Church, consent to grant the denarius of eternal life. By the strictness of wine we may understand the active life, and by the richness of oil the contemplative life. Therefore what he says, “Do not harm the wine and the oil,” is as if he said: Do not harm those who have endeavored to serve you through the works of the active life or the contemplative life, when you come to judgment. It continues:

Revelation 6:7 And when he had opened the fourth seal.

The opening of the fourth seal refers to the prophets, who foretold the coming of Christ, his passion and resurrection, and the calling of the Gentiles, both in plain speech and through various figures. Therefore our Savior opened the fourth seal when he granted the teachers of the Church understanding of the prophets. But regarding the sayings of the prophets, it is not necessary for us to say anything, since they have been explained by those same teachers. And because not only the things written by the prophets, but also the things done by them, have a spiritual meaning, let us say a few words about some of the deeds accomplished through David, Elijah, and Elisha.

In the book of Kings it is written that when David wished to go forth to battle against the Philistine, he said to Saul: “Your servant was tending his father’s flock, and a lion or a bear would come and seize a ram from the midst of the flock, and I would follow after and strike them, and rescue it from their mouth. And they rose up against me, and I seized them by the jaw and strangled and killed them; for your servant has killed both lion and bear” (1 Sam. 17:34–36). David signifies Christ; by the lion and the bear are meant the persecutors of the Church: by the lion, emperors and the other princes of the earth; by the bear, the Jews are indicated, who persecuted the Church of God; by the ram, however, the martyrs are signified. Therefore the lion and the bear came and took away the ram from the midst of the flock, because persecutors carried off some of the faithful from the Church and killed them for the name of Christ. And just as David, having pursued, struck the lion and the bear and rescued the ram from their mouth, so too our Redeemer pursued certain persecutors when he inflicted upon them in this present age the punishment they deserved, as he did first to Herod, and then a second, and a third, and many others. He struck them when he condemned them to eternal death; he rescued the ram from their mouth because he freed the faithful people from their ravaging. And again they rose up against David, because in the times of the persecutors, when some were punished, others rose up against Christ, to extinguish utterly his name and worship. But David seized them by the jaw, and strangled and killed them (1 Sam. 17:35). By the jaw, which is close to the mouth, are signified the blasphemies of the Jews and pagans. Christ seized the jaw of the persecutors and strangled and killed them when, as emperors came to believe in Christ, every form of pagan worship and error was so choked out that none of the persecutors dared open his mouth to blaspheme Christ; and all were wiped out, as the nations came to believe in Christ.

Likewise, when David went to war against the Philistine, he took with him his staff, which he always had in his hands, and he chose for himself five very clear stones from the stream and put them into the bag that he had with him, and he took the sling in his hand and advanced against the Philistine. By the staff with which a man is supported, the apostles are signified, who, as it were, supported Christ when they completed the work for which he had come into the world, as the apostle Paul says: “For we are God’s co-workers” (1 Cor. 3:9). By his bag, however, his humanity is signified; by the five stones, the five books of Moses, which he put into the bag, because he gave to the man whom he had assumed the knowledge of the five books. By his sling, moreover, we can understand his tongue. The advance of David against the Philistine can be understood in two ways according to allegory: our Savior advanced against the Philistine when he was led into the desert by the Holy Spirit to be tempted by the Devil and to conquer the Devil. David put five stones into the bag, but with one he struck down Goliath; because our Savior possessed equally the knowledge of the five books, but from one, that is, Deuteronomy, he took three testimonies, by which he overcame the Devil’s three temptations. And just as David cast the stone with the sling and killed Goliath, so also our Lord Jesus Christ brought forth testimonies with the tongue of Holy Scripture and defeated the Devil.

Alternatively: David went to the battle against the Philistine; likewise Christ, in order to conquer the Devil, hurried to his Passion. David cast down Goliath with a stone; likewise Christ destroyed the Devil by his own death. And since David did not have a sword in his hand, he took the sword of the Philistine and killed him with his own sword, and cut off his head. The sword of the Devil is death: for through the envy of the Devil, death entered into the world (Wisdom 2:24). This is the sword with which he killed first the man, then humanity, until it was redeemed by Christ. We run upon this sword when we sin; but Christ did not have this sword, because he had not committed sin, nor was deceit found in his mouth (1 Peter 2:22). He was not liable to original sin either, because he had not arisen from the intercourse of a man, who cannot be without sin. Therefore Christ, who did not have death as a debt, borrowed death from the Devil, who was its author, and through it slew that same prince of death. By the head of the Philistine, humanity is signified in this place. David cut off the head of the Philistine, because Christ separated the multitude of the elect from the body of the Devil. He brought it to Jerusalem, because he placed the multitude of the elect in heavenly beatitude, which is called Jerusalem.

But concerning Elijah and Elisha, although their deeds are full of spiritual meanings, let us bring forward only those which are recalled by the Lord in the Gospel. For when the Lord came to Nazareth, his fellow citizens said to him: “The great things we have heard were done in Capernaum, do them here also in your own country.” And he said to them: “Amen I say to you, that there were many widows in the days of Elijah in Israel, when the heaven was shut, and Elijah was sent to none of them, except to Sarepta of Sidon, to a widow woman. And there were many lepers in the days of Elisha in Israel, and none of them was cleansed except Naaman the Syrian” (Luke 4:23-27). In the book of Kings it is written that when Ahab, king of Israel, began to persecute Elijah, at the Lord’s command he went into the desert and sat beside the brook Cherith; and ravens brought him bread and meat in the morning, and bread and meat in the evening, and he drank from the brook (1 Kings 17:1-7). Elijah prefigured Christ; by the brook Cherith, however, we must understand the Law. Cherith is interpreted “falsehood,” not that the Law is a falsehood; for wise teachers are accustomed to present parables to their hearers, so that the things they wish to say may be understood more clearly, and they introduce beasts and birds speaking. If you look only at the words of the parables, you will see that they are falsehood; but if you look at the meaning, you will recognize the truth. Likewise certain legal instructions, if you attend only to the letter, will seem useless to you; if to the spiritual understanding, a great truth will shine forth for you in the letter. Certain legal instructions, therefore, are “falsehood” to the Jews, because they understand them in a carnal way; but to us they are truth, because we strive to understand them spiritually. By the ravens, in fact, are signified the priests of the Jews, darkened by the veil of their hearts; by the meats, the continual sacrifice which was offered morning and evening. The breads, moreover, signify the loaves of the presence, which were constantly set before the Lord; and he drank from the brook, because Christ took delight in the observances of the Law. But after many days the brook dried up, because when Christ came in the flesh and preached the Gospel, the legal observance ceased, and from that time on it was of no profit, as the Lord says: “The Law and the Prophets were until John,” etc. (Matt. 11:13). And again he says to Israel through the prophet: “Do not offer sacrifice any more in vain. Incense is an abomination to me; I will not endure your new moons and your sabbaths; your assemblies are wicked” (Isa. 1:13).

And the Lord said to Elijah: Go to Zarephath of the Sidonians, and stay there; for I have commanded a widow woman there to feed you (1 Kings 17:9). He arose and went to Zarephath. Elijah went to Zarephath, because Christ, abandoning the faithless Jews, passed to the Gentiles through his apostles. Now Zarephath is interpreted as “burned” or “scarcity of bread”; for the Gentiles, who were the city of the Devil and who are signified by Zarephath, were set ablaze by the fire of vices. Of this fire Scripture says: All of you who kindle a fire, girded with flames, walk in the light of your fire and in the flames which you have kindled for yourselves (Isa. 50:11). For they are girded with flames who are burned on every side by the fire of vices, and they are also called “scarcity of bread,” because no preacher had ever come to them to grant them the nourishment of the word of God. And when he came to the gate of the city, a widow woman appeared, gathering wood. The woman has the same meaning as the city; for she signifies the Church gathered from the Gentiles. She was a widow because, through her sins, she had lost God, her husband. And he called her and said to her: Bring me a little water in a vessel, that I may drink. He asked for lesser things so that he might reach greater ones; for in that little water, which refreshes the soul of the thirsty, we can understand the will of those who listen; because as water cools the tongue of the thirsty, so the eager spirit of listeners refreshes the minds of preachers. And as she was going to bring it, he cried after her, saying: Bring me also a morsel of bread in your hand. What we do not know is, as it were, behind our back; therefore Elijah cried after the woman’s back, because holy preachers proclaimed to the Gentiles the God whom they did not know; and therefore in the morsel of bread we can understand the beginnings of faith.

And because they come suitably to the faith who first strive to confess their sins, it is rightly added: “And the woman said to him: As the Lord your God lives, I have no bread, except only as much flour in a jar as a handful can grasp, and a little oil in a flask.” (1 Kings 17:12) The flask was a vessel in which oil was kept. We can, in fact, understand by the flour reason, and by the oil works of mercy. Therefore the Gentile people, recognizing that they had erred gravely, confessed that there was no good in them, except only that they seemed to have a little reason and a little from works of mercy. For the Gentiles had little reason; because they were rational in part, but in the greatest part irrational. For what is more irrational than to say to wood or stone: “You are my god”? And they had also little mercy; because nature itself often pressed them to this, that they might help the wretched. By the vessels in which the flour and oil were contained, their hearts are signified. “Behold, I am gathering two sticks, that I may go in and prepare it for myself and my son, that we may eat and die.” (1 Kings 17:12) The sticks that are gathered in the city have no usefulness except to be burned in the fire. By the sticks, therefore, are signified the errors of the Gentiles invented by diabolical deceit. The woman was gathering two sticks, because the Gentile people, from the teaching of philosophers and poets, gathered various errors, on account of which they would be handed over to perpetual fire. But what does it mean when she says: “That we may eat and die”? For we eat in order to preserve life in the body; but she says that she eats in order that she may die; because the teaching of the Gentiles brings death rather than life.

To whom Elijah answered: “Go and do as you have said; nevertheless, from that little flour make for me a small bread baked under the ashes, and bring it to me; but for yourself and your son you shall make afterwards.” (1 Kings 17:13) By bread, faith is signified; by ashes, however, repentance is often indicated, as blessed Job says to the Lord: “Therefore I reproach myself, and do penance in dust and ashes” (Job 42:6). Therefore bread is covered with ashes when any unbeliever, coming to the faith, first strives to weep over the evils he has done, so that he may approach more fittingly to receive the faith of Christ, which is given in baptism. Therefore Elijah commanded the woman to make bread from the flour and bring it to him, because holy preachers urged the nations to prepare for God the food of faith from the good of reason which they had received from God, and to bring it to them; for each person coming to baptism brings faith to the preacher when, at the priest’s questioning as to what he believes, he confesses. Elijah ate the bread which he had commanded the woman to make, because the progress of the children is the reward of the preachers. For this reason Elijah commanded the woman that she should first prepare bread for him, and afterwards for herself and her son; because the conversion of a sinner is first credited to the preacher, but afterwards the converted person prepares bread for himself, when, exercising himself in good works, he obtains that he become an heir of the heavenly homeland. And when Elijah says: “The jar of flour shall not fail, nor the flask of oil be diminished until the day when the Lord will send rain upon the earth” (1 Kings 17:14), this signifies that faith and good works will not fail from the hearts of the elect, until the Lord shall come to judgment with all his saints.

Similarly, Elisha also prefigured Christ, and Naaman represented the people of the nations. There were many lepers in Israel, and none of them was cleansed except Naaman the Syrian, because when Christ came in the flesh, both the Jews and the Gentiles were surrounded by the leprosy of their sins; but as the Jews remained in their wickedness, the nations were cleansed from the leprosy of their sins. Therefore Elisha ordered Naaman, saying: Go and wash seven times in the Jordan, and your flesh will receive health, and you will be made clean (2 Kings 5:10). He went and washed seven times in the Jordan, and his flesh was restored like the flesh of a little child, and he was cleansed from the leprosy. The water of the Jordan prefigured baptism, because in it Christ was baptized. Naaman was washed in the waters of the Jordan and cleansed from leprosy; for the people of the nations have been purified from the leprosy of their sins by the water of holy baptism. And his sevenfold washing prefigured the sevenfold Holy Spirit, through whose working the remission of sins is given in baptism; for just as the body is visibly washed by the waters of baptism, so the soul is invisibly cleansed from all sins through the working of the Holy Spirit. But Gehazi, who received the payment for the healing from Naaman and inherited his leprosy, signifies those who sell to men the teaching of their preaching: these the Lord in the Gospel calls not shepherds but hirelings (Jn. 10:12). Therefore Elisha bestowed healing, and Gehazi received payment; because from those whom the divine mercy saves, perverse teachers exact the price of their healing. And therefore they become heirs of their leprosy, because by remaining in the evils which these others have despised through the grace of God, they draw upon themselves everlasting condemnation. Concerning the opening of the four seals, which certainly refers to the Old Testament, we have said a few things out of many, so that we might show that the opening of the seals is nothing other than the revealing of spiritual meanings in the Old Testament, which has been made for us through Christ. And it follows:

Revelation 6:8a And behold a pale horse, and the one who sat upon it, his name was Death, and Hell followed him.

These things that are said about the fourth rider seem to refer to Antichrist; but since we have interpreted the previous three riders in a good sense, so that they might fit with the opening of the three seals, the logical order compels us also to understand this one in a good sense, insofar as it agrees with the opening of the fourth seal, which we have shown refers to the prophets. Therefore, the pale horse signifies the prophets. Now paleness comes to human beings in various ways: sometimes from fear, sometimes from sickness, sometimes from death taking hold of the whole body, and in many other ways people grow pale. Therefore the prophets, who foretold to the sinning people that they would be given over to plunder and the sword and destruction, are not inappropriately said to have had a pale color. But the rider of this horse is the Lord, who dwelt in his prophets. Yet it seems very harsh that he is said to have had the name of Death; for he himself is the life of all his elect, just as he himself says: “I am the life” (Jn. 14:6). But if we examine the divine Scriptures carefully, we shall find that our Lord Jesus Christ is also called Death. For just as he is the life of all the elect, to whom he daily grants eternal life, so he can also be called the death of the depraved, whom, because of their crimes, he daily hands over to everlasting death; for he has the power to destroy both soul and body in Gehenna (Matt. 10:28). Therefore he is said to have had the name of Death, because he brought death upon the sinning Israelite people, as he clearly shows in the Song of Deuteronomy, when he says: “A fire is kindled in my wrath, and it shall burn to the lowest parts of hell [inferni]; and it shall devour the earth with its produce, and burn the foundations of the mountains. I will heap evils upon them, and I will spend my arrows on them: they shall be wasted with hunger, and birds shall devour them with a most bitter bite. I will send the teeth of beasts among them, with the fury of those that trail upon the earth and of serpents. Outside the sword shall lay them waste, and inside terror,” and so on (Deut. 32:22–25). Hell [infernus] was following this horseman, because hell swallowed up all those who despised the threats of the prophets which they had proclaimed from the mouth of the Lord to the people. Therefore it also continues:

Revelation 6:8b And power was given to him over the four parts of the earth to kill with sword, and with famine, and with death, and with the beasts of the earth.

By the four parts of the earth the whole land of Israel is indicated. But how they were consumed by sword, and famine, and death, and the beasts of the earth, the books of the Prophets and of the Kings clearly show. We must indeed inquire why the opening of four seals refers to the Old Testament, but that of three refers to the New. For there are four elements by which, according to the body, we survive; therefore the Old Testament is not inappropriately placed under the number four, because the law of Moses promised to the Hebrew people nothing other than things that refer to the four elements. But the New Testament is placed under the number three, because the faith of the Holy Trinity, without which we cannot be saved, is especially commended in it.

Revelation 6:9a And when he had opened the fifth seal.

The opening of the fifth seal, and of the sixth and seventh, as we have already said, belongs to the opening of the New Testament; and although the New Testament is the opening of the Old Testament, still many things are set down in it obscurely, which must be explained. Therefore the Savior opened the fifth, sixth, and seventh seals when he made known to the teachers of the Church those things which he himself had spoken in parables and had carried out in a figurative way. But concerning the exposition of the New Testament it is not necessary for us to say anything, since it has been wonderfully and abundantly explained by the holy fathers. Therefore the opening of the fifth seal refers to the martyrs: Christ then opened the fifth seal when he revealed to the teachers of the Church those things which he had spoken, through parables and certain figures, about the persecution of the martyrs and their glorification. He also opened the fifth seal when he bestowed on them the heavenly glory which he had promised, and made them glorious in the world among human beings.

Revelation 6:9b I saw under the altar of God the souls of those slain because of the word of God and the testimony of Jesus which they had.

The altar of God is Christ: therefore the souls of the saints rest beneath the altar, because just as the members are placed beneath the head and cling to it, so also the souls of the saints, established in heavenly glory, are subject to Christ the Savior and are bound to him through the union of the members.

Revelation 6:10 And they cried out with a loud voice, saying: “How long, holy and true Lord, do you not judge and do not avenge our blood on those who dwell on the earth?”

Since Scripture commands that we are not to return evil for evil (Rom. 12:17), and the Lord says in the Gospel: “Love your enemies, and do good to those who hate you” (Matt. 5:44), what does it mean that the saints, established in heaven, seek vengeance on their enemies? But we know that the saints of God desire the last day, on which they will receive from the Lord both their bodies and the full reward of their labors. For the Lord showed that this day should be desired by his disciples when he foretold the signs of the day of judgment, saying: “But when you see these things happening, look up and lift up your heads, because your redemption is drawing near” (Luke 21:28). And in the Lord’s Prayer we continually ask, “Your kingdom come” (Matt. 6:10). But because the resurrection and glorification of the saints will occur on the day of judgment, and the day of judgment will be condemnation for the wicked, the saints ask for vengeance on their enemies when they ask that the day of the Lord may come, because on that day they will be condemned with eternal condemnation. Yet here a question arises: why do the saints desire the day of the Lord, when the prophet says, “Woe to those who desire the day of the Lord” (Amos 5:18)? For we know that the day of the Lord is the condemnation of the wicked, as we have already said. And what need was there for the prophet to say, “Woe to those who desire the day of the Lord,” when no wicked person is found who either desires his own death or the day of the Lord, since he fears being condemned in it? And because what the prophet said is true, we must investigate what it signifies. Indeed, the condemnation of the wicked is their sins: whoever therefore desires to go to bed with a prostitute, desires his own condemnation; whoever desires to get drunk, desires his own condemnation; whoever desires to steal a horse or an ox or anything like that, desires his own condemnation; whoever desires to inflict some harm on his neighbor, desires his own condemnation. Therefore, since the condemnation of the wicked will be the day of judgment, whoever desires to commit those crimes for which he will be condemned on the day of judgment, desires the day of the Lord, that is, he desires his own condemnation.

Revelation 6:11a And there were given to them each a white robe.

The white robes are the glory of holy souls; the saints of God now possess individual white robes, because while their bodies rest in the earth, they enjoy the eternal blessedness of the soul alone. They will then receive double robes when, after the resurrection, souls together with bodies will possess without end the glory of the heavenly homeland.

Revelation 6:11b And it was said to them that they should rest yet for a little time, until their fellow servants and their brothers should be fulfilled, who are to be killed as they were.

A little time is the time until the day of judgment; it is called little because, in comparison with the future time, it is very small. Therefore the saints who desire this, that the day of judgment should come, on which they will receive both their bodies and the full reward for their labors, are ordered to wait for a little time, until the number of their brothers is fulfilled; because the day of judgment will not come until the whole number of the elect is complete. But we must inquire why only the souls of those slain for the name of Christ are said to have rested beneath the altar of God and to have received individual robes, when the souls of all the elect rest beneath this altar and likewise possess these same robes. Therefore, by the souls of those slain we must understand not only martyrs, but also all the souls of the righteous; because they too are dead to the world, so that they might live to God, as the Apostle says: “For you have died, and your life is hidden with Christ in God” (Col. 3:3). And he says of himself: “The world has been crucified to me, and I to the world” (Gal. 6:14); and: “All who want to live devoutly in Christ Jesus suffer persecution” (2 Tim. 3:12); and: “Through many tribulations we must enter the kingdom of God” (Acts 14:21). And John, the author of this book, is described as having drunk the cup of the Lord, although he rested without being killed by the sword11.

Revelation 6:12a And when he had opened the sixth seal.

The opening of the sixth seal refers to the casting down of the Jews, as we have said, and to the calling of the Gentiles. And since Christ spoke much in parables about the destruction of the Jews and the choosing of the Gentiles, he also performed certain acts that signified this; he opened the sixth seal when, for the teachers of the Church, he made clear by the grace of his inspiration the things that he had said and done through figures. He also opened the sixth seal when he fulfilled, in deed, the things he had foretold.

Revelation 6:12b A great earthquake took place.

In this passage the Jews are represented by the earth: a great earthquake took place when this nation was devastated by the Romans.

Revelation 6:12c And the sun became black like a sack made of goat’s hair.

By the sun the same people of the Jews are represented: therefore the sun became black like a sack of goat’s hair, because the Jewish people, who, on account of the knowledge of the one God and the keeping of the Law, shone in the world like the sun among the other nations, became hateful to all men because of their iniquities; for penitents are clothed with sackcloth to show that they are sinners. Therefore the sun took on the blackness of sackcloth, because the crimes of the Jews became known to all the nations.

Revelation 6:12d And the moon became like blood.

The moon has the same meaning as the sun; it signifies the Synagogue. Therefore the moon was seen to have the appearance of blood, because it became evident to all that the Jews were wiped out because of the shedding of the blood of Christ and of his saints.

Revelation 6:13 And the stars fell upon the earth, as a fig tree casts its unripe figs when it is shaken by a great wind.

By the stars are signified the leaders of the priests, the scribes, and the Pharisees; by the fig tree, however, we can understand the land of the Jews; and the unripe figs of the same tree are called useless fruits, which quickly fall when that same tree is shaken by the wind. Therefore the fig tree was shaken by the wind and cast out its useless fruits from itself, because the land of the Jews was invaded by the Romans, and drove out from itself the useless nation, hateful to God, so that it might become a dwelling place for the faithful. Therefore the stars fell to the earth like unripe figs, because the same punishment overtook those who, through the teaching of the Law, shone among the people like stars, as it did the rest of the multitude.

Revelation 6:14a And the heaven withdrew like a book that is rolled up.

By “heaven” the Old Testament is signified; when a book is rolled up, its writing is hidden from the eyes of those who look at it. Therefore the book is rolled up for the Jews, because they were unwilling to understand the spiritual meaning in the Old Testament that pointed to Christ. Therefore the heaven withdrew like a rolled-up book, because the Old Testament abandoned the Jews and passed over to the Gentiles.

Revelation 6:14b And every mountain and island were moved from their places.

And since the land of the Jews is mountainous, we can understand by the mountains those who lived in the cities, and by the islands those who lived in the countryside. They were moved from their places, because all were either exterminated or led away into captivity.

Revelation 6:15-17 And the kings of the earth, and the princes, and the tribunes, and the rich, and the strong, and every slave and free person hid themselves in caves and in the rocks of the mountains, and they say to the mountains: Fall upon us, and hide us from the face of the One who sits upon the throne, and from the wrath of the Lamb; for the great day of their wrath has come, and who will be able to stand?

By the kings of the earth, the princes and the tribunes, those who seemed to rule this people, we can understand: by the rich and the strong, the slaves and the free, the rest of the multitude. How they hid themselves in caves and in the rocks of the mountains, Josephus clearly relates, who describes the destruction of Jerusalem (Josephus, The Jewish War 6.11). For such was the affliction that, being consumed by long-lasting famine, and oppressed by enemies from without and, much worse, by bandits from within, they hid themselves in wells and sewers and in certain hidden places in the earth, desiring to die, so that they might end their long misery by the short cut of death. And knowing that they were suffering these things for their crimes, and that after death they would endure the torments of hell [inferni], they desired to be crushed by the mountains under which they were hiding and to be concealed from the face of God and from the face of His Christ, whom they knew they had gravely offended. For the Lord also, when He was being led to His Passion, foretold to the women who were lamenting Him that they would suffer these things, saying: Daughters of Jerusalem, do not weep for me, but weep for yourselves and for your children; for behold, days will come when they will say: Blessed are the barren, those who have not borne, and the breasts that have not nursed! Then they will begin to say to the mountains: Fall upon us; and to the hills: Cover us. For if they do these things in the green wood, what will happen in the dry? (Luke 23:28-31). He calls Himself the green wood, but them the dry wood, as if He had said: If they do these things to me, who am wood according to my humanity, but green according to my divinity, that is, who am God and man; what will happen to those who are dry wood, that is, who do not have the greenness of faith? It is understood: they will perish in the worst way. And the fig tree which the Lord, before His Passion, by cursing made dry because He found no fruit on it (Matt. 21:19), signified those same Jews, whom He condemned with a perpetual curse, because He found no fruit of faith in them.

Chapter 7

Revelation 7:1a After these things I saw four angels standing at the four corners of the earth.

With the casting down of the Jews mentioned beforehand, he now begins to relate how the Church was gathered from the nations. For by the four angels four kingdoms are signified, namely those of the Assyrians, the Persians, the Macedonians, and the Romans. But because the power of the four kingdoms had passed over to the Romans alone, by the four angels only the Romans are understood, since they claimed for themselves the power of the four kingdoms; and by the four corners are signified all the nations contained within the four quarters of the world, which the Romans had subjected to themselves.

Revelation 7:1b Holding back the four winds of the earth, so that they should not blow upon the earth, nor upon the sea, nor upon any tree.

By the winds, which stir up the clouds so that, watering the earth, they make its face cheerful and make it fruitful, is signified the peace longed for by all mortals, which both gladdens the minds of men and grants them the opportunity to devote themselves to their own interests. By the earth and the sea all nations are figured; and by the trees, those who held leadership among them. Therefore the four angels were holding the four winds, so that they should not blow upon the earth, because the Romans, who are signified by the four angels, filling the whole earth with slaughter, plunder, and the deaths of men, did not permit humanity to be peaceful and at rest.

Revelation 7:2a And I saw another angel coming down from the rising of the sun, having the seal of the living God.

The other angel is Christ, of whom it is written: “And his name shall be called the Angel of great counsel” (Isa. 9:6). He is the sun, of whom it is written: “But for you who fear my name, the sun of righteousness shall rise, with healing in its wings” (Mal. 4:2). From the rising of the sun Christ descended, that is, from himself; since he did not take on flesh through the intercourse of a man, but from himself, who indeed for himself created spotless flesh from the flesh of the Virgin. Now the seal of the living God is divinity: for, being in the flesh, he had the seal of the living God, because he performed works which showed that he was the only Son of God, as he himself says: “The works that I do, they bear witness about me” (Jn. 10:25).

Revelation 7:2b-3 And he cried with a loud voice to the four angels, to whom it had been given to harm the earth and the sea, saying: Do not harm the earth or the sea or the trees, until we have sealed the servants of our God on their foreheads.

For God to have cried out is for Him to have acted, since by His coming He brought it about that the Romans ceased from oppressing humanity, and that peace was made throughout the whole world, as the Psalmist says: In his days justice shall arise, and abundance of peace (Ps. 71:7), until the seeds of faith were scattered among all nations, and those who had been predestined to eternal life were sealed with the seal of faith.

Revelation 7:4 And I heard the number of those who were sealed, one hundred and forty‑four thousand sealed out of every tribe of the sons of Israel.

By the one hundred and forty‑four thousand, the elect of the present Church are indicated; for twelve times twelve makes one hundred and forty‑four. Therefore all who keep the faith of the twelve apostles and imitate their deeds are included in this number. But we must ask why they are said to be gathered from every tribe of the sons of Israel, when far more people from the nations than from the Jews have come to believe. Yet if we pay attention to the meanings of the names, we will find all the elect in the names of the twelve tribes; for the names of the twelve tribes refer to all the elect.

Revelation 7:5-8 From the tribe of Judah twelve thousand sealed; from the tribe of Reuben twelve thousand sealed; from the tribe of Gad twelve thousand sealed; from the tribe of Asher twelve thousand sealed; from the tribe of Naphtali twelve thousand sealed; from the tribe of Manasseh twelve thousand sealed; from the tribe of Simeon twelve thousand sealed; from the tribe of Levi twelve thousand sealed; from the tribe of Issachar twelve thousand sealed; from the tribe of Zebulun twelve thousand sealed; from the tribe of Joseph twelve thousand sealed; from the tribe of Benjamin twelve thousand sealed.

Judah is placed first, but Benjamin last; yet if we look into the interpretations of the names, we find Judah lower and Benjamin higher; for whoever desires to ascend to God begins from lesser things, so that by growing step by step he may arrive at greater things, as the Psalmist says: The saints will go from strength to strength (Ps. 83:8). And because the saints ascend to God by the journey of virtues, he adds: The God of gods will be seen in Zion (ibid.). Judah is interpreted “confession.” Judah therefore is placed first, because whoever desires to be converted to God after sins must first confess his sins, as the apostle James says: Confess your sins to one another, and pray for one another, that you may be saved (James 5:16), that is, so that through confession of sins and repentance he may make God favorable to himself, whom he had offended. And because the one who repents in a saving way and weeps over the evils he has done causes joy in heaven and turns the eyes of God’s mercy toward himself, as the Lord says: Return to me, and I will return to you (Zech. 1:3). Reuben is placed second, whose name is interpreted “son of vision,” because through repentance he has been made a son of vision, who was previously a son of perdition. After Reuben comes Gad, which is interpreted “girded,” because it is necessary that the one who repents for having done evil and asks not to fall back into the same, arm himself against all vices and cut off from himself carnal pleasures; for it cannot come about that he abstains from sins if he rests in carnal delights.

And because whoever fights bravely against vices already begins to be blessed, Aser is placed in the sequence, which is interpreted “blessed.” After Aser, Nephthalim is placed, which is interpreted “my enlargement.” For everyone who knows he has offended God and wants the divine mercy to help him must himself also have mercy on whomever he can, and in whatever way he can, as the Lord says: “Blessed are the merciful, for they shall obtain mercy” (Matt. 5:7). For the mind of that person is expanded on every side, who, through the love he ought to show to his neighbors, has mercy on all people he can. After Nephthalim comes Manasses, which is interpreted “forgetful”; because it is fitting that the one who mourns his sins, who gives alms to the poor so that his sins may be forgiven, should so abandon those same sins that he appears to have completely forgotten them.

And so Simeon follows, whose name is interpreted as “hearing”; for he, because of the evils he has done, praying that they may be forgiven him, is heard by God, since he abandons all vices completely. Then Levi follows, whose name is interpreted as “added” or “taken up”; because the one who, for the sins he has committed, has now practiced repentance, who has fought against vices and conquered them, who has shown mercy to those in need, and by these works has earned that his sins be forgiven, already secure in pardon, casts away from his mind the fear of eternal fire, and is wholly taken up into the love of God and the heavenly homeland, and is joined to those who are called and truly are sons of God. Issachar follows, whose name is interpreted as “wages” or “reward.” But we shall see the meaning of this name more clearly if we set forth what his mother Leah said at his birth; for she said: “God has given me my reward, because I gave my maidservant to my husband” (Gen. 30:18). And therefore she called his name Issachar, that is, “reward.” Our flesh ought to be our maidservant; our husband is our God. We give our maidservant to our husband if we bind our flesh to the service of God, if we compel it to keep watch, to fast, and to abstain, and if we subdue its pleasures. Issachar, therefore, was given to Leah as a reward by God; because to those who tame the flesh, who extinguish vices, that reward is given by God, that they may be enriched with virtues.

And therefore, after Issachar, Zebulun is placed, which is interpreted as “dwelling of strength.” But who is strong except God, of whom the Psalmist says: “The Lord strong and mighty, the Lord mighty in battle” (Ps. 23:8). And what is the dwelling of strength, if not the habitation of God? For God dwells in that person in whom faith, patience, chastity, humility, love, and the other virtues reside. And because through the exercise of virtues the rewards of merits are increased, Joseph follows, whose name is interpreted as “increase.” Benjamin is placed last, or rather as even higher, whose name is interpreted as “son of the right hand.” For the Son is called the right hand of the Father; and the Gospel says that the holy ones of God will be placed at the right hand of God. Therefore the elect are made sons of the right hand when, taken away from this life, they are set in the heavenly dwellings [sedibus]; where there is nothing left-handed, but all is right hand (Matt. 25:33). Therefore, since we have carried the interpretations of the names of the twelve tribes up to that point at which they are placed in the heavenly dwellings, it is fitting that we refer what follows to the glorification of holy souls.

Revelation 7:9-10 After this I saw a great multitude that no one could count, from every nation and tribe and people and language, standing before the throne and in the presence of the Lamb . . . And they cried out with a loud voice, saying: Salvation belongs to our God who sits upon the throne, and to the Lamb.

By the multitude above, which is included in the number, the elect who labor in the Church of God at every period are signified, as we have already said. And because many are called, but few are chosen (Matt. 20:16), and in comparison with the wicked the multitude of the elect is small, not without reason are the elect who in every age labor in the Church of God represented by the number one hundred and forty‑four thousand; but in heavenly blessedness, where at every period people are gathered from all nations, tribes, and languages, such a multitude of them comes to be that it cannot be comprehended by number. By the white robes the purity of souls is signified; the palms, however, are set as a sign of victory. Therefore not only do the holy martyrs, to whom victory is especially ascribed, possess palms, but all the elect who, placed in this present world, have fought bravely against visible and invisible enemies, possess them in heavenly blessedness. The palms are the rewards of the saints’ victory; and they are said to cry out with a loud voice: Salvation belongs to our God who sits upon the throne, and to the Lamb. The holy ones of God never cease from the praises of God, nor is there any toil in praising God; for just as the vision of God is sweet to the saints, so too is the praise of God.

Revelation 7:11-12 And all the angels stood around the throne, and the elders, and the four living creatures, and they fell in the midst of the throne on their faces and worshiped God, saying: Amen, blessing, and glory, and wisdom, and thanksgiving, honor, and power, and strength to our God forever and ever, amen.

Above, thousands upon thousands of angels around the throne are said to praise God; but here they are not contained by any number, rather all are said to stand around the throne. We can, by the angels mentioned above who are numbered, understand those who are appointed to various tasks in the world; and those who are set without number, those who dwell with the Lord in heavenly places. And here too seven blessings are set forth, as above, to show that in the same way these bless God who watch over the world by their governance, just as those who dwell with the Lord in heaven.

Revelation 7:13-14a And one of the elders answered and said to me: “These who are clothed in white robes, who are they and from where have they come?” And I said to him: “My lord, you know.”

He asks in order to teach; for by “elder” the teachers of the Church are understood, and by “John” the listeners. For holy preachers reveal, in whatever ways they can, the glory of holy souls to their hearers, so that their minds may be kindled with love for the heavenly homeland.

Revelation 7:14b And he said to me: “These are the ones who have come out of great tribulation; and they have washed their robes and made them white in the blood of the Lamb.”

Here he shows that these things refer not to the elect who labor in this life, but to those who rest with the Lord in the heavenly life. For the saints do not come out of great tribulation, but into the world to endure great tribulations, as Solomon says: “Man is born to labor, and the bird to flight” (Job 5:7). And the Lord says in the Gospel: “Blessed are those who suffer persecution for the sake of righteousness, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven” (Matt. 5:10). And Paul says: “Through many tribulations we must enter into the kingdom of heaven” (Acts 14:21); for here is the time of tribulation, there the time of peace. Therefore the saints come out of great tribulation, because, taken away from this life where it is the time of tribulation, they are placed in eternal rest. Moreover, they have washed their robes because, while living in this life, they cleansed their souls from every stain of sins: first indeed through baptism, and afterwards through penance and tears, and works of mercy, and other good works. These are also said to have been made white in the blood of the Lamb, because the souls of the elect, which were previously wrapped in the blackness of sins, through the redemption by the blood of Christ have taken on the brightness of purity.

Revelation 7:15a Therefore they are before the throne of God, and they serve him day and night in his temple.

For in the presence of God the saints of God stand, and they unceasingly delight in the vision of him. The temple of God he calls the heavenly homeland, and the service of God he calls the love of God. For what service is so acceptable to God as that he be loved with all the heart and all strength? And who loves God so passionately as those who constantly contemplate his face?

Revelation 7:15b And he who sits upon the throne dwells above them.

The throne of God is the saints of God, as we said above. Those therefore who are signified by the throne of God are the very ones upon whom God dwells: but God dwells upon his saints when he enlightens them with his light, surrounds them, and protects them.

Revelation 7:16a They will not hunger, nor thirst anymore.

Since the Lord says in the Gospel: “Blessed are those who hunger and thirst for righteousness, for they shall be satisfied” (Matt. 5:6), why are the saints of God here said never to hunger? But, as we said above, this is believed to refer not to the elect who dwell in this life, but to those who rejoice with God in heavenly blessedness. For the righteous, placed in this life, seeing the unrighteousness of men, hunger and thirst for righteousness, desiring that all men be righteous, as they themselves are. They also hunger and thirst that their own righteousness may grow day by day; they hunger and thirst for eternal life. But the souls of the saints will not hunger nor thirst anymore, because what they hungered for, they have now been found worthy to receive.

Revelation 7:16b And the sun shall not fall upon them, nor any scorching heat.

By “sun” we can understand the tribulations which the saints endure from men; by “scorching heat,” the temptations of demons or carnal pleasures. And because the saints, placed in this life, have overcome the persecutions of wicked men by bearing them, have conquered the temptations of demons, and have extinguished carnal desires within themselves, once placed in heavenly blessedness they feel no toil forever.

Revelation 7:17a For the Lamb who is in the midst of the throne will rule them.

God rules his saints when he so orders their wills that they can want nothing except what God wills.

Revelation 7:17b And he will lead them to the springs of the waters of life.

The fountain of life is Christ, who says: I am the life (Jn. 14:6). For whatever is without him is dead; but whatever is in him is life. To the fountain of life Christ leads his saints, because he leads them to himself, so that they may be one with him. By the waters we can understand these same saints; for he himself is also the fountain of waters, because all holiness of the elect proceeds from him, as he said: Without me you can do nothing (Jn. 15:5).

Revelation 7:17c And God will wipe away every tear from their eyes.

For the time for weeping was here; the time for laughing will be in that blessedness, as the Lord says: Blessed are those who now mourn, for they shall be comforted (Matt. 5:5).

Chapter 8

Revelation 8:1a And when he had opened the seventh seal.

The opening of the seventh seal refers to the birth of Christ; but we must ask why it is placed in the seventh place, and not in the fifth. In Genesis it is written that in six days God made all things that took their origin from heaven and earth, but on the seventh He rested; and therefore He called the seventh day “sabbath,” that is, “rest” (Gen. 2:2). But what is our rest, if not Christ? Therefore, it is not unfitting that at the opening of the seventh seal the birth of Christ is placed, because He Himself is the rest of all His saints, which is prefigured by the seventh day. And because the things that were done at the birth of Christ possess a spiritual meaning, Christ opened the seventh seal when He revealed, through the inspiration of the Holy Spirit to the teachers of the Church, the things that had been done in a symbolic way.

Revelation 8:1b There was silence in heaven for about half an hour.

By this silence is signified the peace that, with the birth of Christ, was established throughout the whole world by Octavian Augustus. But the silence was for about half an hour, because the peace which the Church had among the pagans at the beginning of the faith lasted only a short time; for it was broken by the emperor Nero, and after Peter and Paul were killed, he ordered that all Christians be persecuted.

Revelation 8:2 And I saw seven angels standing in the presence of God, and seven trumpets were given to them.

The order of these seven angels is shown in what follows; for just as an announcer is sent before a king or prince to announce his coming, so also John, because the things signified through the angels were great, wished to call them to mind before he came to relate their story.

Revelation 8:3 Then another angel came and stood before the altar, having a golden censer in his hand; and much incense was given to him, that he might offer from the prayers of all the saints upon the golden altar which is before the Lord.

This angel signifies Christ; he came because through the mystery of the Incarnation he appeared in the world. By the golden altar the Church is indicated. He is said to have stood before the altar; for Christ lay prostrate among unbelievers, who considered him a mere man; but he stood among the faithful, who believed that he was truly God. Therefore he stood before the altar, because to the apostles and the other believers, from whom the Church was then constituted, he revealed that he was God. By the censer which he held in his hand are shown the hearts of the apostles and of the other faithful. It is said to have been golden, because the hearts of the disciples, purified by faith and golden by the wisdom they had learned from Christ, were like gold. The censer is said to have been held in his hand, because the hearts of the apostles were in the hand of God, as Solomon says: “The king’s heart is in the hand of the Lord; he will bend it wherever he wishes” (Prov. 21:1). Was the heart of that Solomon in the hand of God when he was worshipping idols? Was the heart of Antiochus or Herod in the hand of God? Were the hearts of the kings who persecuted the saints of God in the hand of God? They were in the hand of the Devil, who bent them toward whatever crimes he wished; but the hearts of spiritual kings, that is, of the saints, are in the hand of God, and therefore he will bend them wherever he wishes.

Consequently: “And much incense was given to him.” The many incenses are the teachings of the Gospel; for incense is placed in the censer so that, once it has received the heat of the fire, it may spread the fragrance of its scent all around. In the same way Christ placed the teaching of the Gospel in the hearts of his disciples, who, inflamed with the fire of love, spread the fragrance of their faith among all nations. Therefore it also continues: “That he might offer from the prayers of all the saints upon the golden altar which is before God.” There are many kinds of prayers; for the faithful pray with words, begging God that all their sins may be forgiven them. They also pray by giving alms, as the Lord says in the Gospel: “Give alms, and behold, all things are clean for you” (Luke 11:41). And in another place it is written: “Just as water extinguishes fire, so almsgiving extinguishes sin” (Sirach 3:33). They also pray by forgiving their neighbors for what they have done against them, as the Lord says in the Gospel: “If you forgive people their sins, your heavenly Father will also forgive you your sins” (Matt. 6:14). Likewise, they also pray by keeping the commandments of love, as it is written that “love covers all offenses” (Prov. 10:12). Therefore, that person prays to God in a wholesome way who keeps God’s commandments; for it is of no benefit to us to implore God’s mercy with words and to provoke it by our deeds. So God gave “upon the golden altar from the prayers of the saints” when he placed upon the hearts of believers the faith, teaching, and actions of the apostles and of the other preachers; so that they might have from what they could be instructed and what they could imitate. Whence it follows:

Revelation 8:4 And the smoke of the incense went up from the prayers of the saints, from the hand of the angel before the sight of God.

By the smoke of the incense not only the prayers of the faithful are signified, but also the fragrance of good works, which unceasingly rises from the Church before the sight of God. This smoke is said to ascend from the hand of the angel, because whatever good the faithful do, they do with God working in them, as He Himself says: “Without me you can do nothing” (Jn. 15:5).

Revelation 8:5 And the angel took the censer and filled it with fire from the altar, and cast it upon the earth. And there were lightnings, voices, and thunderings, and an earthquake.

By fire the Holy Spirit is signified: therefore the angel, that is, Christ, filled the censer, that is, the hearts of the disciples, with fire from the altar, that is, with the Holy Spirit; when, after his ascension into heaven, on the tenth day, with the Holy Spirit sent from heaven, he filled the hearts of the disciples and gave them knowledge of all languages. And if by the altar the Church is signified, why is the Holy Spirit, before he was given, said to be the fire of the altar, as the same John says: “The Spirit had not yet been given, because Jesus had not yet been glorified”? (Jn. 7:39) But the Holy Spirit, before he was given, was the fire of the altar, because through the grace of forgiveness the Church already possessed him. For the Lord, before his passion, promised that he would give the Holy Spirit to his disciples; and the same John, when he had set down the words of Caiaphas saying, “It is useful for you that one man should die for the people, and that the whole nation should not perish,” added, saying: “But he did not say this of himself; rather, since he was high priest that year, he prophesied that Jesus was going to die for the nation; and not only for the nation, but also that he might gather into one the children of God who were scattered abroad” (Jn. 11:50–52). Were those who would come to believe in Christ, who, scattered among all nations, were serving idols, children of God? They were already children of God by predestination, because they had already been predestined to eternal life. They were truly gathered into one later, when, through the faith which they received in baptism, they were joined to the body of the Church. Therefore the angel cast the fire which he had placed in the censer upon the earth, because the doctrine of faith which he had placed in the hearts of the apostles, through the coming of the Holy Spirit, he poured out by the mouths of the apostles into the hearts of the Jews, who through unbelief were earth.

There were lightnings, voices, and thunderings, and an earthquake. By the lightnings we may understand the miracles that were worked through the apostles; by the voices, the preaching; and by the thunderings is signified the threat of eternal fire. By the earthquake, however, we can understand the conversion of the Jews; for they were converted to repentance through the preaching of the blessed Peter, as the book of the Acts of the Apostles relates: “When they heard this, they were cut to the heart, and said to Peter and the other apostles: ‘Brothers, what shall we do? Show us’” (Acts 2:37). And in what follows it is written that at this first preaching of Peter, three thousand of them believed, and afterwards countless others. May our Lord Jesus Christ, in his mercy, consent to pour out this fire, that is, the Holy Spirit, upon us who are earth, so that from carnal people we may be made spiritual, he who lives and reigns for ever and ever, amen.

On the Fourth Vision

By the abundance of the grace of our Lord Jesus Christ, we have said a little about the openings of the seven seals; now let us bring forward some points about the sounding of the trumpets of the seven angels which follows. By the seven angels are signified the teachers who have been sent by God, and will be sent until the end, to instruct the people of God. And because, by God’s inspiration, the mysteries of the divine Scriptures have been more clearly revealed to the teachers of the Church than to the rest, the order of reason required that, after the opening of the seals, which signifies the unveiling of the divine Scriptures, the seven angels should be set forth, by whom all the teachers of the people of God are signified, who have received the knowledge of the divine Scriptures more abundantly. And just as we divided the whole body of the elect into seven parts, so also the seven angels were seen to have sounded with trumpets: through them all the teachers of the people of God are signified, although they differ somewhat from that arrangement. For above, through the tunic called poderis, which is a long garment and is placed first among the ornaments of the Lord, we said that the righteous who were before the flood were signified, and likewise in what follows through the first horn of the Lamb; through the golden belt on the body of Christ, and through the second horn, the patriarchs and the others who were before the Law; but here, through the first angel, the teachers who were from the beginning of the world up to the Law are signified, and through the second, the teachers of the Law.

Revelation 8:6 And the seven angels prepared themselves to sound the trumpets.

By the sounding of the trumpet, preaching is signified; for the holy preachers are always ready for this purpose, that by the teaching of their preaching they may win for God whomever they can.

Revelation 8:7a And the first angel sounded the trumpet.

By the first angel, as we have already said, the preachers who were before the Law are indicated. Therefore the first angel sounded the trumpet, because the holy men who lived before the Law, although they had no law except the natural one, are believed to have urged whomever they could to this: that they should fear and love God their creator, and that what someone would not wish to suffer, he should not do to another.

Revelation 8:7b And there came hail and fire, mixed with blood, and it was sent upon the earth.

The things that were seen to have happened when the seven angels sounded the trumpets are, in my view, to be taken in a good sense; just as, on the contrary, the pouring out of the bowls of the seven angels which follows is to be taken in a bad sense. Therefore, by the hail that strikes, the words of the holy men are indicated, which, by reproving, struck the hearts of wicked people. This hail is described as having fire and blood mixed with it: by the fire the Holy Spirit is signified, who, dwelling in his saints, became the helper of their words; by the blood, Christ is understood, who was often foretold by the Fathers and prefigured in their deeds; while the earth shows the people of the world subject to sin.

Revelation 8:7c And a third part of the earth was burned … and every green grass was burned.

By the third part of the earth we must understand those who were saved through the teaching and examples of good people: who, by the fire of fear of God and of love, burned up and wiped out whatever was wicked in themselves. The trees, in fact, signify those who held leadership among them: among whom many were saved through the teaching and examples of good people (Gen. 20:17), as Abimelech through Abraham, and through Job his three friends (Job 42:8). By the green grass, however, which is said to be all burned, those are indicated who above were meant by the third part of the earth and the trees that were burned, that is, the elect who lived at that time; for green grass is hard to set on fire, but dry grass is easily consumed. By the green grass, then, the elect are signified, and by the dry, the wicked. Of this dry grass the prophet says: “Truly the people are grass. The grass is dried up and the flower has fallen; but the word of the Lord remains forever” (Isa. 40:7). By the green grass, therefore, those are signified whose faith was green, who burned up by the fire of love everything wicked that was in them.

Revelation 8:8a And the second angel sounded the trumpet.

By the second angel Moses and the other teachers of the Law are signified.

Revelation 8:8b And behold, as it were a great mountain burning with fire was cast into the sea.

By the great mountain the Law of Moses is signified, which, because of the weight of its burden, is likened to a mountain; and because of the spiritual understanding that shone forth in the Law, it is said to burn with fire. The Hebrew people themselves, because of the magnitude of their crimes, are signified by the sea.

Revelation 8:9 And a third part of the sea became blood, and a third part of the creatures that had life in the sea died, and a third part of the ships was destroyed.

Every remission under the Law took place through the pouring out and sprinkling of blood; therefore Moses also sprinkled the people with blood, saying: This is the blood of the Covenant which God has commanded for you (Heb. 9:20). And Aaron and his sons, so that they might be made pure for offering sacrifices to God, the same Moses sprinkled with blood, nor could there be remission without the shedding of blood (Exod. 24:8). Therefore, by the third part of the sea, those chosen ones who lived at that time are signified; and by blood in this passage we may understand the remission of sins. Therefore the third part of the sea was turned into blood, because the elect who were then alive deserved to be cleansed from their sins through the observance of the Law. The third part of the creatures that had life in the sea, which is said to have died, has the same meaning as the third part of the sea that was turned into blood. By the sea, however, as we have already said, the multitude of sinners is signified; while fish, when taken out of the waters, lose their lives. Therefore, as long as the elect are immersed in the waters of their vices, they have sea-bound souls, that is, souls wrapped in the darkness of sins; but when, either through baptism or repentance, they are drawn out from the waters of vice, they as it were lose their former souls, since from being evil they are made good, and they die to the Devil so that they may live to God. Therefore a third part of the fish gave up their lives, because the elect who were then alive died to sin through the observance of the Law and began to live to God. By the ships, which have the waters beneath them and rise above them, the leaders of that people are signified. And the third part that perished signifies those among them who were righteous according to the meaning we have explained; in that they perished and died to sin and lived to God.

Revelation 8:10a And the third angel blew the trumpet, and a star burning like a torch fell from heaven.

The star has the same meaning as the angel; for it signifies the prophets. But we must ask how it is said to have fallen from heaven to earth. The saints in a certain way ascend to heaven when divine love lifts them up to contemplate heavenly things; they fall to earth when love for their neighbors draws them back down, so that by their preaching they may lead sinners back to the way of truth. A torch, moreover, is lit to drive away the darkness of the night. Therefore the words of the prophets were torches, because they drove the darkness of sins from the minds of men. We can also understand by the fire of the torch the Holy Spirit, by whom the prophets were inflamed, and corrected many from their iniquities, and foretold the things that were to come.

Revelation 8:10b And it fell upon a third part of the rivers and upon the springs of waters.

By the rivers and springs the twelve tribes with their families are represented; by the third part, those among them are figured who were marked out for eternal life. The star therefore fell upon the third part of the rivers, because those who were predestined to eternal life received the teaching of the prophets and planted it in their hearts.

Revelation 8:11a And the name of the star is called wormwood.

Wormwood is very bitter; by wormwood, therefore, are signified the threats of the prophets, which they directed against the sinning people, declaring to them that unless they repent, they would be given over as spoil and into captivity to their enemies; and that if even then they did not repent, they would be condemned to eternal death.

Revelation 8:11b And a third part of the waters was turned into wormwood.

Those whom we said above were signified by the third part of the waters, that is, the elect, received the bitterness of penance through the threats of the prophets.

Revelation 8:11c And many men died from the waters, because they were made bitter.

For the bitterness of penance brought death to those who deserved to be saved; for they died according to what was evil in them, and lived according to what they began to be in goodness.

Revelation 8:12a And the fourth angel sounded the trumpet.

The fourth angel signifies Christ and his apostles.

Revelation 8:12b And a third part of the sun was struck, and a third part of the moon, and a third part of the stars, so that a third part of them was darkened, and a third part of the day did not shine, and likewise of the night.

The sun, the moon, and the stars all refer to one thing; for by the sun the people of the Jews are signified, by the moon the Synagogue, and by the stars the leaders of the priests and the Pharisees. But if we should wish to transfer the darkening of the sun and moon and stars to those who were unwilling to believe in Christ, the order established above will be disturbed. For above we have shown that the burning of a third part of the earth, which is said to have been done under the sounding of the trumpet of the first angel; the change of a third part of the sea into blood, which was seen to have been done under the sounding of the trumpet of the second angel; and likewise the change of a third part of the waters into wormwood, which is read to have been done under the sounding of the trumpet of the third angel, must be taken in a good sense, and therefore referring to the elect. It follows, then, that we interpret the striking of the sun and moon and stars in a good sense. Therefore, by the third part of the sun which was struck, that is, darkened, those who from among the people of the Jews believed in Christ are signified; for they were darkened in the sight of the wicked Jews, but they shone in the sight of God. Likewise, by the third part of the moon and of the stars which was darkened, those who from among the chief rulers of the Synagogue and the priests and from among the Pharisees believed in Christ are signified; because they too, on account of the true light, that is, Christ, which they received, were darkened to those whose mental eyes the Devil had blinded, so that they would not believe in Christ; for the brightness of the sun blinds unfocused eyes more than it illuminates them. Indeed, we can understand by the third part of the day the apostles, and by the third part of the night those who from among the Gentiles believed. For the apostles were darkened to those who not only scorned their teaching, but also persecuted them everywhere. Of this darkening of theirs the Lord says: “You will be handed over by parents and brothers and relatives and friends, and they will put some of you to death. And you will be hated by all people because of my name” (Luke 21:16).

Revelation 8:13 And I saw and heard the voice of one eagle flying through the midst of heaven, saying with a loud voice: Woe, woe, woe to those who dwell on the earth, because of the voices of the three angels who were about to sound the trumpet.

By “heaven” in this place the Church is signified, while the eagle signifies Christ and His apostles. The eagle, then, was flying through the midst of heaven and crying out, because Christ first through Judaea, and His apostles through all the nations, preached the Gospel; and they also foretold in many ways what persecutions from adversaries the Church would have to endure. Therefore he set down three woes, of which the first refers to the heretics, who have tried in many ways to attack the Church of God; the second to the pagans, who have persecuted that same Church of God and killed a very great number of the faithful; the third to the Antichrist, who at the end of the world will wage war against the people of God. Nor must we think that the three woes refer to the elect, who have suffered various calamities from the wicked; rather, they refer to those same wicked people, who brought about those calamities. Therefore he says, Woe, woe, woe to those who dwell on the earth; for the wicked, who seek nothing but the earth, are rightly called inhabitants of the earth, but the righteous, who always desire heavenly things, are rightly called inhabitants of heaven, as the apostle Paul says: But our citizenship is in heaven (Philip. 3:20).

Chapter 9

Revelation 9:1a And the fifth angel sounded the trumpet.

The fifth angel signifies the defenders of the Church of the orthodox faith: they sounded the trumpet when they labored in every way for the defense of the faith.

Revelation 9:1b And I saw a star fall from heaven to earth.

By the star the founders of heresies are signified; for just as stars shine in the heavens, so also these men, before they fell, shone in the Church with doctrine and wisdom. It fell from heaven to earth, because, cut off from the Church, they were joined to the body of the depraved, who are signified by the earth. But we must ask why he first placed the heretics, who are signified by the star and the locusts, and afterwards the persecutors, who are signified by the armored horsemen, of whom in what follows he is going to speak in the sounding of the trumpet of the sixth angel. It is because, before the emperors of the Romans had begun to persecute the Church of God, heretics had already arisen, such as Nicolaus, who existed in the times of the apostles, and many others. (Acts 6:5).

Revelation 9:1c And to him was given the key of the pit of the abyss.

By the key is signified the free will of the heretics, which they possessed for evil; by the abyss, their hearts; and by the pit, their mouths are signified. Therefore the key of the pit of the abyss was given to the heretics, because God allowed there to be heresies, as the apostle Paul says: “There must be heresies, so that those who are approved may be revealed among you” (1 Cor. 11:19).

Revelation 9:2a And he opened the pit of the abyss.

The star that fell from heaven opened the pit of the abyss, because the heretics, to bring forth the evils that lay hidden in their hearts, opened their mouths.

Revelation 9:2b And smoke went up from the pit, like the smoke of a great furnace.

By the smoke the teaching of the heretics is signified, because just as smoke, driven by the wind, quickly vanishes, so too the teaching of the heretics vanished like smoke through the teaching of the holy Fathers; but by the furnace the hearts of the wicked are often signified, as the prophet says: Their hearts are like a burning oven (Hosea 7:4).

Revelation 9:2c And the sun and the air were darkened by the smoke of the pit.

By the sun Christ is often understood, but in this place his members, that is, the holy preachers, are indicated; by the air, however, the faithful people who were subject to them. But how were the elect darkened, whom the wickedness of the heretics was not able to deceive? Rather, they were darkened for those whom the heretics were able to deceive, who are also indicated in what follows by the locusts; yet their brightness could not remain hidden from the other faithful, just as a lamp cannot be hidden under a bushel (Matt. 5:15).

Revelation 9:3a And from the smoke locusts came out upon the earth.

From the smoke, that is, from the teaching of those who are called heresiarchs, a great multitude of heretics has been generated. They came out upon the earth, because, separated from the fellowship of the elect, they have been joined to those who are called “earth,” that is, to unbelievers.

Revelation 9:3b And power was given to them, like the power the scorpions of the earth have.

God is often said to have given what He allows; for God allows the wicked to act badly, so that from their evils He may bring forth good. By the scorpions, however, we may understand demons; for just as scorpions, when they are not seen in advance, infect and destroy human bodies with their venom, so also demons inject the poison of evil persuasions into unsuspecting people, and while they draw them on to commit crimes, they kill their souls, infected with the venom of their malice. Therefore the power of the locusts, that is, of the heretics, is similar to the power of the scorpions, that is, of the demons; because just as demons are always intent on deceiving whomever they can, so also the heretics struggled with all their strength to plunge whomever they could into the pit of their own destruction.

Revelation 9:4a And they were commanded not to harm the grass of the earth, nor any green thing, nor any tree.

What “grass of the earth” signifies, this also is meant by “every green thing”; for just as above (Chapter 8), under the sound of the trumpet of the first angel, by all the green grass that is said to be burned, the elect who were in the earliest times are signified, so here too by the grass of the earth and every green thing the faithful people is signified; whereas by the trees the teachers of the Church are signified. Therefore the heretics were forbidden to harm the grass of the earth, nor any green thing, nor any tree; because God did not allow them to deceive those who possessed the greenness of faith, that is, who fulfilled by works the faith they professed, nor the holy preachers, who were not thorns or briars, from which neither grapes nor figs are gathered, but rather trees bearing the fruits of good works, by which the faithful peoples were refreshed; where it also follows:

Revelation 9:4b Except the men who do not have the seal of God on their foreheads.

But what is the seal of God, if not love, of which the same John speaks, saying: “God is love” (1 Jn. 4:8)? Indeed, by the foreheads, which very often show the qualities of minds, we can understand the hearts of the depraved. Therefore, those were easily deceived by the heretics who did not have the seal of the living God, that is, love, in their hearts.

Revelation 9:5a And it was commanded to them that they should not kill them.

When he says, “it was commanded to them,” it seems to me to refer to the demons; for such is the wickedness of demons that, if God had allowed it, as soon as a man had committed a mortal crime, they would immediately kill him. But God waits for wicked people, so that at some time they may return to him through repentance and be saved, or so that the righteous may be tested by them. For the Psalmist also, when he was speaking about the wicked, prayed, saying: “Do not kill them, lest my people forget” (Ps. 58:12). And in the book of Judges it is written that God left the Canaanites and the Philistines, so that in them Israel might be tested, and might gain knowledge of how to fight against enemies (Judg. 3:1–2). Therefore God commanded, that is, did not permit, that all heretics or all wicked people be wiped out, so that the righteous might have those by whom they would be tested, like gold which is tested by fire. And so it follows:

Revelation 9:5b But that they should be tormented for five months.

By the five months we can understand the whole span of the present life, which consists of five ages. For the first age is infancy, the second childhood, the third adolescence, the fourth youth, the fifth old age; although blessed Gregory, in order to make the ages of human beings correspond to the six ages of the world, divided old age into two parts12.
