Therefore in the five months, that is, in all the time of the present life, not only heretics but all the wicked are tormented. For, whether they wish it or not, when they call to mind death and the punishment that follows death, and although they seem to exult in worldly prosperity, nevertheless they are inwardly tormented, because they know that for their crimes they are to be tormented eternally. By contrast, the righteous, although outwardly afflicted in various ways, yet inwardly exult in the hope of heavenly glory, which they believe they will attain. We can also understand by the five months all the time of this world: for the first month is from the origin of the world up to the Flood; the second from the Flood up to Moses; the third from Moses up to David; the fourth from David up to Christ; the fifth from Christ up to the end of the age. In these five months, therefore, all the makers of errors are tormented, either in the way we have said above, or because they see their false inventions overcome by the truth.

Revelation 9:5c And their torment is like the torment of a scorpion when it strikes a man.

We said above that by “scorpions” demons are indicated; for just as the scorpion wounds the body with its sting, so that through the wound it may inject its venom by which it kills a man, so too the Devil wounds the mind by anger, or pride, or lust, or some carnal delight, so that through this he pours in the venom of his malice and kills the soul. Therefore the torment of the locusts is said to be like the torment of a scorpion, because heretics, imitating their father the Devil, with smooth and well‑crafted words wounded the minds of unlearned people, and by injecting in the venom of their error killed their souls. Indeed, by the torment of souls we can understand their destruction.

Revelation 9:6 And in those days men will seek death, and will not find it; and they will desire to die, and death will flee from them.

This statement refers to the elect. “In those days,” that is, in the days when the grace of the Gospel shines forth throughout the whole world, men will seek death. Paul sought death by persecuting the Church of God, and did not find it; because Christ, who is an impenetrable wall, placing himself before him, forcibly led him back from the death he was seeking to the life which is himself. There were also many others who sought death either by persecuting the Church of God or by practicing other evils; but death fled from them, because through the preaching of the saints they were recalled to life. We too have seen some who sought death and did not find it; and they desired to die, and death fled from them. For we know that there have been some who, loving this world more than they ought, toiled for a long time and in many ways to become rich, desiring to live in luxury and to fulfill the desires of their flesh; but when they could not attain what they wanted, rejecting the world’s false promises, they turned themselves to acquiring heavenly riches, and, as we believe, by persevering in good works they merited to attain their possession. We have likewise seen some who left monasteries and returned to the world, and gave themselves into the service of certain powerful men, so that by their support they might obtain earthly riches and possessions which they could not have on their own; but when they could not attain what they desired, they abandoned the world, returned to their monasteries, and ended their lives in good works. These, then, sought death and did not find it; and they desired to die, and death fled from them. Concerning such people the Lord speaks to the prophet under the figure of Jerusalem, saying: Behold, I will hedge your way with thorns, and I will fence it with a wall, and she will not find her paths. And she will follow her lovers and will not overtake them; she will seek them and will not find them (Hosea 2:6). Our lovers are carnal desires; we follow our lovers when we labor in this, that we may become possessors of riches, so that we may have the means to satisfy carnal desires. We follow our lovers and do not overtake them when, with the mercy of God preventing us, we are not able to reach that which we uselessly desire.

Revelation 9:7a And the likenesses of the locusts were like horses prepared for battle.

By horses the holy preachers are often signified, as the prophet Habakkuk speaks in his song, saying: You made a way in the sea for your horses, in the mud of many waters (Hab. 3:15). God made a way in the sea for his horses, in the mud of many waters, when at the beginning of the faith he granted peace to the world, so that his apostles could run throughout all the nations, which are signified by the sea and the mud, and scatter the seeds of faith. Therefore the horses of God were being prepared for battle when the holy preachers were armed for this purpose both with word and with miracles, so that they might rescue humanity from the power of the Devil. The locusts, however, are said to be like horses prepared for battle, because just as the holy preachers labored with all their strength to free, as far as they could, people from the snares of the Devil, so also the heretics were always armed for this purpose, that they might deceive as many as they could and plunge them into the abyss of their own damnation.

Revelation 9:7b And upon their heads were something like crowns, similar to gold.

Gold is often used to signify wisdom, as we have already said above; by the crowns of the locusts worldly wisdom is shown. The crowns of the locusts, then, were not themselves gold, but had the appearance of gold, because the worldly wisdom in which the heretics placed their greatest trust was not gold, that is, it was not true wisdom, but rather foolishness, as the apostle Paul says: The wisdom of this world is foolishness before God (1 Cor. 3:19).

Revelation 9:7c And their faces were like the faces of men.

Man is a rational animal; and by the faces of men we can understand the knowledge of the divine Scriptures, which the heretics strove to possess, so that by it they might more easily deceive simple people. Therefore the locusts seemed to have the faces of men, but it was likeness, not truth; for the heretics did indeed appear to have knowledge of the divine Scriptures, but they were far removed from the truth, because they did not understand them correctly.

Revelation 9:8a And they had hair like women’s hair.

By the hair that is cut off without feeling, we can understand the great multitude of fools, who were easily deceived by heretics; for just as hair is cut without harm, so also these were cut off from the Church without any loss. By the women we can understand various Churches. Therefore the locusts had hair like women’s hair; because just as the holy Churches of God had their flocks, which at set times gathered together to celebrate the divine offices, so also the heretics had mad crowds, who assembled in different places, not to celebrate divine mysteries, but to celebrate devilish ones.

Revelation 9:8b And their teeth were like lions’ teeth.

The function of teeth is that whatever they seize within, they grind down and consume, and cast it into the pit of the stomach. Therefore we can understand the persecutors of the Church by the lions and the teeth: by the lions, the rulers; by the teeth, those who under their command tore apart God’s saints. Therefore the teeth of the heretics were like lions’ teeth; because just as pagans persecuted the Church of God and, whoever they could, entangled in the abyss of destruction by terrors and tortures, so also the heretics, joining to themselves certain powerful men, persecuted the Church of God, and, like teeth, after tormenting many with threats and violent pressure, they plunged them into the belly of their own malice.

Revelation 9:9 And the sound of their wings was like the sound of many horses running into battle.

We can understand by the wings the words of the heretics; for just as wings lift birds up from the earth, so the heretics were exalted by their eloquence. By the horses, moreover, preachers are indicated, as we have already said above. Therefore the sound of the wings of the locusts was like the sound of many horses running into battle; because just as the holy preachers sent forth the voices of their preaching when they went to war against the Devil, so that they might free humanity from his power, so too the heretics ran about everywhere, spreading false teaching, so that they might deceive and enslave to the Devil all whom they could.

Revelation 9:10a And they had tails like scorpions, and stings were in their tails.

The prophet Isaiah says that a prophet teaching falsehood is a tail (Isa. 9:15). Therefore heretics, who because of their knowledge of the divine Scriptures think themselves to be prophets, are not inappropriately likened to scorpions’ tails, because their teaching was false and poisonous; therefore they are said to have had stings in their tails. For by the stings are signified the blasphemies that they inserted into the divine Scriptures. And just as a scorpion would be harmless if it lacked a sting, so too the teaching of heretics would be good if they did not defile it with blasphemies.

Revelation 9:10b Their power to harm men lasted for five months.

What the five months are has been said above.

Revelation 9:11 And they had over them as king the angel of the abyss, whose name in Hebrew is Abaddon, in Greek Apollyon, and in Latin has the name Destroyer.

It is certain that the prince of all heretics is the Devil, just as the Lord says to Job: “For he is indeed king over all the sons of pride” (Job 41:25); and therefore he is called the Destroyer, because he is the destroyer of all goodness.

Revelation 9:12-13a One woe has gone, and behold, two woes still come. And the sixth angel sounded the trumpet.

By the sixth angel the martyrs are signified: the sixth angel sounded the trumpet, because the martyrs of Christ offered the doctrine of faith to unbelievers.

Revelation 9:13b And I heard a single voice from the horns of the golden altar that is before the Lord, saying to the sixth angel.

By the golden altar, as we have already said above, the Church is signified. The four horns of the altar therefore show the four evangelists: he says that he heard one voice from the four horns of the golden altar, because there is one faith, one doctrine which the four books of the Gospels teach. But what this voice says to the sixth angel it makes clear by adding:

Revelation 9:14 Go and release the four angels who are bound at the great river Euphrates.

First let us say what the four angels are, what the bonds are, what Euphrates is; and afterwards we will investigate more carefully why the Gospel commands the sixth angel to release the four angels. For just as above (chapter 7), at the opening of the sixth seal, four angels were seen standing over the four corners of the earth, holding the four winds of the earth so that they would not blow upon the earth nor upon any tree; so also here, under the sounding of the trumpet of the sixth angel, four angels are reported as being bound at the great river Euphrates. Therefore these four angels have the same meaning as those; for they signify four kingdoms, namely of the Assyrians, Persians, Macedonians, and Romans. And because the power of the four kingdoms, as we said above, had passed over to the Romans, in this place the Romans are signified by the four angels; while by Euphrates the world is understood. What, then, the bonds of the four angels are, is made clear above; for it is written above that John saw an angel come down from heaven, having the seal of the living God, and he cried with a loud voice to the four angels to whom it was given to harm the earth and the sea, saying: “Do not harm the earth or the sea or the trees until we have sealed the servants of our God on their foreheads” (Rev. 7:2–3). We said that Christ was signified by the angel who had the seal of the living God; therefore the bonds of the four angels are the words of the Lord, not that the Lord commanded the persecutors to cease from persecution until the seeds of faith were scattered through all nations, but that what he accomplished by his power is said to have been commanded by his words. For the Romans, before the coming of Christ, could in no way be satisfied with killings and plunder and the shedding of human blood. But when Christ came in the flesh, he bestowed peace upon the world, and the Romans, who could not restrain their hands from the shedding of human blood, were bound by peace and prevented from the shedding of blood. Therefore the voice that came forth from the four horns of the golden altar commanded the sixth angel who had the trumpet to release the four angels, because the teaching of the Gospel commanded this to the preachers of the word of God: that they should not be frightened by any threats or terrors of wicked men, so as to cease from the duty of preaching; as the Lord says in the Gospel: “Do not fear those who kill the body, but cannot kill the soul” (Matt. 10:28). For the emperors of the Romans and the other princes of the earth, when they saw the Christian religion increasing day by day and the worship of idols being abolished, threatened death to all who would worship or preach Christ. Therefore the sixth angel, that is, the martyrs, as it were released the four angels, that is, their persecutors, when, on account of their unconquerable steadfastness in preaching the Gospel, they armed themselves to persecute them. And so it follows:

Revelation 9:15 And the four angels were released, who had been prepared for the hour, and day, and month, and year, to kill a third part of mankind.

The four angels were released because wicked men, having cast aside peace and loosed their hands, which peace had bound, persecuted the people of God. But when he says, “They had been prepared,” he shows the will of wicked men; for before the time of persecution came, they were prepared by intention to kill the people of God. Indeed, the third part of mankind, as was said above, signifies the elect. Furthermore, in that it is said that the four angels were prepared in the hour, and day, and month, and year, to kill a third part of mankind, we can refer this to all the wicked, who have been from the beginning of the world and will be until the end. By “hour,” in fact, is signified the time from the beginning of the world until the Flood; by “day,” the time from the Flood until the Law; by “month,” the time under the Law; and by “year,” the time under grace. In these four periods, therefore, the depraved always persecute the elect.

Revelation 9:16 And the number of the cavalry army was two myriads of myriads; I heard their number.

Those who are signified by the four angels are the very ones signified by the cavalry army, namely the persecutors of the Church. Moreover, the army of persecutors is said to be cavalry; because just as God has his horses, that is, holy preachers, who have carried him throughout the whole world; so the Devil has his horses, that is, all the wicked, upon whom he sits and drives them to commit various crimes. They are said to be two myriads of myriads; for by a finite number of persecutors an innumerable multitude is expressed.

Revelation 9:17a And thus I saw the horses in the vision: and those who were sitting upon them had breastplates of fire, and of hyacinth, and of sulfur.

In this place, by the horses the mad peoples are indicated; by the riders of the horses, the princes of the earth are indicated; and by the breastplates, which repel sword blows from themselves, the hardness of the hearts of the depraved is indicated, which does not allow the sword of the Spirit, which is the word of God, to approach their hearts. These breastplates are said to be fiery, hyacinth-colored, and sulfurous. By fire, clearly, is signified the cruelty of the persecutors’ mind; by hyacinth, which has the figure of heaven, is signified the honor of divinity which they rendered to their gods; by sulfur, which stinks, are signified the blasphemies which they uttered against Christ. Therefore the persecutors are said to have had fiery, hyacinth-colored, and sulfurous breastplates, because partly on account of the unbelief of their mind they hardened their hearts, so that they not only did not believe in Christ, but also persecuted his servants; partly on account of the defense of false gods, and partly on account of the blasphemies which they uttered against Christ, saying that he had been a magician and a deceiver.

Revelation 9:17b And the heads of the horses were like the heads of lions.

By the heads of the horses the emperors of the Romans are indicated, because they appeared to be the heads of all the persecutors, who also, on account of their unquenchable rage, are likened to lions.

Revelation 9:17c And from their mouth proceeds fire and smoke and sulfur.

By fire are indicated the wicked commands of the emperors, by which the people of God were ordered to be slain; by smoke, the honor of divinity which they commanded to be rendered to their gods. And rightly by smoke is indicated the falsity of idolatry, because just as smoke vanishes before the face of the wind, so the worship of idols has perished in the sight of the preaching of the Gospel; by sulfur, moreover, as was said above, are indicated the blasphemies which they hurled against Christ.

Revelation 9:18a By these three plagues a third part of men was killed;

That is, a multitude of martyrs:

Revelation 9:18b namely by the fire, smoke, and sulfur, which went out of their mouths.

For fire went out of their mouths, that is, the most wicked command by which they ordered that the holy martyrs of God be killed; and smoke went out, because they ordered that the honor of divinity be given by all men to their own gods; and sulfur went out, that is, blasphemies against Christ, commanding that the Christian religion be completely extinguished from the world.

Revelation 9:19a For the power of the horses is in their mouths and in their tails.

Above, the heads of the horses are said to be like those of lions: but in the mouths of lions there are teeth, with which they crush and tear to pieces everything they seize. We can therefore understand by the teeth the executioners, who, being in the mouth of the lions, that is, in the power of the persecutors, by their command tortured the bodies of the saints with various torments. Therefore the persecutors had the bodies of the saints in their mouth, that is, in their power, but they did not have their souls, as the Lord says in the Gospel: “Do not fear those who kill the body but cannot kill the soul” (Matt. 10:28). And therefore the power of the persecutors was only in their mouth, because they had the bodies of the saints in their power, but they did not have their souls: they could kill those whom they were not able to conquer. By the tails, however, the teaching of the philosophers and poets is signified.

Revelation 9:19b For their tails are like serpents, having heads, and in these they harm.

It is fitting that the teaching of the philosophers is likened to serpents, because it was full of the poisons of lies. These serpents are said to have heads, because all the errors of the pagans had their own authors. Therefore the power of the horses was in their tails, because the persecutors of the Church, through the teaching of the philosophers, wanted to overthrow the Christian religion.

Revelation 9:20-21 And the rest of the men who were not killed by those plagues did not do penance for the works of their hands, so as not to adore demons and idols of gold and silver and bronze and stone and wood, which can neither see nor hear nor walk; and they did not do penance for their murders, nor for their sorceries, nor for their fornication, nor for their thefts;

It is understood that they perished. Therefore, the third part, which was killed, was made safe, that is, the elect, among whom many were killed for the name of Christ; the rest would most gladly have died, if necessity had demanded it. But the others, that is, the pagans, who were unwilling to believe in Christ so that they might do penance for their iniquities, perished.

Chapter 10

Revelation 10:1a And I saw another mighty angel coming down from heaven, clothed with a cloud, and a rainbow on his head.

Since above he had related the hardships of the Church, which it has suffered from heretics and pagans, it remains that he tell of its prosperity, how it has grown amid persecutions. For it is certain that the Church has grown in persecutions, and the more it was oppressed, the more it was strengthened and increased: as we read written about the children of Israel, in the figure of this same Church, that the more they were oppressed by the Egyptians, the more they multiplied. The mighty angel signifies our Lord Jesus Christ, of whom the Psalmist says: “The Lord strong and mighty, the Lord mighty in battle” (Ps. 23:8). He is said to be coming down from heaven clothed with a cloud, because our Lord, coming into the world, put on flesh, which is signified by the cloud, so that by assuming flesh he might make himself like the humans whom he came to redeem. By his rainbow, moreover, his mercy is signified, as we said above; and by his head in this passage his divinity is understood. He had the rainbow on his head, because his divinity, before his incarnation, had already taken on such great mercy that he did not disdain to become man for the sake of men, and finally to be condemned to the most grievous death.

Revelation 10:1b And his face was like the sun.

The Lord is often signified by the sun, because the sun surpasses all visible creatures in its brightness and beauty; but what creature can be compared to its Creator? Yet through visible figures invisible realities are often shown. Therefore in the heart of the elect Christ is the sun, whom they never cease to behold by hope, and they believe that they will come to contemplate his beauty.

Revelation 10:1c And his feet like pillars of fire.

By the pillar of fire we can understand the Church, either because, inflamed by the Holy Spirit, it shines, or because it burns unceasingly with love of God and neighbor. By the two feet we must understand the two peoples. From the two feet, then, one pillar is formed, because from the two peoples, namely from Jews and Gentiles, one Church of God is built. We can also understand by the two feet the two Testaments, as we will show more fully in what follows. Nor does this disagree with the earlier meaning, since the Old Testament was delivered especially to the Jews, and the New to the Gentiles.

Revelation 10:2a And he had in his hand a little book opened.

This Book above is described as having been sealed with seven seals; but about its opening, by the Lord’s generosity, we have said some things. Therefore, our Lord Jesus Christ was seen holding the open little book in his hand, because he himself opened the meaning of the divine Scriptures for the teachers of the Church.

Revelation 10:2b And he set his right foot upon the sea, but his left upon the land.

By the right foot, as we have said, the New Testament is signified; by the left, the Old is indicated. We can, however, understand by the sea the people of the nations, on account of the depth of their ignorance and error; but by the land which is cultivated so that it may bear fruit, the Jews, who were cultivated through the discipline of the Law and bore fruit when they fulfilled the legal instructions, though in a carnal manner. Therefore the Lord set his right foot upon the sea when he gave, more specifically to the Gentiles than to the Jews, the teaching of the Gospel, just as Paul speaks to these same Jews, saying: “It was necessary that the word of God should be spoken to you first; but since you reject it and judge yourselves unworthy of eternal life, behold, we turn to the Gentiles” (Acts 13:46). He set his left foot upon the land when he gave the Law specifically to the Jews. But let no one be disturbed that he has mentioned the later things first and the earlier things afterwards, because in the divine Scriptures this often occurs. To prove this, let us offer only one example. In the one hundred and fifth Psalm (verse 17), when he had said: “The earth opened and swallowed Dathan, and covered over the congregation of Abiron; and fire blazed in their assembly, a flame burned the sinners”; he then goes back and says: “And they made a calf in Horeb, and worshipped a carved image.” Nor is it inappropriate that by the right foot the New Testament, and by the left the Old is signified, because the Old Testament was a shadow of the New Testament.

Revelation 10:3a And he cried out, as when a lion roars.

Christ cried out through the Law, but not as when a lion roars; because in the one house of Israel his voice was heard only there. Afterwards he cried out through the Gospel, as when a lion roars; because his voice thundered forth into the whole world.

Revelation 10:3b And when he had cried out, the seven thunders spoke their voices.

By the seven thunders we can understand seven virtues. Christ cried out through the Law, but the seven thunders did not sound; he cried out through the Gospel, and the seven thunders spoke their voices. For those to whom the Law said, “Cursed is he who does not leave his offspring in Israel”; and for whom on this account it was permitted to have many wives and concubines, the thunder of chastity had not yet sounded. Likewise, for those to whom the Law said, “An eye for an eye, a tooth for a tooth; and as someone has inflicted a blemish, so he must be forced to endure one” (Exod. 21:24); they had not yet heard the thunder of patience. And they had not yet perfectly heard the thunder of love, those to whom the Law said, “You shall love your friend and you shall hate your enemy” (Matt. 5:43). The same is to be understood of the other virtues. Therefore the seven thunders spoke, when through the teaching of the Gospel the loftiness of the virtues began to be shown.

Revelation 10:4 And I heard a voice from heaven saying to me: “Seal up what the seven thunders have spoken, and do not write them.”

The voice from heaven is the voice from the Church; but to write is to preach, because just as a writer writes with ink in a scroll so that those who read may be trained, so too every teacher writes the words of God when, by preaching them, he plants them in the hearts of the listeners. Why then, by the voice that sounded from heaven, was it forbidden that the seven thunders be written, we can find in the Acts of the Apostles and in the letters of Paul. For at the beginning of the faith, the holy preachers, seeing the weakness of the nations who were coming to faith, did not dare to entrust to them the more severe commands of Christ, lest perhaps, frightened by the severity of the teachings, they would not venture to approach the acceptance of the faith of Christ; just as the Apostle Paul wrote to his disciples, saying: “As to little children in Christ I gave you milk to drink, not solid food; for you were not yet able, nor indeed are you even now able; for you are still carnal” (1 Cor. 3:2). And the apostles who were in Jerusalem commanded those who had believed from among the Gentiles, saying: “It seemed good to the Holy Spirit and to us to lay upon you no greater burden, except that you should abstain from food sacrificed to idols, and from fornication, and from what is strangled, and from blood” (Acts 15:28). Therefore the voice from heaven speaks to John, that he should not write the seven thunders, because the apostles, who are signified by heaven, ordered the first preachers not to force those who were newly coming to the faith to climb at once to the heights of virtues, until, strengthened in faith, they could then pass over to the practice of virtues.

Revelation 10:5-7 And the angel whom I saw standing upon the sea and upon the land, when he had lifted up his hand and had sworn by the One who lives forever, who created heaven and the things that are in it, and the earth and the things that are in it, the sea and the things that are in it, that time shall be no more; but in the days of the seventh angel, when he begins to sound the trumpet, the mystery of God will be completed, which he proclaimed through his servants the prophets.

When we swear, we want what we say to be firm. For the Lord, therefore, to have spoken is to have “sworn,” because it cannot come to pass that what God speaks should become void, just as he himself says in the Gospel: “Heaven and earth will pass away, but my words will not pass away” (Luke 21:33). Since Christ therefore swore by the One who lives forever and by him who made all creatures, he swore by himself, because all things were made through him, and without him nothing was made (Jn. 1:3). He swore, moreover, not by binding himself with certain words, but by speaking the things that were to come. By the seventh angel, in fact, the preachers who are to be at the end of the world are signified, about whom we will speak in what follows. Christ therefore swore that in the days of the seventh angel the mystery of God will be completed, which he proclaimed through his prophets, because he foretold that the end will come then, when the full number of the elect has been fulfilled.

Revelation 10:8 And the voice that I had heard from heaven spoke with me again and said: Go and take the book from the hand of the angel who is standing upon the sea and upon the land.

He calls it the first voice, when it was said to him from heaven: Seal up the things which the seven thunders have spoken, and do not write them. (Rev. 10:4) Therefore he says that he heard the same voice again, because that voice was of the same Holy Spirit as this one. In this passage we must understand all the apostles through John. Therefore the divine voice spoke to John, that he should go to the angel and receive the book from him; because the grace of the Holy Spirit inspired the apostles to leave all things and follow Christ, so that they might receive from him the teaching of the Gospel.

Revelation 10:9a And I went to the angel and told him to give me the book.

The apostles went to Christ so that they might be instructed by him in the teaching of the divine Scriptures.

Revelation 10:9b-10 And he said to me: Take it and devour it, and it will make your stomach bitter, but in your mouth it will be sweet like honey. And when I had devoured it, my stomach was made bitter.

By the mouth, in which tastes are distinguished, we can understand the hearts of the apostles; by the stomach, in which all the filth of the body stays, we must understand the memory of sins. Therefore, while the book was being devoured, it is said to have been sweet like honey; because when the divine Scripture is turned over in the mind and promises eternal life to those who keep the commandments of God, it becomes in the heart sweet as honey. But when it leads the mind from higher things to lower things, that is, from contemplation of the heavenly fatherland to the consideration of one’s own sins, and shows the punishments which the wicked are going to suffer for their sins, then that which previously had been sweet in the revelation of heavenly glory becomes bitter in the disclosure of sins. We can also understand this specifically about John, who, before he wrote the Gospel, was led into exile on Patmos; but because, during his absence, heretics had invaded the Church of God, after his return from exile he was compelled to write the Gospel to destroy their blasphemies. Therefore the divine voice commanded John to go to the angel and receive the book from him; because the Holy Spirit, by inspiring this in John, urged him to go to Christ, not with the steps of the body but with the steps of the mind, to consider the power of his divinity and his equality with the Father, so that he might be able to compose a little book about his divinity, saving for all the faithful. John truly devoured the book when he fully received the knowledge of the Gospel which he later wrote. So, while the book was being devoured, it offered the sweetness of honey to the mouth; because the more divine Scripture is chewed over in the heart, the more health-giving sweetness it offers to the mind. But by the stomach, in which food decays, we can understand this mortal flesh. Therefore the book which was sweet in the mouth produced bitterness in the stomach; because the more holy men devote themselves to meditation on the divine Scriptures, and the sweeter their meditation becomes in the mind, the greater bitterness they inflict on their own flesh, namely by afflicting it with fastings, vigils, abstinence, and the other torments by which the flesh is worn down.

Revelation 10:11 And he said to me: You must prophesy again to peoples, and nations, and tongues, and many kings.

It is foretold to him that he will return from exile to preach. But since the holy Fathers hand down that after his return from exile he preached only in Ephesus and the places neighboring to it, and that there he died and his body rests, how is it said that he prophesied to peoples, and nations, and tongues, and many kings? Yet he prophesied, that is, he preached through his Gospel, which he later wrote for all nations, and tongues, and many kings; because it has been spread among all nations, and through meditation on it all the peoples of the earth and many kings advance in spiritual growth.

Chapter 11

Revelation 11:1 And a measuring reed like a rod was given to me, saying: Rise, and measure the temple of God, and the altar, and those who worship in it.

By the reed with which the ancients used to write, the Gospel that was given to him by the Lord, and which he later wrote, is indicated; but by the rod discipline is often understood. Therefore the reed that was given to John had the likeness of a rod, because the discipline of Christians is the Gospel. Therefore he is also commanded to measure the temple and the altar and those who worship in it; for by the temple is meant the Church; by the altar, the holy and perfect men who stand within the Church; and by those who worship in it, the rest of the multitude who are instructed by their teaching is signified. For each preacher in fact measures the temple of God and the altar and those who worship in it, when he bestows the teaching of spiritual grace to each person according to that person’s own capacity; for the wise must be taught in one way, the foolish in another; the learned in one way, the unlearned in another; the pagan in one way, the Christian in another; the heretic in one way, the Catholic in another. For the Lord also says in the Gospel that a householder, going abroad, called his servants and entrusted his goods to them. And to one he gave five talents, to another two, and to another one; to each according to his own ability (Matt. 25:14). It follows:

Revelation 11:2a But as for the court which is outside, cast it outside, and do not measure it; for it has been given to the Gentiles.

If it was outside, how could it be cast outside? Therefore by the court the Jews are signified: therefore the Jews were outside through unbelief; but John and the other apostles still held them in their hearts through love, as the Apostle Paul says: I myself wished to be accursed from Christ for my brothers, who are my kinsmen according to the flesh, who are Israelites (Rom. 9:3). Therefore it is commanded him not to measure it, that is, not to minister the words of preaching to them, just as in the Gospel he himself commands, saying: Do not throw your pearls before swine, lest they trample them under their feet, and turn and tear you to pieces (Matt. 7:6). And to the prophet Ezekiel the Lord speaks, saying: Behold, I will make your tongue cling to the roof of your mouth, and you shall not be as a man who rebukes; because it is a provoking house (Ezek. 3:26). And this court is said to have been given to the Gentiles; because the Jews, on account of their unbelief, were joined to the pagan nations: or they were handed over to the nations, that is, to the Romans, so that they might be destroyed and exterminated by them.

Revelation 11:2b And they will trample the holy city for forty-two months.

Suddenly he makes a leap to Elijah and Enoch, who are to come in the times of Antichrist: and in a quite beautiful order, namely, that he who had set forth the rejection of the Jews through the figure of the court, should quietly add their restoration; for it is certain that at the end of the world the Jews will return to faith in Christ through the preaching of Elijah and Enoch. Therefore the holy city, that is, the Church, Antichrist and his ministers will trample, that is, will persecute, for forty-two months, which make three and a half years.

Revelation 11:3 And I will grant to my two witnesses, and they will prophesy for one thousand two hundred and sixty days, clothed in sackcloth.

The Lord calls Elijah and Enoch his two witnesses, who will go before his second coming, just as John went before the first.

Revelation 11:4 These are the two olive trees and the two lampstands standing in the presence of the Lord of the earth.

The olive tree bears oil, which is afterward placed upon the lampstand, so that it may give light to all who are in the house. (Matt. 5:15) In this passage we can understand spiritual wisdom by the oil; for each person is then an olive tree, when, resting within himself, he strives by the grace of contemplation to serve God; when he strives to fulfill that which the Lord says in the Gospel: “Let not your left hand know what your right hand is doing, so that your alms may be in secret; and your Father, who sees in secret, will repay you” (Matt. 6:3). He becomes a lampstand when he fulfills that which the Lord says in the Gospel: “Let your good works be seen, and glorify your Father who is in heaven” (Matt. 5:16). Therefore these two holy men are now olive trees, because, placed in some spot on earth where the divine majesty has ordered them to dwell, they serve God through the grace of contemplation; they will then become lampstands when they come to fulfill the ministry of their preaching, when they will proclaim the word of God to all, namely to the faithful and to the depraved. Of this lampstand the Lord says in the Gospel: “Neither do they light a lamp and put it under a bushel, but on a lampstand, so that it may give light to all who are in the house” (Matt. 5:15). For a lamp is set upon a lampstand when the ministry of preaching is entrusted to a holy man, who illuminates the good he preaches with the flames of good works; but a lamp is put under a bushel when the ministry of preaching is entrusted to someone depraved, who obscures the good he preaches by wicked deeds, so that it does not give light to those who are in the house, as Saint Gregory (Homily 12 on Gospels) says, because when a man’s life is despised, it follows that his preaching is also scorned. Therefore the two olive trees and the two lampstands are said to stand in the presence of the Lord; because, placed on earth, they await the time when they will fulfill the ministry of their preaching and pay the debt of death, as the following passages show plainly.

Revelation 11:5 And if anyone should wish to harm them, fire will go out from their mouth and will devour their enemies; and if anyone should wish to hurt them, in this way he must be killed.

For the Holy Spirit speaks through the mouths of the saints, as the Lord says in the Gospel: “For it is not you who speak, but the Spirit of your Father who speaks in you” (Matt. 10:20). It is therefore not unfitting that the words of the saints are called fire, as the Psalmist says of the Lord: “Your word is intensely fiery, and your servant has loved it” (Ps. 118:140). Therefore fire will go out from the mouth of these saints and devour their enemies, because their teaching will so destroy the errors of Antichrist that, for all who possess a sound mind, nothing will seem to exist of what Antichrist and his ministers will try to assert. Moreover, what follows, “And if anyone should wish to hurt them, in this way he must be killed,” refers to the same matter.

Revelation 11:6 They have the power to shut the heaven, so that it may not rain during the days of their prophecy; and they have power over the waters, to turn them into blood, and to strike the earth with every plague as often as they wish.

We read that Elijah by prayer shut the heaven and again opened it; and that Moses also turned the waters into blood. Therefore it must not be thought that Elijah will have less power when he comes to fulfill the duty of his preaching than he had at the time when he lived among men, but rather greater. We can also understand spiritually by “heaven” the Church, and by “rain” the words of the saints; for heaven is shut to the wicked when God in his judgment allows their hearts to be so hardened that in no way do they grant the word of God access to their heart so that they might be saved. For the prophet Isaiah also shut heaven to the Jews when it was said to him by the Lord: “Blind the heart of this people, and make their ears heavy, and shut their eyes; lest perhaps they see with their eyes, and hear with their ears, and understand with their heart, and be converted, and I heal them” (Isa. 6:10). Jeremiah also shut heaven to his enemies when he said to the Lord: “Do not be gracious to their iniquity, and let not their sin be wiped out from your presence. Let them fall down before you; in the time of your visitation they shall perish” (Jer. 18:23). Did not Moses have the power of shutting and opening heaven, to whom the Lord said, when the people had sinned: “Let me alone, that my wrath may be kindled against them and that I may destroy them, and I will make you into a great nation” (Exod. 32:10)? Therefore those holy men will shut heaven for some, so that the rain of the mercy of God may not descend upon them, namely for those whom they have not been able to recall from the errors of Antichrist; but for others they will open it, namely for those whom they will have recalled from the errors of Antichrist; and they will pour rain upon them when by their prayers they make God favorable to them. By the waters which they are said to turn into blood, peoples are signified; but the blood shows divine vengeance. Therefore they will turn the waters into blood when, by their own judgment, they decree that the nations which have departed from the faith of Christ are to be condemned to everlasting death.

Revelation 11:7 And when they have finished their testimony, the beast that rises up from the abyss will wage war against them, and will conquer them, and will kill them.

This beast signifies the Antichrist. It will wage war against them when it persecutes them; and it will conquer them not by overcoming them with words, but by putting them to death. And so it follows:

Revelation 11:8 And he will kill them, and their bodies will lie in the street of the great city, which is called spiritually Sodom and Egypt, where also their Lord was crucified.

If we should wish to understand by the great city the earthly Jerusalem because of what it says: “Where also their Lord was crucified,” we will stray from the truth; since that Jerusalem has been destroyed down to its foundations, and the one that has been built in its place is said not to be in that same location, but to be situated in another; nor should it be called Sodom and Egypt, since it is inhabited by Christians. At the same time we must consider that wherever in this book the great city is mentioned, it signifies Babylon, which is the city of the Devil and is made up of all the wicked. As for the fact that their bodies are said to be cast in the street of the great city, we can understand it simply, so that they lie in whatever street belongs to the jurisdiction of some wicked man. But it seems better to me that, just as we said that by the great city all the wicked are to be understood, so also by the street we should understand the hearts of the wicked: therefore the bodies of the saints will lie in the street of the great city, because the saints lie before those who consider them to be of no importance; on the contrary, they stand firm for the faithful, who with the eyes of the heart perceive how great their virtue and glory are. The city of the Devil is quite fittingly called Sodom and Egypt, because Sodom is interpreted as blindness, and Egypt as darkness. For who suffers a worse blindness and darkness than the wicked, who with headlong step never cease hurrying on in doing evil, so that they may be plunged into the sewer of hell [inferni]? In the street of the great city, where the Lord was crucified, the bodies of the saints are described as destined to be cast down; because just as Christ was crucified by the members of the Devil, so by these same ones those two prophets will be killed.

Revelation 11:9-12 And people and tribes and tongues and nations will see their bodies for three and a half days, and they will not allow their bodies to be placed in tombs; and the inhabitants of the earth will rejoice over them and be glad, and will send gifts to one another, because those two prophets tormented those who dwelt on the earth. And after three and a half days, the spirit of life from God entered into them, and they stood upon their feet, and great fear fell upon all who saw them. And they heard a great voice from heaven saying to them: Come up here. And they went up into heaven in a cloud, and their enemies saw them.

If this is fulfilled according to history, it is indeed unknown to men, but all things are known to God. Yet we also can add a spiritual understanding to this history; but it seems rash to wish to adorn with spiritual meanings that history which we understand. For when we are outside a house, we are in no way able to decorate its interior; but so that we may not appear to have kept entirely silent, let us say a few things. We can understand the three and a half days as three and a half years, during which those two prophets are to preach; and by the tombs we can understand the minds of the wicked. Concerning these tombs the Lord speaks, saying: Woe to you, scribes and Pharisees, because you are like whitewashed tombs, which indeed appear outwardly beautiful, but within are full of all uncleanness and the bones of the dead (Matt. 23:27). So you also outwardly appear righteous to men, but within you are full of plunder and iniquity. Therefore in the tombs the depraved will not allow the bodies of the saints to be placed, because they will cast the teaching of those saints out of their minds. But that it is said that after three and a half days the spirit of life from God will enter into them and they will stand upon their feet, we can take as the time of the resurrection, which, after three and a half periods, although not immediately, will nevertheless take place shortly afterward. For they will stand upon their feet when, after the time of the resurrection, their power and magnificence will be revealed to all the wicked. And great fear will fall upon their enemies when they see them; because on the day of judgment, when they see them and their glory, they will be disturbed with terrible fear, saying: These are the ones whom we once held up to ridicule and as a likeness for reproach. We, foolish as we were, considered their life to be madness and their end without honor. How have they been counted among the sons of God, and how is their lot among the holy ones? (Wisdom 5:3). And because the prophet Malachi speaks in the person of the Lord, saying: Behold, I will send you the prophet Elijah, who will turn the hearts of the fathers to the children (Mal. 4:5), and the other things that follow: we believe that the Jews, through the preaching of Elijah, as we have shown above as best as we could, are to be converted to faith in Christ. Yet we can also understand by the two witnesses the preachers of the two peoples, namely of the Jews and of the Gentiles. They are said to be about to prophesy for one thousand two hundred and sixty days, (Rev. 11:3) because during all the time in which the persecution and deception of Antichrist will rage throughout the whole world, they will strive to bring back to the way of truth as many as they can.

Revelation 11:13a And in that hour a great earthquake occurred.

Now it returns to the beginning of the preaching of Elijah and Enoch. An earthquake, as we have already said above, usually signifies the movement of hearts: an earthquake will then happen when, through the preaching of Elijah and Enoch, many will be converted to repentance and many to faith.

Revelation 11:13b And a tenth part of the city fell.

It does not seem sensible to me to understand by the city, of which a tenth part fell, the Church; because this passage refers more to the city of the Devil than to the city of God. Therefore, in this place he says that a tenth part of that city collapsed, which he previously called the great city, and Sodom and Egypt, and in whose street the bodies of the saints are said to lie unburied for three and a half days. And because the number ten often signifies the Law, since the Law was received through the Decalogue, we can understand by the tenth part that fell the Jews, observers of the Law, who, by the preaching of Elijah, will accept faith in Christ and will fall away and be cut off from the city of the Devil.

Revelation 11:13c And seven thousand people were killed by the earthquake, and the rest were struck with fear and gave glory to the God of heaven.

Just as the Law is signified by the number ten, so the grace of the Gospel is often represented by the number seven. Behold, the tenth part of the city, which we have taught does not consist of stones but of people, has fallen; and not ten thousand, but seven thousand are said to have died. Therefore, the number ten has been changed into seven; because the Jews, rejecting legal observances, will receive the teaching of the Gospel, which, on account of the seven gifts of the Holy Spirit, is signified by the number seven. Therefore seven thousand are described as dead, because they will die to the Devil so that they may live to God; but the rest who are struck with fear represent the other elect, who, seeing the conversion of the Jews and the divine Scripture fulfilled in them, will grow in the fear and love of God and will not cease to praise God for their conversion.

Revelation 11:14 The second woe has passed, and behold, the third woe will come quickly.

The third woe refers to those evils that the Devil will bring about through Antichrist, and it concerns the Day of Judgment. And because, above, he says that the angel whom he saw standing upon the sea and upon the land swore by Him who lives forever; that in the days of the seventh angel, when he begins to sound the trumpet, the mystery of God will be fulfilled, which He proclaimed through His servants the prophets, he wished to set down the Day of Judgment briefly, and then returns to narrate the evils of Antichrist afterward. But if we examine this carefully, we will see that he has done this in an orderly way; for we know that the holy preachers who are to be born at the end of the world will preach this: that the Day of the Lord is near; so that those whom they cannot lead back to the way of truth by encouragements, they may at least stir to repentance by terrors. And because, before the persecutions of Antichrist, the Day of Judgment will begin to be preached, John wished to place that same Day of Judgment beforehand, before he came in his narrative to the persecutions of Antichrist himself.

Revelation 11:15a And the seventh angel sounded the trumpet.

By the seventh angel, as we have said, the holy preachers who are to be born at the end of the world are signified. They will sound the trumpet when they announce the word of God.

Revelation 11:15b And great voices were made in heaven, saying.

We can understand by heaven the Church, and by the great voices the words of the saints, who will rejoice when the Lord comes to the judgment, as the Lord says in the Gospel: Look up and lift up your heads, because your redemption is drawing near (Luke 21:28).

Revelation 11:15c The kingdom of this world has become that of the Lord and of his Christ, and he shall reign for ever and ever.

Was the kingdom of this world not previously that of the Lord and of his Christ? But he called “the kingdom of this world” the human beings in whose minds either God reigns or the Devil reigns. God reigns in his saints; the Devil reigns in those who are on his side, for he is the king over all the children of pride (Job 41:25). Therefore the kingdom of this world will become the Lord’s and his Christ’s when the Devil, with all those whom he has deceived, has been sunk into hell [infernum], when that word will be fulfilled which the Angel speaks to Daniel, saying: “Then the kingdom and the power that is under the whole heaven will be handed over to the people of the saints, and they shall possess it forever, and for ever and ever” (Dan. 7:27).

Revelation 11:16 And the twenty-four elders who sit in the presence of God on their thrones fell on their faces and worshipped God, saying.

By the twenty-four elders in this place we can understand the souls of the saints, who dwell with the Lord in heavenly blessedness; the thrones, moreover, signify eternal rest. The twenty-four elders sit in the presence of God on their thrones, because the souls of the saints, without bodies, now rest with the Lord in heavenly blessedness. They fell on their faces and worshipped, because the saints of God, when they see the most just judgment of God carried out and his indescribable power, will cast themselves down in submissive humility before their Creator, knowing that whatever exists is nothing in comparison with him who is God.

Revelation 11:17-18 We give thanks to you, Lord God Almighty, who are, and who were holy, and who have taken your great power and have reigned. And the nations were enraged, and your wrath has come, and the time for the dead to be judged; and to give the reward to your servants the prophets, and to the saints, and to those who fear your name, small and great; and to destroy those who corrupted the earth.

Christ now, after he assumed man, is of the same holiness as the Father, of the same eternity, of the same power; and he was, even before he assumed man, in all things equal to the Father. He took his great power when, through his passion, he conquered the Devil; he reigned indeed when he subjected humanity to himself. The nations were enraged because wicked men persecuted the Church of God, so that they might extinguish the name of Christ from the world. But the wrath of God will come when the day of judgment arrives; the time will come for the dead to be judged, that is to say, for the dead to be judged. Those dead, namely, who, placed in this life, did not fear to destroy their souls by living wickedly. And to give the reward to your servants the prophets and the saints, and to those who fear your name, small and great. This will then be fulfilled when, on the day of judgment, he will say: Come, blessed of my Father, receive the kingdom that has been prepared for you from the foundation of the world (Matt. 25:34). He calls those “small” who are of lesser merit; “great,” those who obtain greater favor with God. And to destroy those who corrupted the earth. He calls the wicked the corrupters of the earth; this will then be fulfilled when he will say: Depart, accursed, into the eternal fire which has been prepared for the devil and his angels (Matt. 25:41). But we must ask which earth the wicked have corrupted. Surely they have corrupted themselves by living wickedly, when in luxuries and in other vices they have pampered their bodies. They have also corrupted the earth of others, namely, of those whom they have drawn, by words and by example, to commit evil deeds. Now he returns to those things which he had passed over, that is, to recount the evils of Antichrist. And so that he may arrive more fittingly at expressing them, he begins from the coming of Christ, and, by recalling the evils which the Church has suffered from the Devil, he adds how that same Devil will try to overthrow this same Church through Antichrist.

Revelation 11:19a And the temple of God was opened in heaven, and the Ark of His Testament was seen in His temple.

We can understand by the temple of God the blessed Mary; and by the ark of the Testament, Christ, who took flesh from her. But the temple of God is not said to be opened because the womb of the blessed Virgin Mary was opened in giving birth to the Lord, she was a virgin before birth, a virgin in birth, a virgin after birth, but it is said to be opened because through her our Lord Jesus Christ was made visible to us. We can also understand by the temple the Old Testament, and by the ark the mysteries concerning Christ that are contained in the Old Testament. For when Christ came in the flesh, the Old Testament was opened, and the spiritual understanding that lay hidden in it was revealed to the faithful:

Revelation 11:19b And there were lightnings, and voices, and an earthquake, and great hail.

By the lightnings we understand the miracles that were done through Christ; by the voices, His preaching is signified. The earthquake signifies the movement of hearts; for many were moved by the preaching of Christ to carry out repentance for their sins: they were moved from unbelief to faith; they were moved from evil to good. And because hail is accustomed to strike the earth, we can understand by the hail the threats of eternal fire, which are often found in the Gospels, by which the hearts of the wicked are struck, so that they may turn back from their iniquities.

Chapter 12

Revelation 12:1 And a great sign appeared in heaven: a woman clothed with the sun, and the moon under her feet, and on her head a crown of twelve stars.

This woman signifies the Church; indeed, it was fitting that, after the preaching of Christ, her form should be described, the form that the preaching of Christ brought forth. In this passage we can understand “heaven” as this world; the “great sign” as the great salvation that the coming and preaching of Christ brought to the world; and by the sun, Christ is indicated, as the prophet says: “But for you who fear the name of the Lord, the sun of righteousness shall rise, with healing in its wings” (Mal. 4:2). The woman, then, was clothed with the sun, because the faithful, of whom the Church is composed, put on Christ in baptism, as the Apostle says: “All of you who have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ” (Gal. 3:27). By the moon, which she was seen to have under her feet, and which grows and decreases, we can understand this world, which the Church tramples underfoot by despising it, so that she may strive more freely toward heavenly things. But since the moon gives light to the night, it seems better to me that by the moon we understand Holy Scripture, without whose light we are unable to walk along the paths of righteousness in the night of this age. Of this light the Psalmist says: “Your word is a lamp to my feet, and a light to my paths” (Ps. 118:105). The woman, therefore, was seen to have the moon under her feet, because the Church, fixing the steps of her mind in the teachings of the divine Scriptures, knows how daily to strive toward heavenly things. She was also seen to have on her head a crown of twelve stars. The head of the Church is Christ, and the twelve stars are the twelve apostles. Nevertheless, by the head we can also understand the minds of the faithful. The woman, then, was seen to have on her head a crown of twelve stars, because the minds of the faithful are adorned by the teaching and deeds of the apostles, when they strive to imitate them.

Revelation 12:2 And she was with child in her womb, and cried out in pains, and was tormented, that she might give birth.

Just as a child in the womb is formed and receives human shape and breath, so likewise the Church bears in her womb all unbelievers; while she nourishes them by preaching for so long a time, until they receive human form, that is, until they believe in Christ, and receive the spiritual soul that is given in baptism, when they become sons of God. Therefore she is said to cry out in pains and to suffer torment in order to give birth; for the Church cries out by preaching, and she also cries out by begging God for the salvation of her hearers. She is indeed tormented so that she may give birth, when she grieves for those whom she sees lying in sins, just as Paul the apostle speaks to his weak disciples, saying: My little children, for whom I am again in labor until Christ is formed in you (Gal. 4:19). And in the Acts of the Apostles he speaks to the Ephesians, saying: For three years I did not cease daily, with tears, to warn each one of you (Acts 20:31). And the Psalmist speaks of preachers, saying: They went forth and wept, casting their seed (Ps. 125:6).

Revelation 12:3-4a And another sign was seen in heaven: and behold, a great red dragon, having seven heads and ten horns, and on his heads seven crowns; and his tail was dragging a third part of the stars of heaven, and he cast them to the earth.

This dragon signifies the Devil; now such is the red color, as if you were to mix a blood-red tone with a saffron color. Indeed, the pale color, that is, the saffron color, agrees with the blood-red; because when blood is poured out, it brings about death, and paleness covers the whole body of the dying person. Rightly, therefore, the Devil is said to have the color of death; because through his envy death entered into the world. (Wisdom 2:24) By his seven heads are signified the wicked persons whom the Devil has used to deceive humanity. The crowns signify his royal power; for he is king, as Scripture says (Job 41:25), over all the children of pride. For just as by the seven horns of the Lamb all the elect are signified, whom we divided into seven groups, so also by the seven heads of the dragon are signified the wicked who opposed them on the way of God. For the first head of the dragon were the wicked who were before the Flood, whom Scripture calls the sons of men, because they were a snare to those who were called the sons of God. The second head of the Devil were those who lived after the Flood up to the time of the Law, namely the inventors of idols and of various errors, by whom humanity was deceived. The third head of the Devil were those who, after the Law was given to the people of God, urged the worship of idols. The fourth head of the Devil were the false prophets and wicked kings, by whom the people of God were so greatly deceived that, on account of their iniquities, they were led into captivity. The fifth head were the wicked Jews, who both killed the Lord and persecuted his apostles. The sixth head were the persecutors of the Church. But the seventh head of the Devil will be the Antichrist. What the ten horns signify, we shall say later, in that passage where the harlot woman is seen sitting upon a scarlet beast (Rev. 17:3). By the tail, which is the end of the body, the Antichrist is signified. Therefore, the tail of the dragon was seen to drag a third part of the stars of heaven; because the Antichrist will deceive many of those who were thought by men to be elect, and who in the Church shine, like stars in heaven, with knowledge and understanding, and he will plunge them into the pit of destruction.

Revelation 12:4b And the dragon stood before the woman who was about to give birth, so that when she had brought forth, he might devour her child.

In this place we can also understand the blessed Mary by the woman, because she is the mother of the Church, since she gave birth to him who is the head of the Church; and she is the daughter of the Church, because she is the greatest member of the Church. Therefore the dragon stood before the woman, so that when she had given birth, he might devour her child; because at the very beginning of Christ’s birth he wished to kill him through Herod, his servant (Matt. 2:13). He also stands before the woman, that is, the Church, so that those whom she brings forth for God through baptism he may destroy by evil persuasion.

Revelation 12:5a And she gave birth to a male child.

Insofar as this involves our Lord Jesus Christ, why was it necessary to say that the woman gave birth to a male child, since we all know that our Lord Jesus Christ is male? But insofar as it involves the Church, why does it say that she gave birth to a male, since she bears sons and daughters to God every day from both genders? For the female gender is indeed weak: therefore a woman who, having taken on strength of soul, conquers the Devil and strives to please God through good works is not incorrectly called a man; because, although she is a woman in body, yet in the virtue of her soul she is made equal to good men. Therefore the woman gave birth to a male; because the Church gives birth every day to the members of him whom the blessed Virgin Mary gave birth to. Therefore there is one single male, whom the Virgin Mary gave birth to and whom the Church gives birth to every day; because Christ together with all his members is one Christ.

Revelation 12:5b Who is to rule all nations with an iron rod.

This was said above (On chapter 2).

Revelation 12:5c And her child was caught up to God and to his throne.

This was fulfilled when, on the fortieth day after he rose again, he ascended into heaven. The children of the Church, that is, the members of Christ, are caught up to God and to his throne every day, when after death they are led to heavenly things.

Revelation 12:6 And the woman fled into the wilderness, where she has a place prepared by God, so that they may nourish her there for one thousand two hundred and sixty days.

He calls Christ “the wilderness”; for it is called “wilderness” because it is alone, deserted by its inhabitants. Likewise Christ was forsaken by his own, as the Evangelist says: “He came to his own, and his own did not receive him” (Jn. 1:11). And through the prophet the Lord speaks, saying: “I have trodden the winepress alone, and of the nations there was no man with me” (Isa. 63:3). Therefore the woman fled into the wilderness, because the apostles and the other disciples, of whom the Church then consisted, having left the Devil and having left all that they possessed, followed Christ (Matt. 19:27). By the one thousand two hundred and sixty days is signified the time during which Christ preached. Therefore the woman is nourished for one thousand two hundred and sixty days, because the apostles and the other disciples, of whom, as we said above, the Church then consisted, for three and a half years were fed by the Lord with the feast of the Gospel.

Revelation 12:7a And there was a great battle in heaven, Michael and his angels were fighting with the dragon.

Since above he has briefly included the birth and preaching of Christ, it follows that he should add how, through his Passion, he conquered the Devil. Michael, which is interpreted “Who is like God,” signifies Christ. The battle was fought in heaven, that is, on account of heaven; namely, Michael fought for the salvation of all the elect against the dragon, because Christ, by preaching, suffering, and dying, fought for the salvation of humanity. The angels of Michael, that is, the apostles of Christ, fought against the dragon, namely by preaching, working miracles, and finally by dying for the name of Christ.

Revelation 12:7b And the dragon fought, and his angels.

The dragon and his angels fought against Michael when the Devil and the rest of the host of demons stirred up the Jews against Christ so that they might kill him. He also fought against the angels of Michael when he persecuted the apostles of Christ, both through the Jews and through the pagans, until he killed them.

Revelation 12:8 And they did not prevail, nor was any place found for them any longer in heaven.

The Devil did not prevail, nor his ministers, in overcoming Christ or his apostles; for in the very way they thought they would conquer, they were conquered. They thought they had conquered Christ through death, but from his death his name became renowned in the world. Therefore they did not prevail, nor was any place found for them any longer in heaven, because through the preaching of the apostles and the other saints they were driven out from the hearts of the elect, who are signified by heaven.

Revelation 12:9 And that great dragon was cast out, the ancient serpent, who is called the Devil and Satan, who deceives the whole world; and he was cast down to the earth, and his angels were sent down with him.

The Devil is rightly called a serpent, either because through the serpent he deceived the first human being, (Gen.3) or because with the poison of his malice he has destroyed humanity. The word “Devil” is interpreted as “one who falls downward”; for he first fell from heaven through pride; he fell again when, through Christ, he was cast out from the hearts of the elect, as the present Scripture shows. “Satan,” moreover, is interpreted as “adversary”; for he is an adversary to God and to all good people. Woe to those to whom the Devil is a friend! For the more he is anyone’s friend, the greater tortures he inflicts on that person; truly unhappy is the Devil’s friendship. But in the phrase, “who deceives the whole world,” the present tense is used in place of the past. He had deceived the human race, which is indicated by “the world”; but he had lost it in great part through Christ. Therefore the Devil and his angels were cast down to the earth, because, driven out from the elect, he took possession only of the wicked, who are signified by “the earth.”

Revelation 12:10a And I heard a loud voice from heaven saying: Now is made the salvation and the power and the kingdom of our God, and the authority of His Christ.

The loud voice from heaven is the voice of the angels, rejoicing for the salvation of humanity and praising God, just as the Lord says in the Gospel: There is greater joy in heaven over one sinner who does penance than over ninety-nine righteous persons who do not need penance (Luke 15:7). We can understand by the one sinner the Gentile people, and by the ninety-nine righteous persons the Jews: who do not reach the number one hundred, because the Law brought no one to perfection (Heb. 7:19); for a much greater joy arose in heaven from the conversion of the nations than from the observances of the Jews. Therefore, at the casting down of the Devil, the salvation of believers was accomplished: they also received the power to conquer the Devil, by whom they had previously been held as those who had been conquered. Likewise, the kingdom of our God was established; because in those over whom the Devil had reigned, the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit began to reign: and the authority of His Christ came about, when Christ began to possess those whom the Devil had possessed.

Revelation 12:10b Because the accuser of our brothers has been cast down, he who accused them before the sight of our God day and night.

Guilty men are accused before judges when their crimes are made known to them; but in the sight of God, who considers not only deeds but even thoughts, how can the Devil accuse anyone? Yet we are accused in the sight of God when we sin; and the Devil also accuses, when by his evil persuasion he casts people down into sins.

Revelation 12:11a And they themselves conquered him by the blood of the Lamb and by the word of their testimony.

They conquered by the blood of the Lamb, because the redemption by the blood of Christ granted this to the saints, that they could overcome the Devil. They also conquered by the word of their testimony, by keeping the faith they had professed and fulfilling it with works. But what follows is very obscure:

Revelation 12:11b And they did not love their lives unto death.

For how do the saints not love their lives, for the salvation of which they endure all hard and difficult things? How do they not love their lives, for whose sake they mortify themselves with vigils, fastings, abstinence, and practice other virtues? But if we wish to understand “life” as the present life when “soul” is mentioned, who is the saint that does not love his life, in which, as he lives, he gains for himself that heavenly glory which no eye has seen nor ear heard, nor can any mind comprehend (1 Cor. 2:9)? Or who has ever reached that glory without this present life? For the present life brings great gain to the saints, just as, conversely, it brings great loss to the wicked. Nevertheless, in a certain sense the saints are rightly said to hate their life; because, on account of the love of eternal life, the present life becomes disgusting and tiresome to them, as the Psalmist mournfully said: Alas for me, that my sojourning has been prolonged (Ps. 119:5). The Lord also says in the Gospel: Whoever loves his soul will lose it, and whoever hates his soul in this world will keep it unto eternal life (Jn. 12:25). For when he says in this world, he shows how we ought to understand this. Therefore, whoever loves his soul in this world will lose it, that is, he will lose it according to this age; and whoever hates his soul in this world, that is, according to this age, will keep it unto eternal life. The lovers of the world love their souls according to this age, because they would never want to leave this present life, so that they might always live in luxury. That rich man loved his soul according to this age, who said: Soul, you have many good things laid up for many years; eat, drink, rejoice, and be satisfied (Luke 12:19). But the saints, who do not wish to be conformed to this age, as the Apostle says (Rom. 12:2), but to be transformed in the renewal of spiritual understanding; who do not love the world nor the things that are in the world; who do not wish to be friends of this age, lest they be made enemies of God; they hate their souls in this world (1 Jn. 2:15), that is, they do not love them according to this age (James 4:4), but according to God, for whose love they despise all things that are temporary, so that they may keep their souls unto eternal life. Therefore, the saints did not love their souls unto death, in that they did not wish to preserve them in this present life by denying Christ; but for the name of Christ they chose rather to die, that they might find their souls in eternal life.

Revelation 12:12a Therefore be glad, heavens, and you who dwell in them.

Through “heavens” and “inhabitants of the heavens” we can understand the various Churches, which are one. Therefore the children of the Church are commanded to rejoice, because they have deserved to be freed from so immense an enemy through the grace of the Lord.

Revelation 12:12b Woe to the earth and the sea! because the Devil has come down to you, having great wrath, knowing that he has but a short time.

By “earth” we can understand the Jews, and by “sea” the pagans and the other wicked ones. Woe, then, to the Jews and to the unbelieving nations! because, on account of the wrath the Devil has, since he has lost such a vast multitude, he rules the wicked with such cruelty and casts them down into such great sins, that they are in no way worthy to return to the paths of life and be healed. It is also said that the Devil knows he has but a short time; for the more he sees the day of judgment drawing near, the more clever he becomes in deceiving humanity.

Revelation 12:13 And after the dragon saw that he was cast down to the earth, he persecuted the woman who had given birth to the male child.

Seeing that he, the Devil, had lost a very great multitude of the elect, and had been shut up within the straits of the hearts of the wicked, he persecuted the woman, that is, the Church; for he stirred up the emperors of the Romans and the whole multitude of the wicked to persecute the people of God.

Revelation 12:14 And two wings of a great eagle were given to the woman, so that she might fly into the desert, to her place, where she is nourished for a time, and times, and half a time, away from the face of the serpent.

By the eagle we can understand Christ; the two wings are the two Testaments. Therefore, two wings were given to the woman, because the Church has received the two Testaments, so that by their teaching she may both escape the Devil and daily ascend to the heavenly homeland. This homeland he calls a desert, just as the Lord says in the Gospel, when he said that he left the ninety-nine sheep in the desert and went to seek the one that had gone astray (Luke 15:3–7). He calls this homeland the place of the Church, as he himself will say in judgment: “Come, blessed of my Father, receive the kingdom that has been prepared for you from the foundation of the world” (Matt. 25:34). By “a time, and times, and half a time” is signified the time from the Passion of Christ up to the end of the world. In this span of time, therefore, the souls of the saints, that is, the Church, are fed with the feasts of the glory of the heavenly homeland in heavenly blessedness.

Revelation 12:15 And the serpent sent water out of his mouth after the woman, like a river, so that he might cause her to be swept away by the river.

The river of water is fleshly desires; for the Devil, seeing that the Church cannot be cast down by persecutions, but rather grows and is strengthened, wanted to send against her a multitude of fleshly desires, by which, once deceived, she would be drawn back to commit evil deeds.

Revelation 12:16 And the earth helped the woman, and opened its mouth and swallowed the river that the serpent cast out of its mouth.

By “earth” we can understand the wicked, who became receivers of bodily desires, by which the Devil wanted to entangle the faithful and draw them to himself. We can also understand Christ by this same earth; and since there is great power in the words of the Lord: For he spoke, and they were made; he commanded, and they were created (Ps. 148:5); his power can quite fittingly be understood by “the mouth of the earth.” Therefore the earth helped the woman, that is, Christ helped the Church, and opened his mouth and received the river that the serpent cast out of its mouth: that is, he opened the heart of his mercy, and his power utterly ended the river of vices.

Revelation 12:17 And the dragon was enraged with the woman, and went off to make war with the rest of her offspring, who keep the commandments of God and hold the testimony of Jesus.

The rest of the offspring of the Church are the elect who are to be born at the end of the world. Moreover, how the Devil carries out this battle is made clear in what follows.

Revelation 12:18 And he stood upon the sand of the sea.

By the sand of the sea is signified the multitude of the wicked who will exist at that time.

Chapter 13

Revelation 13:1a And I saw a beast coming up out of the sea, having seven heads and ten horns, and upon its horns ten crowns.

This beast signifies the Antichrist; those, however, who are indicated by the sand, and likewise by the sea, are the multitude of the wicked.
