Therefore the beast was seen to come up from the sea, because out of the company of the wicked the Antichrist will arise; and this beast is described as having seven heads and ten horns. Likewise, the dragon above was said to have had seven heads, and on the heads seven crowns, by which we said all the wicked were signified, those whom the Devil has used to deceive humanity. But this beast is described as having ten crowns not on the heads, but on the ten horns; and therefore by the ten horns are signified the nations that the Antichrist will conquer to himself; while the seven heads signify the seven principal vices. And so that we may better understand what follows, it has pleased me, in the listing of the vices, to follow Prudentius13, who spoke excellently in verse about the conflict of vices and virtues. For he placed idolatry as the first vice, lust as the second, anger as the third, pride as the fourth, luxury as the fifth. For although lust is so joined to luxury as a limb is to a body, on account of which the Fathers often used “luxury” in place of “lust”, yet there is a difference between lust and luxury. He set greed as the sixth, blasphemy or discord as the seventh. And because those who will be subject to these vices the Antichrist will easily deceive and join to himself, it is rightly through the seven heads that the seven vices are signified.

Concerning these seven vices the Lord speaks in the Gospel, saying: When an unclean spirit has gone out of a man, it walks through waterless places, seeking rest; and not finding it, it says: I will return to my house from which I came out. And when it comes, it finds it swept and decorated. Then it goes and takes with it seven other spirits more wicked than itself, and they enter in and dwell there (Matt. 12:43). The unclean spirit went out of the man when the Devil went out from the Jews when they received the Law. It walked through waterless places, that is, through the nations of the Gentiles, which were waterless because they did not have the greenness of faith. It did not find rest there, because when Christ came in the flesh, it lost the Gentiles whom it had long held. It returned to its house from which it had come out, because it went back to possess the Jews, in that they were unwilling to believe in Christ. It found the house of the Jews empty, because they did not have Christ as a guest. Yet we must ask why he said that those minds which did not have God as an inhabitant were swept and decorated. But they were swept for the Devil, not for God; for what is clean in the sight of God appears unclean to the Devil, and what is unclean to God is clean to the Devil. For just as to a beetle the dung of any living creature seems cleaner than any clean and delightful food, so too the Devil loves more those hearts that are filled with every vice than those that excel in all virtues. Now, brooms are what sweep a house free of garbage. Therefore God has his own brooms, and the Devil has his. The brooms of God are those which sweep his house, so that he himself may be able to dwell in it: namely, confession of sins, repentance, tears, and almsgiving. The brooms of the Devil are those which, according to him, sweep his house: that is, love of the world, frivolousness of mind, contempt for the commandments of God, and many others. Now the seven spirits that the Devil took with him are the seven principal vices. With these seven attendants, then, the Devil possesses the Jews even to this day. But it can be asked why these seven principal vices are said to be worse than the Devil. In truth, the vices are worse for us than the demons; for the latter attack us from outside, but the vices from within wage war on us continually. For just as enemies who attack a city from outside are more easily endured, since there are walls set between, than those who, being inside, are already ravaging the city itself, so demons, who assault from without, are more easily endured than the vices which ceaselessly lay waste to us within.

Revelation 13:1b And upon his heads names of blasphemy.

The blasphemy of Antichrist will have many names, because in many ways he will blaspheme Christ.

Revelation 13:2a And the beast that I saw was like a leopard, and its feet were like a bear’s feet, and its mouth was like the mouth of a lion.

By the leopard, which is said to have various colors, the hypocrisy of the Antichrist is signified: for although he will be a most wicked man, he will vary himself with different virtues, so that he may more easily deceive all foolish people. By the bear, moreover, which is a very calculating animal, his slyness for deceiving men can be shown. By the lion, likewise, his cruelty for tearing apart the people of God is signified.

Revelation 13:2b And the dragon gave him his power and great authority.

The Devil will give the Antichrist his power, which is wholly evil; for he will dwell in him, and through him he will carry out whatever the wickedness of the Devil can devise. But since Scripture says: “There is no power except from God” (Rom. 13:1), how will the Devil give great authority to the Antichrist? God will give power to the Antichrist, but he will so far transform that very power into evil that it may be believed, and truly be, entirely demonic.

Revelation 13:3a And I saw one of its heads as if slain unto death, and its mortal wound was healed.

We said above that by the seven heads of Antichrist the seven principal vices are signified. Therefore, by the head which was seen not slain, but as if slain, blasphemy is signified. We must therefore ask how blasphemy is said to be as if slain. For in the earliest times of the Gospel there were many heretics who tried to undermine the faith of the Church by their errors; but almighty God chose as defenders of the Church the orthodox Fathers from among the children of that same Church, and, filling them with the river of his wisdom, through their teaching he rooted out every heresy from his Church. Such were Ambrose, Hilary, Jerome, Augustine, Gregory, and many others who also published a very great number of books, by which all heresies that existed and that could exist were destroyed. Therefore blasphemy is now as if slain, because although there are many who conceive many blasphemies in their minds, they do not dare to utter the evil they have conceived, lest they be shamefully refuted by the teaching of these men. Therefore this head, that is, blasphemy, will seem to be dead in the times of Antichrist; but it will be raised up again by the Devil in Antichrist, whom he will inflame to blaspheme Christ to such an extent that he will surpass all blasphemers in their blasphemies.

Revelation 13:3b And the whole earth marveled after the beast.

By the earth sinful men are signified.

Revelation 13:4 And they worshiped the dragon, because he gave power to the beast; and they worshiped the beast, saying: Who is like the beast, and who will be able to fight with it?

How will they worship the dragon, that is, the Devil, whom they will not see? But those who are signified by the earth will worship the Antichrist, and in the Antichrist they will worship the Devil, saying that no one is like the Antichrist, nor is there anyone who can be matched to his strength.

Revelation 13:5a And it was given to him a mouth speaking great things and blasphemies.

“It was given” means “it was permitted by God,” so that he might speak great things about himself, saying that he is the Son of God, and that he might speak blasphemies against God.

Revelation 13:5b And power was given to him to act for forty-two months.

It seems difficult that in so very short a time, that is, in three and a half years, he could subject all nations to himself and draw humanity, apart from a few of the elect, to his worship; for there will indeed be many elect, but in comparison with the unbelievers, very few. Yet it can come about that at first he subjects all nations to his dominion, but afterwards, lifted up in pride, he decrees that he be worshiped as God. For as it seems to me, in the three and a half years only the time of persecution is included, in which, both by himself and by his disciples and by the display of wondrous signs, he will work with all his strength so that all men may devote to him the worship due to divinity. But those who will have wished to resist, he will afflict with many torments that have never been heard of, so that, compelled, they do what they will refuse to do of their own free will.

Revelation 13:6 And he opened his mouth in blasphemies against God, to blaspheme His name, and His tabernacle, and those who dwell in heaven.

For he will indeed open his mouth to blaspheme God the Father, that is to say, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit. He will also blaspheme the name of God by saying that Christ is not God; he will likewise blaspheme the tabernacle of God, that is, the Church of God, saying that the Christian religion is of no benefit to the faithful, but rather brings destruction than salvation. And he will blaspheme those who dwell in heaven, that is, the martyrs and the other saints, saying that they can be of no help to anyone, and that whatever is stated about them by the faithful is false.

Revelation 13:7 And it was given to him to wage war against the saints and to overcome them.

He will wage war against the saints, winning over some by flattery, others by terror, and in the end by inflicting the worst torments on them, so that he may lead them into his errors; but it is impossible for the elect to be led into error. He will overcome them not by conquering, but by killing.

Revelation 13:8a And power was given to him over every tribe and people and tongue and nation; and all who dwell on the earth adored him.

This will be fulfilled according to the literal sense. Many will say, why does God allow such things to happen, that Antichrist should so wickedly deceive humanity? In truth, since there are two reasons, he will permit such things; for as he himself says in the Gospel: In those days the love of many will grow cold, and iniquity will abound (Matt. 24:12); indeed the wickedness of men will grow so greatly, and they will so provoke God to anger by their crimes, that they will be worthy to be subjected to so wicked a man and to be deceived by him, as Scripture bears witness: He causes a hypocritical man to reign because of the stubbornness of the people (Job 34:30). For God will hand them over, as the Apostle says, to a wicked mind (Rom. 1:28), so that they do what is not fitting; and they will have no concern for anything, provided only that they carry out whatever evil they have perceived. By the most just permission of God it will come to pass that, deceived by Antichrist, they perish more grievously. God will also allow this to happen for the testing of his elect, so that, just as gold is tested by fire, so they too may be tested and examined in the persecutions of Antichrist, as it is written about them in Daniel: Many shall be chosen and made white, and shall be tested like gold. (Dan. 12:10) It continues:

Revelation 13:8b Whose names are not written in the book of life of the Lamb who was slain from the foundation of the world.

The book of life is the predestination of almighty God: the wicked are not written in the book of life, because they are not predestined to eternal life. But we must ask how the Lamb, that is, Christ, is said to have been slain from the foundation of the world. For that phrase which follows,

Revelation 13:9 If anyone has ears, let him hear,

Wherever it is set down in the divine Scriptures, shows that what has gone before is obscure; for it calls for the ears of the heart and of understanding. Therefore the Lamb was slain from the beginning of the world, because his death was prefigured from the dawn of humanity in the holy fathers. For it was prefigured in the first man, when, as he slept, Eve was made from his rib; likewise it was prefigured in Abel, who was killed by his brother Cain; it was also prefigured in Isaac, whom his father offered to the Lord as a burnt offering; likewise in Joseph, whom his brothers sold; his death was also prefigured in the numerous rites of sacrifices. As often, then, as the death of Christ was either prefigured or foretold by the holy fathers, so often, as it were, was the Lamb slain. Yet this can also be understood simply, so that it does not say that the Lamb was slain from the beginning of the world, but that the names of the saints have been written from the beginning of the world in the book of the Lamb, who was slain for their salvation.

Revelation 13:10a Whoever leads into captivity goes into captivity; whoever kills with the sword must be killed with the sword.

Antichrist, who will take the Christian people captive from their Creator, will himself be taken captive from this present life and thrust down into hell [infernum]; and because by the sword of his malice he is going to destroy the souls of many, and with the bodily sword he will kill the bodies of the righteous, he himself will be condemned by the sword of everlasting death.

Revelation 13:10b This is the patience and the faith of the saints.

That is, the patience and the faith of the saints will be demonstrated in the persecutions of Antichrist.

Revelation 13:11 And I saw another beast coming up out of the earth, having two horns like a Lamb, and it spoke like a dragon.

This beast either signifies one of the disciples of Antichrist, worse than the others, or many who will preach him. It was seen to have come up out of the earth because it will arise from the company of the wicked; and it was seen to have two horns like the Lamb. Above we said that by the seven horns of the Lamb all the elect are signified, whom we divided into seven parts: by the fifth horn, we said the Jews who believed in Christ were signified; and by the sixth, the Gentiles who likewise believed in Christ. Therefore the beast will have two horns like the Lamb, because by deceiving the Jews and the Gentiles it will gain possession of them. For although many from among the Jews will believe in Christ through the preaching of Elijah, yet the greatest part of them will follow Antichrist, as the Lord says in the Gospel: “I have come in the name of my Father, and you did not receive me; another will come in his own name, and you will receive him” (Jn. 5:43). Therefore this beast spoke like a dragon, because its words were demonic; for the Devil who will speak through Antichrist will himself also speak through his disciples.

Revelation 13:12 And it exercised all the power of the former beast in its presence.

When he says “in its presence,” he means “in its name”; for Antichrist will not be able to be personally present in all the places where he will have been preached by his own followers. For Antichrist will indeed work miracles by demonic craft [arte], and his disciples will also perform them; but their miracles will be false and not true, as the Apostle says: “Whose coming is according to the working of Satan, in all powers and signs and lying wonders” (2 Thess. 2:9). For it is not believable that they could restore to health those whom God has blinded or weakened; but only those whom they themselves have harmed, injuring them in secret and healing them openly. And Antichrist himself will display himself to people with such splendor that he will be believed to be God by the foolish, as the Apostle says: “So that he sits in the temple, showing himself as though he were God” (2 Thess. 2:4).

Revelation 13:13 And he made the earth and those who dwell in it worship the first beast, whose mortal wound was healed. And he performed great signs, so that he even made fire come down from heaven to earth in the sight of men.

We read in the book of Job that the Devil, with God’s permission, made fire come down from heaven and consumed the sheep and children of Job (Job 1:16). What he did there by himself, this he will do in the final time through the Antichrist and his followers.

Revelation 13:14a And he deceived the inhabitants of the earth because of the signs that were given to him to perform in the sight of the beast.

There is nothing that so deceives simple people as the display of signs. And therefore the ministers of Antichrist, by reason of the working of signs, will easily deceive the multitude of fools.

Revelation 13:14b Saying to the inhabitants of the earth that they should make an image of the beast which has the wound of the sword and lived.

That is, that in images Antichrist may be worshiped. But when he said that the beast had the wound of the sword and lived, it may come to pass that by demonic art Antichrist pretends to die and to rise again, so that by this deed he may more easily deceive men; which Simon Magus14 is said to have done.

Revelation 13:15a And it was given to him to give spirit to the image of the beast, and for the image of the beast to speak.

But we have also heard that certain magicians have done this; for by necromantic craft, they used to make statues seem to move and to speak. We can understand this in another way. An image has the appearance of a man, but is not a man; likewise the Antichrist, on account of the working of powers and signs, will be thought to be God, but this will be utterly false; for it cannot be that a most ungodly man should have anything of divinity in himself. They will therefore make an image of the Antichrist, those who will think him to be God; and the ministers of the Antichrist will give spirit to the image of the beast when they compel those who will have believed in the Antichrist to blaspheme Christ.

Revelation 13:15b And he will cause that whoever will not have worshiped the image of the beast will be killed.

The faithful who will not have been willing to worship the Antichrist will be put to death, worn down by many torments; for there will be such a tribulation, as the Lord said in the Gospel, as has not been from the beginning, nor will be, so that even the elect would be led into error, if that were possible. (Matt. 24:21)

Revelation 13:16 And he will cause all, small and great, rich and poor, free and slave, to have a mark on their right hand or on their foreheads.

It can be that, just as we have the mark of Christ, that is, the cross with which we are signed, so Antichrist may have his own mark, with which those who will have believed in him are signed. We can also understand this in another way. The disciples of Antichrist will be praisers of vices, for they will teach that wine and the other riches were created precisely so that people might use them abundantly and sufficiently; and that God therefore made the reproductive members so that men might have intercourse with women, and that it is wrong for anyone to abstain from sexual relations; that all carnal desires must be fulfilled. And when they have strengthened that unspeakable teaching by mighty works [virtutibus], they will easily deceive earthly people who are inclined to evil rather than to good. Very often, indeed, works are understood by “hands”; therefore they will have the mark of Antichrist on their right hand, because they will carry out works of wickedness in harmony with the teaching of Antichrist; and they will have it also on the forehead, through which the mind is often discerned, because from their depraved deeds it will be recognized what utterly wicked minds they bear.

Revelation 13:17 And that no one may be able to sell or to buy, except one who has the mark of the name of the beast, or the number of his name.

We know that human life cannot continue unless people at some time either sell or buy; therefore the persecution of Antichrist against the faithful will rage more cruelly, in proportion as this is forbidden to them, without which they will not be able to live. But the ministers of Antichrist will do this in order that, in a time of necessity, they may catch those who will have wished covertly to preserve the faith of Christianity.

Revelation 13:18 Here is wisdom. Whoever has understanding, let him calculate the number of the beast; for it is a human number, and its number is six hundred sixty-six.

Many have said numerous things about this number, and have discovered several names in whose letters this number is found. Yet they have not been able to foresee whether Antichrist will possess any of these names; and concerning a matter so uncertain I dare to define nothing. For who knows whether the name that will be provided to him by his parents will have this number?

Chapter 14

Revelation 14:1a And I saw, and behold, a Lamb was standing upon Mount Zion.

If we carefully inspect the text of the following reading, we will see that the things said about the one hundred and forty‑four thousand belong rather to the elect, whose souls rejoice with the Lord in heavenly blessedness, than to those who toil in this life. Therefore the Lamb represents Christ; and Zion is interpreted as “watchtower” or “lookout”; by the mountain of Zion we must understand the heavenly homeland. Therefore, on Mount Zion the Lamb was seen, because in heavenly blessedness Christ abides with his saints.

Revelation 14:1b And with him were one hundred and forty‑four thousand, having his name and the name of his Father written on their foreheads.

In the third vision which goes before this one, it is said that one hundred and forty‑four thousand from the twelve tribes were chosen and marked with the sign of faith. That one hundred and forty‑four thousand, therefore, as the explanation of the names of the twelve tribes shows, refers to the elect who work in this life; but this one hundred and forty‑four thousand signifies the elect who rejoice with the Lord in heavenly glory, as the text of the present reading plainly signifies. But why does he say that they have only the name of the Father and the Son written on their foreheads, and keep silence about the Holy Spirit? Because, evidently, wherever we read of the Father and the Son, we must understand the Holy Spirit as well. Moreover, by the foreheads we can understand the hearts of the saints: they have the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit written on their foreheads, because from their minds the love of God can never be torn away, as it was torn away from the hearts of certain angels who, on account of pride, fell from heaven.

Revelation 14:2a And I heard a voice from heaven, like the voice of many waters, and like the voice of great thunder.

Often through John the faithful of the present life are shown; by waters, however, peoples are signified. And because from all nations the elect are daily gathered and placed in heavenly blessedness, the holy ones of God established on earth, who are signified through John, constantly hear the voice of many waters; because they perceive with the ears of the heart the teaching of the saints who dwell in heaven, and, with whatever strength they can, imitate their actions. Therefore the same voice is said to be like thunder, because it is accustomed to strike fear into the hearts of those who hear; for they are frightened when they realize that they cannot rise to their perfection.

Revelation 14:2b And the voice that I heard was like the voice of harpists playing on their harps.

The voice of harpists is exceedingly sweet to those who hear; and what is sweeter to the faithful than the remembrance of the saints dwelling in heaven, while they ponder in their minds how great their glory is, and humbly pray that they may someday achieve it?

Revelation 14:3 And they sang, as it were, a new song before the throne, and before the four living creatures and the elders; and no one could sing the song except those one hundred and forty‑four thousand who were redeemed from the earth.

Here there is very great obscurity; for he says that the one hundred and forty‑four thousand had not been defiled with women and that they are virgins, and that therefore they sing a new song which no one can sing except they alone. Yet we know that some of the apostles and martyrs had wives. Are those who strove to preserve bodily virginity to chant praises to God, and these others to be silent in the praises of God? Especially since we know that from both sexes people of little merit have preserved bodily virginity until death, such as infants and children, whom their age does not allow to lose virginity. We have also seen certain persons, responsible for some sins, preserve bodily virginity until death, whom we believe will be purified by purgatorial fire, so that they may someday deserve to be numbered among the righteous. Shall we dare to prefer such persons to the apostles and martyrs, whom Scripture does not conceal had wives, so that they should offer that most sweet song to God, from which Peter and those like him would be excluded? For just as the vision of God is sweet to the saints, so also is the praise of God; and just as they cannot be full with the vision of God, so neither can they be satisfied with the praises of God. And the closer they are to the Lord God according to the quality of their virtues, so much the more fervent they are in the praises of God. Who indeed can rightly consider how great a portion of glory is taken away from the saints if they are regarded as lacking a share in the praises of God? Nor do we say this in order to detract from virginity, which is a great virtue and the companion of the angels, provided, however, that they have not defiled it by vice. For who can weigh how great a good it is to possess integrity of body and soul? Therefore, because we cannot understand the things said above about virgins as referring only to those who have preserved bodily virginity, it is necessary that we search out who the virgins are, and who those are who will not be able to sing the song of virgins. For if we carefully attend to the surface of the reading, we shall find that these things are said not about virgins alone, but about all holy souls.

We said above that by the one hundred and forty‑four thousand are signified the souls of the saints, who in heavenly blessedness never cease continually to praise God; but by the throne, and the four living creatures, and the elders before whom they are said to sing a new song, are signified the faithful who are placed in this life, from whom the Church is established, as we said in the second Vision. Therefore, before the throne and the four living creatures and the elders the one hundred and forty‑four thousand are said to have sung a new song; for the New Testament is called the new song, as the prophet says: “Sing to the Lord a new song, his praise from the ends of the earth” (Isa. 42:10). Now it is called the New Testament because another preceded it, which is called Old; or because it makes new peoples, when it makes from the children of the Devil the children of God. The faithful also, placed in this life, have their songs; but in these songs there is often confession of sins, often supplication for sins, often a remembrance of eternal torments. And so in singing they often stir themselves to tears; often they are accustomed in the praises of God to pray; often they lift up the minds of the faithful to the hope of the heavenly fatherland. But in that song of the heavenly spirits nothing sad, nothing that disturbs the mind, resonates; and the more it is poured forth, the more it pours joy and gladness into the minds of those who sing. Therefore, just as it is called the New Testament because the Old preceded it, so that song also of the heavenly spirits is called a new song, because another preceded it which is old to those who daily pass over to heavenly blessedness, and new is that to which they come. And this song they are said to have sung before the throne of God, that is, in the sight of the throne; for what is in our sight we look upon without ceasing. Therefore, in the sight of the faithful, who are signified by the throne and the four living creatures and the elders, the souls of the faithful are said to have sung a new song; because holy men, placed on this earth, continually behold with the eyes of the mind the glory of the saints dwelling in heaven, and that they may some day come to their fellowship, they strive with all their strength.

And no one could sing the song except those one hundred and forty-four thousand who were redeemed from the earth; that is, none of those who are signified by the throne and the four living creatures and the elders. For faithful men, placed now on earth, see as it were through a mirror and in a mystery the glory of the saints abiding in heaven, and because they passionately desire that song, they as it were hear it, but they cannot sing it; because “eye has not seen, nor ear heard, nor has it entered into the heart of man what God has prepared for those who love him” (1 Cor. 2:9). They are said to have been redeemed from the earth; indeed, while placed on earth, they were redeemed with the precious blood of Christ. But it is better that by “being redeemed” we understand “being taken up”; for they were taken up from the earth and placed in heavenly blessedness.

Revelation 14:4a These are those who have not been defiled with women; for they are virgins.

Here again a question arises; for if in that blessedness there are different ranks, such as apostles, martyrs, confessors, and virgins, how is it that all the souls of the saints are said to be virgins, and so virgins that they are said not to have been defiled with women? And if what the Lord says is true, indeed it is true, since it cannot happen that God should lie: “Whoever looks at a woman to lust after her has already committed adultery with her in his heart”, (Matt. 5:28) how could all the souls of the saints, throughout the whole span of this present life, have been able to be clean from the defilement of women? We must therefore inquire how this is to be understood; for many were adulterers and fornicators before baptism, but when they believed in Christ and did penance for their sins and sought the font of baptism, whatever was filthy in them was wiped away by the grace of the Holy Spirit through the waters of baptism. As they persevered, even to the end of life, in the good which they promised in that same baptism, when the Lord came to remove them by death from this earthly dwelling, their souls were found to be virgins, that is, whole, and clean from all corruption of vices. The souls of these, therefore, are rightly called virgins, because all their corruption has been erased by the waters of baptism and by good works. There were also many who committed many crimes after baptism, but when, through penance and tears and through mercy and other good works, they strove to blot out the evil they had done, and persevered in their good commitment, when the Lord came, their souls were found to be virgins; because whatever was wicked in them was purified by the remedies of love. There were also married people who loved God with all their strength and strove to lead a life in virtues. The souls of these, then, are rightly called virgins, because whatever stains they drew from union with women (Gen. 2:24) has been wiped away by good works. In a similar way this must be understood of the other ranks.

We can also understand the bodies of the saints by “women,” and the souls by “men”; for just as man and woman are called one flesh, so the body and soul are one human being. And just as the woman is lower than the man and therefore must be governed by the man and accept his rule, so the body is far lower than the soul and therefore must be governed and restrained by the spirit, and its desires broken. Because the saints strove to do this, they were not defiled with women, because from the union of bodies they drew no stain by which they would greatly offend God.

Revelation 14:4b They follow the Lamb wherever he goes.

Where does he go who fills all things and is wholly present everywhere? But the Apostle teaches how he goes, saying: “He has mercy on whom he wills, and he hardens whom he wills” (Rom. 9:18). God therefore “goes” by having mercy on some and withdrawing his mercy from others. The saints in truth follow, because to those on whom he grants his mercy, they also grant it; and from those from whom he cuts it off, they also cut it off. We cannot understand this as referring to the elect while they are placed on earth; for they often show mercy to those on whom God does not have mercy, like Samuel, who mourned for Saul (1 Sam. 15:35), whom God had cast away from his presence; and some saints also wish to condemn those on whom God has mercy, like Jonah, who desired the Ninevites to be destroyed, whom God had decreed to save. (Jonah 4:3)

Revelation 14:4c They have been redeemed from among all people as first fruits for God and the Lamb.

In the Law it is commanded that the first fruits of the crops be offered to God (Exod. 22:29); by the first fruits of the crops, the elect are signified. For just as from the multitude of fruits a small portion of first fruits was offered to God, so from the multitude of peoples a small number of elect is chosen, who are offered by the angels to God in the temple of heavenly glory, as the Lord says in the Gospel: “For many are called, but few are chosen” (Matt. 22:14). Therefore the saints, in the manner of first fruits, are taken from among human beings and placed in heavenly blessedness.

Revelation 14:5 And in their mouth no lie was found.

What is said here seems to be very difficult; for there are indeed some lies that are very serious, and there are others that must be counted among light sins, from which in our times I do not know whether anyone can abstain for his whole life, unless he were an infant who does not know how to speak. Therefore it does not seem to me that this statement is made about every kind of lie, but there is a very serious kind of lying, when we lie about the things that we promised in baptism. This, then, is the lie that was not found in the mouth of the saints; because they endeavored to keep, to the very end of life, what they had promised in baptism. And even if there were some who after baptism committed many sins, yet no lie was found in their mouth; because through penance and works of love they strove to wipe out before death the evil they had done: for they are without stain. And here he shows quite clearly that the things contained in this lesson refer not to the faithful who labor in this life, but to those who rest in the heavenly dwellings. For who, being placed in this life, can be without stain? when this same John says: If we say that we have no sin, we deceive ourselves, and the truth is not in us (1 Jn. 1:8). But the souls of the saints are without stain; because if they have contracted any defilement from dwelling in the world, it has been wiped out either through penance and tears and works of love, or through scourges, or at least after death through purgatorial fire.

I have said what I felt. But if anyone should wish to apply the text of this reading to the present Church, and to understand by the one hundred and forty‑four thousand either the preachers or the other righteous people still living in this life; and to judge that by the new song, which no one could sing except those alone, the Gospel is indicated; let such a person consider that the holy Gospel is preached not only by the elect, but also very often by the wicked, about whom we believe the Lord to have said in the Gospel: Many will say to me in that day, Lord, Lord, did we not prophesy in your name, and in your name cast out many demons, and in your name do many mighty works? And then I will declare to them: Truly I say to you, I do not know you. Depart from me, all you workers of iniquity (Matt. 7:22). About such people the apostle Paul also speaks, saying: There are some who proclaim Christ out of rivalry, not sincerely, thinking to stir up trouble for me in my chains (Philip. 1:17). But if anyone should decide that the arrangement of this reading refers to the virgins of the present time, that is, to those whom love of God urges daily to keep integrity, along with the addition of other good works, it seems to me that in this they possess a sound understanding. Yet they must observe that this understanding cannot be made to fit if, by the new song in this passage, they understand the Gospel; but rather the spiritual joy that grows for them from the keeping of virginity. Therefore no one can sing the new song except those alone, because no one is able to possess the joy of virginity except those alone who are accustomed daily to offer this, adorned with other good works, to God. Likewise, whoever wishes to apply this interpretation to those who, together with the palm of virginity, have now merited to ascend to the heavenly kingdoms, does not depart from right understanding. But let them know that this understanding does not allow that, by the new song, they should understand some song that can be composed by voice, tongue, or lips; but rather the reward of virginity, which they merit to receive eternally from the Lord. And no one can sing this song except those alone, because no one receives the reward of virginity except those who, while placed in this world, have striven to keep it with an unstained mind for love of Christ. About such people the prophet Isaiah speaks, saying: Therefore says the Lord to the eunuchs: Those who keep my Sabbaths, and choose the things that I delight in, and hold fast my covenant, I will give them a place in my house and within my walls, and a name better than sons and daughters; I will give them an everlasting name which shall not perish (Isa. 56:4–5).

Revelation 14:6 And I saw another angel flying in mid-heaven, having an eternal Gospel, to preach to those who sit upon the earth, and to every nation, and tribe, and tongue, and people, saying with a loud voice.

Again he briefly sums up everything; for just as above, when he wished to relate the evils of Antichrist, he began from the coming of Christ, and, first recounting the persecutions which the Church has suffered from the Devil, came at last to Antichrist; so also here, wishing to describe the Day of Judgment, he begins from the coming of Christ, and, under the figure of three angels, briefly going over everything, reaches the Day of Judgment. This angel, indeed, signifies Christ and His apostles, and the other preachers of Christ. He is said to have had the eternal Gospel, because the teaching of the Gospel was given by Christ, the apostles, and the other preachers, that they might preach it to the nations. This Gospel is said to be eternal because it grants eternity to all who live by it. It is also said to have been preached to those who sit upon the earth; for the nations to whom the Gospel was preached sought nothing but the earth, and had placed all their rest in earthly things. But let us hear what this angel preaches.

Revelation 14:7a Fear the Lord, and give Him honor; for the hour of His judgment has come, and adore Him who made heaven and earth, the sea and the springs of waters.

Fittingly he placed the fear of the Lord first, and afterward honor; for the fear of the Lord drives a person to repentance, and repentance in turn urges him to perform good works. Therefore we pay honor to God when we do good works, just as the Lord Himself says: “Let your good works be seen, and let them glorify your Father who is in heaven” (Matt. 5:16). Now the hour of judgment began from the moment the Gospel started to be preached. Therefore, those will be severely judged on the day of judgment who, hearing the word of the Gospel and despising it, were unwilling to accept faith in Christ. Likewise, those who did indeed receive faith in Christ, but were unwilling to fulfill it by works; for to whomever more is entrusted, more will be required from him. But concerning those who did not hear the word of God, the Lord says in the Gospel: “He who does not believe is already judged” (Jn. 3:18).

Revelation 14:7b And worship him who made heaven and earth, and the sea and the springs of waters;

That is, leave behind the deaf, mute, and blind idols, and worship him who made all things out of nothing.

Revelation 14:8b And another angel followed, saying.

Another angel signifies the doctors of the Church, who, when the persecutions had ended, governed the Church of God.

Revelation 14:8c Fallen, fallen is great Babylon.

By Babylon, as we said above, the city of the Devil is signified, which is made up of all the ungodly. Now it had fallen because every practice of demons had been destroyed through faith in Christ. And it is said to have fallen twice, because it fell both on the side of the persecutors and on the side of diabolic worship.

Revelation 14:8d She made all nations drink from the wine of her wrath.

The wine of wrath is the errors of the pagans, by which they provoked God to anger. But since Babylon is nothing other than all the nations, that is, all the wicked, how did she make all nations drink from the wine of her wrath? In truth, since one taught another, and that one a third, and the third a fourth, and therefore the demonic error passed into all men, Babylon certainly made all nations drink the wine of the wrath of God.

Revelation 14:9-10 And the third angel followed them, saying with a loud voice: If anyone worships the beast and its image, and receives its mark on his forehead or on his hand, he too will drink of the wine of the wrath of God, which is mixed with the unmixed in the cup of Him.

The third angel are the preachers who are to come in the times of Antichrist; for they will preach that no one should worship Antichrist, nor bend the ears of the heart to his worst teaching, nor imitate his works; and they will bear witness that if anyone does this, he is to be condemned to everlasting punishment. By the wine of the wrath of God is signified the final damnation that will be inflicted on the wicked on the day of judgment; and we call “unmixed” what is pure. By “unmixed,” therefore, the justice of God is signified, and by “cup” we can understand the judgment of God. Therefore the wine is mixed with what is unmixed in the cup of God, because the final damnation by which the wicked will be condemned will be mingled with justice in the cup of God, that is, in the judgment of God; for it will be brought to completion by the most just judgment of God. The wicked will therefore drink the wine of the wrath of God, when it is said to them: Depart from me, you cursed, into everlasting fire, which has been prepared for the Devil and his angels (Matt. 25:41).

Revelation 14:11a And he will be tormented with fire and sulfur in the sight of the holy angels and in the sight of the Lamb; and the smoke of their torments will rise forever and ever.

Fire burns, but sulfur gives off a most terrible stench. With fire they will be physically tormented, those who were unwilling to extinguish within themselves the fire of vices; they will be tortured by the stench of sulfur, for whom the stench of lust was sweet. They will be tormented in the sight of the holy angels and in the sight of the Lamb, that is, so that they may be tormented before the eyes of those in whose sight they did not fear to commit many evils.

Revelation 14:11b And they will have no rest day or night, those who have worshiped the beast and its image, and whoever receives the mark of its name.

Why does he say that the wicked will have no rest either by day or by night, since the elect are to possess an eternal day, and the wicked an everlasting night? But he spoke in a human manner.

Revelation 14:12 This is the patience of the saints, who keep the commandments of God and the faith of Jesus.

That is, such is the patience of the saints, and it brings forth such fruit as is shown in what follows.

Revelation 14:13a And I heard a voice from heaven saying to me: Write, “Blessed are the dead who die in the Lord.”

Such therefore is the patience of the saints, who keep the commandments of God and the faith of Jesus, that they may be blessed forever. But what is it that he says: Blessed are the dead who die in the Lord? For who, being dead, can die? Surely no dead person can die, unless he first receives back his soul, so that he can die again. But they are blessed, and they die in the Lord, who first die to the world, and afterwards in the flesh: who first extinguish in themselves the old man, that is, all wickedness, both spiritual and bodily, so that they can say with the Apostle: The world has been crucified to me, and I to the world (Gal. 6:14). They indeed are the ones who die in the Lord, of whom the Lord says in the Gospel: But whoever perseveres to the end, this one will be saved (Matt. 24:13).

Revelation 14:13b From now on, says the Spirit, that they may rest from their labors.

“From now on,” that is, from the time of their death, the saints will rest from their labors. For their works follow them. How do they follow? Did not the works of Cornelius go before him, to whom it was said by the angel: “Your prayers and your alms have been remembered in the sight of God” (Acts 10:4)? But he used “follow” with the meaning “to accompany”; for an army that accompanies the king is also said to follow him, though some go before, some follow behind, and some march at his right or left. Therefore the works of the saints follow them, because the reward of those works accompanies them forever. We can also understand this spiritually of those who, in the time of Antichrist, are to be killed for the name of our Lord Jesus Christ; for they will indeed be blessed, and will die in the Lord, those who die for the faith of our Lord Jesus Christ. Now, since he has related the punishment of those who will come to believe in Antichrist, and has briefly summed up the blessedness of those who will persevere in the faith of Christ until the end, it follows that he should describe the day of judgment, in which all these things will be fulfilled.

Revelation 14:14-19 And I saw, and behold, a white cloud, and upon the cloud one sitting like the Son of Man, having on his head a golden crown, and in his hand a sharp sickle. And another angel went out from the temple, crying with a loud voice to him who sat upon the cloud, saying: Send forth your sickle, and reap; for the hour has come to reap, since the harvest of the earth has dried up. And he who sat upon the cloud sent his sickle into the earth, and reaped the earth. And another angel went out from the temple, which is in heaven, having also himself a sharp sickle. And another angel went out from the altar, who had power over fire, and cried with a loud voice to him who had the sharp sickle, saying: Send forth your sharp sickle, and gather the clusters of the vine of the earth, for its grapes are ripe. And the angel sent his sharp sickle into the earth, and gathered the vine of the earth and cast it into the great winepress of the wrath of God.

I have set down almost the whole passage, so that, being examined from every side, its winding turns may be seen more clearly. For each of these angels signifies Christ: similarly also the two sickles have one signification; for they signify that fire by which the whole world is to be destroyed; because just as in the first time the world was destroyed by water, so also at the end of times it will be destroyed by fire. (2 Peter 3:6–7) But it must be asked by us why one Christ is figured through two angels, and one destruction through two sickles. For the first angel was seen to have sat upon a white cloud, and to have had a golden crown: but the second is not said to have had a golden crown, nor to have sat upon a white cloud. And the first is said to have cut the harvest of the earth, but the second the clusters of the vine of the earth: but it is not said in what place the first placed the harvest of the earth: but the second is said to have sent the clusters of the vine of the earth into the great winepress of the wrath of God. It seems therefore to me that by the harvest of the earth the elect are signified: but by the clusters, from which wine arises, which alienates the minds of men, the wicked are signified. This is the harvest, concerning which the Lord says in the Gospel: In the time of harvest I will say to the reapers: Gather first the tares, and bind them in bundles to burn: but gather the wheat into my barns (Matt. 13:30). For those who are there signified by wheat, are signified in this place by the harvest. And those who are there by tares, are figured in this place by the clusters of the vine of the earth: and because Christ will appear otherwise to the righteous, and otherwise to the unrighteous, he is not unfittingly figured by two angels. For the just will see him in the same brightness as he is seen by angels and holy spirits in heavenly beatitude; but the wicked will see him in that form in which he was seen by men placed on earth; For the wicked shall be taken away, lest he see the glory of God (Isa. 26:10). Therefore he will appear pleasing to the righteous, terrible to the unrighteous; where also the first is described as having sat upon a white cloud, and having a golden crown on his head. For by the white cloud his immaculate body is signified: but by the golden crown the multitude of angels and saints, who will accompany him coming to judgment, is expressed. Similarly also one destruction is signified by two sickles; because the righteous will be destroyed in one way, the unrighteous in another: the destruction of the righteous is rather to be called redemption, as the Lord says: Look up, and lift up your heads; for your redemption draws near (Luke 21:28). It is rightly called redemption, because it frees from temporal evils, and leads to eternal joys. But the destruction of the wicked will be grievous both here, and after death more grievous; for perpetual damnation and mourning will remain in them. The righteous will receive a joyful death from the hope of eternal retribution: but the wicked, nervous from perpetual damnation, will be punished with a very sad death.

Another angel who came out of the temple cried with a loud voice to him who was sitting upon the cloud, saying: “Put in your sickle, and reap”; he points out the elect who labor on this earth, but by the temple from which he was seen to have come out, the Church is indicated. So he cried to him who was sitting upon the cloud, that he should put his sickle into the earth and reap; for holy men, seeing that the world lies in evil and trembling at its miseries, pray every day that the kingdom of God may come, in which, freed from all distress, they will receive their own bodies and will rejoice with the Lord forever. The dryness of the harvest, moreover, shows its ripeness; for just as the harvest grows until it is ripe, when it becomes dry, so too the number of the elect will grow until the end of the world, and their ripeness will then be brought to perfection when their number has been fulfilled. Similarly, the fourth angel who came out from the altar, who had power over the fire, has the same meaning as the second, who was seen to have come out of the temple; for he signifies the elect who labor in this life. The altar, however, signifies the Church, just as the temple does; and by the fire we can understand love, which the Holy Spirit, through spiritual kindling, nourishes in the hearts of the saints. The saints are therefore said to have power over this fire because they both possess love and are possessed by it. This angel then cried to the third angel who bore the second sickle, whom we have said signifies Christ, that he should harvest the clusters of the vineyard of the earth, by which the wicked are signified; for holy men, seeing every day humanity being deceived by demons, seeing also the wicked prosper, and the world always sliding into worse things, pray that the day of judgment may come, in which both the wickedness of the demons will perish together with its authors, and all transgression will reach its end together with the transgressors themselves, and everlasting justice will be brought in, which will be limited by no end. For the vineyard signifies the world, which has given birth to and nourished the whole multitude of the wicked; but the winepress, which we call hell [infernum], is therefore called the winepress of the wrath of God, because the vengeance of the wrath of God will be completed in it.

Revelation 14:20a And the winepress was trodden outside the city.

By the treading, we can understand the plunging of the wicked down into hell [infernum]; by the city, however, is signified the heavenly Jerusalem, which is made up of all the chosen. Therefore the winepress will be trodden outside this city, because the wicked will be separated from the fellowship of the righteous and thrust down into hell [infernum], as the Lord says in the Gospel: The angels will go out and will separate the evil from among the righteous, and will cast them into the furnace of fire. There will be weeping and gnashing of teeth there (Matt. 13:49–50).

Revelation 14:20b And blood came out of the lake as far as the horses’ bridles, for one thousand six hundred stadia.

By the blood, the punishment of the wicked is signified; by the distance of one thousand six hundred stadia, which the blood coming out of the lake occupied, false Christians are signified: for although this number is derived from the number four, it is nevertheless spread everywhere, always keeping the same number, and does not leap beyond its boundaries. Likewise, false Christians also receive in baptism the faith of Christ, which consists in the four books of the Gospel; but they abandon the narrow and constricted way, which is enclosed within the limits of the number four and which leads to life, and they walk along the broad and spacious way of this world, which leads to death (Matt. 7:13–14). Yet they do not depart from the number four, because although they do not have the works of Christianity, they do not lose the name of Christianity. Therefore blood came out of the lake and occupied one thousand six hundred stadia; because the punishment that will engulf the demons and all unbelievers will also ensnare the false Christians, who seemed to have faith but did not have the works of faith. By the horses, false shepherds of the Church are signified, who take up the office of governance not from love of God, but from love of the world; by the bridles with which the horses are guided, their false doctrine is signified. Indeed, false shepherds have doctrine in their mouth, so that they may not appear completely alien to their office; but following the example of the Pharisees, they say and do not do. And because they can teach nothing other than what the Gospel says, and in their preaching they announce to sinners what punishments they will suffer unless they are corrected, the blood reaches as far as the horses’ bridles, because the punishments which they threatened sinners with, unless they corrected themselves, they themselves will experience, since they were unwilling to abandon their own vices.

Chapter 15

Revelation 15:1 And I saw another sign in heaven, great and marvelous: seven angels having the seven last plagues, because in them the wrath of God is completed.

Just as above, before he came to the account of the seven angels blowing trumpets, he made it a point to mention them; so also here, before he comes to the account of the seven angels having bowls, he mentions them, in order to indicate that great mysteries are contained in their vision. But about these, in what follows, we will speak as the Lord grants.

Revelation 15:2-3a And I saw something like a sea of glass mixed with fire, and those who had conquered the beast, and its image, and the number of its name, standing on the sea of glass, holding the harps of God, and singing the song of Moses, the servant of God, and the song of the Lamb, saying.

What the sea of glass signifies, we explained above in the second vision (Rev. 4:6); but there it is said to be like crystal, whereas here it is mixed with fire. And because crystal is of outstanding brightness, and, when it has received the heat of the sun, it is accustomed to emit fire from itself, crystal there has the same meaning as fire here: for they signify the spiritual understanding that is found in the divine Scriptures. Therefore the sea of glass is mixed with fire, because divine Scripture is mixed with spiritual understandings. Therefore those who conquered the beast were seen standing upon the sea of glass, because the holy ones of God who are going to fight against Antichrist will plant their steps in the teachings of the divine Scriptures, and by no driving of the winds will they be able to be moved from their faith. By the harps, moreover, which they had, the mortification of the flesh is signified; but the song of Moses represents the Old Testament, the song of the Lamb shows the New. Therefore they sang the song of Moses and the song of the Lamb, because the holy ones will preach both the Old and the New Testament. Let us hear what sort of song this is.

Revelation 15:3b-4 Great and wonderful are your works, Lord God Almighty; just and true are your ways, King of the ages. Who will not fear you, Lord, and glorify your name? For you alone are holy; because all nations will come and adore in your presence, for your judgments have been revealed.

This song agrees with the Old and New Testaments; for seven blessings are found there, which can be fitted to the seven parts of which we spoke above (in chapters 5 and 7). The first blessing, indeed, is: Great and wonderful are your works, Lord God Almighty; which can be suited to the first time, in which heaven and earth were made, and all creatures, which are exceedingly wonderful. The second is: Just and true are your ways, King of the ages; which corresponds to the second part, in which all creatures that had the breath of life were, by the most just judgment of God, destroyed by the waters of the flood. The third is: Who will not fear you, Lord? which suits the third part, namely those to whom the Law was given, who carried out the commandments of the Law more from fear than from love. The fourth is: And will glorify your name; it is evident that this applies to the prophets, who glorified the name of God by their preaching and their writings. The fifth is: For you alone are holy; in the fifth part we placed the coming of Christ, in which our Lord, who alone is holy, consented to become man for the salvation of the human race, and to suffer and to die. The sixth is: Because all nations will come and adore in your presence; it is clear that after the ascension of the Lord all nations came to the faith of Christ, which we placed in the sixth place. The seventh is: For your judgments have been revealed; it is clear that this concerns the day of judgment, on which it will be shown that the judgments of God are just and true. From the condemnation of that judgment may the mercy and loving-kindness of the Lord and our Redeemer Jesus Christ consent to deliver us, who with the Father and the Holy Spirit lives and reigns as God for all ages of ages. Amen.

On the Fifth Vision

The fifth vision, whose meaning we have now reached, with the Lord’s help, to explain, for the most part agrees with the preceding vision, about whose understanding we have so far said what the grace of the Lord has consented to bestow (in chapter 8). For just as there seven angels were seen, to whom seven trumpets were given, so here likewise it is related that seven bowls were given to seven angels; and just as, at the sounding of the trumpet of the first angel, great hail struck the earth, so also, at the pouring out of the bowl of the first angel, men, who are often signified by “earth”, are said to have been struck with very grievous wounds. And just as, at the sounding of the trumpet of the second angel, a third part of the sea was turned into blood, so likewise, at the pouring out of the bowl of the second angel, the whole sea is described as having been turned into blood. The rest follow the same meaning; but how these agree with the former, we shall say in what follows. Therefore these have the same significance as those: they signify, namely, the preachers who were from the beginning and will be even to the end. But they are set forth twice because, as we have shown above, the things that were seen to have been done under the sounding of the trumpets of the first, second, third, and fourth angels refer to the elect. But the things that are reported to have been done under the sounding of the trumpets of the fifth, sixth, and seventh angels, to which also three woes are joined, wherein the persecutions of the Church are described, which she has suffered from heretics and pagans, and will suffer from Antichrist at the end of the world, refer on one side to the wicked, on the other to the elect; because the ungodly, from the evil which they inflict upon the righteous, acquire everlasting punishment, but the righteous, from the evil which they endure, gain for themselves everlasting glory. Therefore all the things that are described under the pouring out of the bowls of the seven angels refer to the wicked; therefore those first are said to have sounded the trumpets, by which the army of God was called to battle; but the following are reported to have poured out bowls full of the wrath of God upon the ungodly.

Revelation 15:5 After these things I saw, and behold, the temple of the tabernacle of testimony in heaven was opened.

In Exodus it is written that Moses made a tabernacle in the desert; (Exod. 25:9) but afterwards, when the people were already dwelling in the land of promise, a temple was built by Solomon. (1 Kings 6) By the desert, therefore, this present age is signified; by the tabernacle, which is moved from place to place, the present Church is signified, which passes daily from the desert of this age to heavenly blessedness, and goes from strength to strength, that it may see the God of gods in Zion (Ps. 83:8). Therefore also the curtains of the tabernacle, which were adorned with various colors, were covered with skins and with goat’s hair cloth, so as to show that mortification of the flesh and humility are the guardians of the virtues. But what is signified by the land of promise except the heavenly fatherland, which God has promised to those who love him? And what is prefigured by the temple that was built of the finest stones, except the heavenly Jerusalem, which is built up daily from living stones (1 Pet. 2:5)? Therefore, what he says, The temple of the tabernacle was opened, is as if it were said: The temple of the Church was opened; and what is the temple of the Church but the heavenly fatherland? In this temple the Psalmist longed to dwell when he said: One thing I have asked of the Lord, this will I seek: that I may dwell in the house of the Lord all the days of my life (Ps. 26:4). This temple, then, has been opened through Christ, because the entrance to the heavenly kingdom has been laid open through him to all the faithful.

Revelation 15:6 And the seven angels went out from the temple, having the seven plagues, clothed in a pure and white stone, and girded about the chest with golden belts.

If by the temple the heavenly homeland is signified, and by the seven angels the preachers are signified, how are they said to have gone out from the temple? Do the saints descend from that heavenly homeland to preach? But the holy preachers go out, as it were, from the heavenly homeland when they are sent by him who is the glory of the saints and from whom all their teaching proceeds. They are said to have had the seven plagues; and above they were said to have had the seven plagues, because in them the wrath of God has been completed. If they had the seven plagues of the wrath of God, how were they made saints? But the saints of God held the seven plagues in their power, not as their own punishment; for the Lord says in the Gospel: “In the regeneration, when the Son of Man shall sit on the throne of his majesty, you also shall sit on twelve thrones, judging the twelve tribes of Israel” (Matt. 19:28). Now by the seven plagues are signified the seven principal vices, from which all evils arise. Therefore the seven angels were seen to have the seven plagues, because the holy preachers on the day of judgment will so have the seven principal vices, that is, all workers of evil, in their power that, by their judgment, they will be punished with eternal torment. Truly, the wrath of God has been completed in the holy preachers, that is, through them; because by their judgment the wrath of God will be fulfilled. We can understand the seven plagues in another way. Above we divided the whole body of the wicked into seven parts (in chapter 1); therefore the seven plagues are the sins that more suitably belong to each part. For those who were before the flood, and deceived the people of God by union with women (Gen. 6:2), will be judged in one way; those who after the flood invented idolatry and various errors, by which the human race was deceived, in another way; those who were under the Law and did not keep the commandments of God, in another way; the false prophets who deceived the people of God, in another way; the wicked Jews who killed the Lord and persecuted his apostles, in another way; the persecutors of the Church who tried to extinguish the faith of Christ, in another way; the ministers of Antichrist who are going to abandon Christ and follow Antichrist, in another way. Therefore the saints of God will have these plagues in their power, because each will, by his own judgment, condemn to eternal punishment those who lived in his own time and despised his warnings. By the pure and white stone, in which the seven angels were seen clothed, Christ is signified, of whom the Apostle says: “All you who have been baptized in Christ have put on Christ” (Gal. 3:27). But by the golden belts with which they are said to be girded around the chest, the wisdom of the preachers is signified, which so encloses the hearts of the saints within itself that it does not allow them to wander outside it through various errors.

Revelation 15:7 And one of the four living creatures gave to the seven angels seven golden bowls, full of the wrath of God who lives for ever and ever.

Although the four living creatures signify the four evangelists, yet both together and individually they signify Christ; but since conciseness does not allow us to say how each one signifies Christ, let us understand Christ through this one living creature. Now the bowls are the hearts of the saints, which, on account of their wisdom, are rightly called golden. Therefore one of the four living creatures gave the seven angels seven golden bowls, because Christ filled the hearts of his saints with wisdom. But if they are filled with wisdom, how are they said to be full of the wrath of God? The Lord says in the Gospel about the wicked Jews: “If I had not come and spoken to them, they would not have sin” (Jn. 15:22). Did the Jews not have sin before the coming of Christ? They were certainly sinners before, but they did not have the sin of unbelief, the sin of blasphemy by which they called him Beelzebul, the sin of murder by which they crucified him, and many other crimes which they drew from his coming. In the bowl, however, of Christ, in which the life of the world was contained, the Jews, through the demands of their own faults, found death. Therefore the bowls of the seven angels were full of the wrath of God, because, as far as the wicked are concerned, the preaching of the saints contained within itself the wrath of God; for by the fact that they despised their preaching, they suffered the wrath of God.

Revelation 15:8a And the temple was filled with smoke from the majesty of God and from His power.

We said above that by “temple” the heavenly homeland is signified; but if smoke is inside a house, it darkens the eyes of those looking in from outside, so that they cannot perfectly see the things that are done in the house. Therefore the temple of the heavenly homeland is now full of smoke, because the elect, placed in this life, when they lift the eyes of their mind to contemplate heavenly things, are darkened by a certain gloom which they cannot pass beyond; this darkening he calls “smoke.” Paul endured this gloom when he said: “Now we see through a mirror, in a mystery; but then face to face.” And again: “Now I know in part, but then I shall know even as I am known” (1 Cor. 13:12). And it must be noted that the elect suffer this dullness as long as they are placed in this life; but when they have passed to that heavenly blessedness, it will be taken away from the eyes of their mind. And so it follows:

Revelation 15:8b And no one could enter into the temple, until the seven plagues of the seven angels were finished.

Do the righteous not enter into that heavenly blessedness every day? But let us understand that this was said about the entry of bodies; for in that heavenly blessedness the righteous do not enter with their bodies until the seven plagues of the seven angels are finished. We spoke above about the seven plagues; but the last plague will be when Antichrist, by his errors, will infect humanity. When the seven plagues have ended, for the examination of which the day of judgment will take place, the saints will receive their bodies, and in the temple of the heavenly homeland they will dwell with the Lord without end. Of this dwelling the Psalmist says: “Blessed are they who dwell in your house, O Lord; they shall praise you for ages of ages” (Ps. 83:5).

Chapter 16

Revelation 16:1 And I heard a great voice from the temple, saying to the seven angels: Go, and pour out the seven bowls of the wrath of God upon the earth.

The voice from the temple is the voice of the Lord commanding the preachers to proclaim the word of God not only to the elect, but also to the wicked, so that the former may have something by which they are corrected, and the latter something by which they are condemned more severely. By “earth,” moreover, sinners are indicated. Therefore the preachers pour out their bowls upon the earth when they announce the word of God to sinners.

Revelation 16:2 And the first went and poured out his bowl upon the earth, and there came a grievous and very evil sore upon the men who had the mark of the beast, and upon those who worshiped its image.

The first angel signifies the preachers who were before the Law, just as the one who in the preceding vision was seen as the first to sound the trumpet (Rev. 8:7). But there, by the third part of the earth that was burned, the elect are signified; here, however, by those who suffered the grievous and very evil sore, the wicked are signified. The angel therefore poured out his bowl upon the earth, because the holy men who were before the Law poured out words of rebuke upon the wicked, in order to correct them; but a grievous and very evil sore came upon them, because, made worse by the preaching of the saints, they despised the commandments of God. For contempt of the commandments of God is indeed a grievous and very evil sore. But if the grievous and very evil sore refers only to the wicked, and only those suffered it who had the mark of the beast, we must ask what the mark of the beast is. If we should wish to understand by the mark of the beast pride, on the ground that because of it the Devil fell from heaven, we must notice that many of the elect have had the wound of pride, but have been healed by the remedy of humility. There is, however, another very evil vice, which always shuts out from itself the remedy of repentance; for we know that the Devil is incorrigible, so that he can in no way be corrected from his iniquities. Therefore whoever is incorrigible does not seek the remedy of repentance, just as the Devil is estranged from this remedy. Therefore those who could not be called back from their crimes by the teaching of the holy Fathers had the mark of the Devil, who continues hopelessly without repentance. But because in our time many suffer from this vice, it is necessary that we say something about its degree; for there are some so incorrigible that, although they know their own proclamation is wrong, they can in no way be drawn back from it, fearing to be defeated by men; and, lest they be disproved, they strive to defend their falsehood with lies and perjuries and burning quarrels. And while they fear to be defeated by men, they are defeated by the Devil. All other vices, then, are healed by the remedy of repentance; but this alone is that which excludes this saving remedy from itself, namely, lest by confession and repentance they should seem to have been defeated. And because, in order to appear to have prevailed, they praise their own evil, that prophetic saying is fulfilled in them: “Woe to you who call evil good, and good evil; who put darkness for light, and light for darkness” (Isa. 5:20)! It is said that there is a certain bird which conquers and eats the serpent, but shortly afterward is killed by its venom; in the same way, these men rejoice that they have conquered their neighbors, but such a victory is the destroyer of their souls. Whoever therefore are such people bear the mark of the Devil. By the image of the beast, however, we can understand the vices, since he himself is the author of all evils. Whoever, therefore, serves vices worships the image of the beast; for Paul also says that the belly was the god of false preachers: “Whose god,” he says, “is their belly, and their glory is in their shame; who set their minds on earthly things” (Philip. 3:19). Likewise he called avarice “idolatrous slavery” (Col. 3:5).

Revelation 16:3 And the second poured out his bowl into the sea, and it became blood like that of a dead person, and every living soul died in the sea.

This angel signifies the teachers of the Law, just as the one who in the prior vision was seen to have sounded the second trumpet (Rev. 8:8-9); but there it is said that a third part of the sea was turned into blood, by which we said the elect were signified; here, however, it is read that the whole sea was turned into blood. The punishment for criminal wickedness according to the Law was the shedding of blood. (Gen. 9:5) Therefore, by the sea are signified the transgressors of the Law; which was turned into blood because they received eternal punishment for their contempt of the commandments of God. And every living soul died in the sea; because many of those who were in the sea, that is, of the number of the wicked, who are signified by the sea, and who before the reception of the Law seemed to be alive, that is, righteous, once the Law was received, it was proved by the transgression of the Law that they had been unrighteous. There are also now many in the Church who, before they rise to ecclesiastical or secular honors, appear holy and righteous; but once they have received honors, when they pervert the gift of God to evil, it is clear to all that they were dead and not alive.

Revelation 16:4 And the third poured out his bowl upon the rivers and upon the springs of waters, and they became blood.

This angel signifies the prophets, while by the rivers and the springs of waters the twelve tribes with their families are signified; for even in the preceding vision we said that by the third angel sounding the trumpet the prophets were signified. But there it is read that the third part of the rivers and of the springs was turned into wormwood, (Rev. 8:10-11) by which, as we said, those were signified who through the teaching of the prophets took on the bitterness of repentance and by it merited to be saved; here, however, in the pouring out of the bowl of the third angel, the rivers and springs are read to have been turned into blood. Therefore the angel poured out his bowl upon the rivers and springs of waters when the holy prophets announced the word of God to the wicked people; but from the preaching of the saints they became far worse, and not only despised the commandments of God, but also killed the ministers of the word of God themselves. They were turned into blood indeed, because they brought everlasting death upon themselves.

Revelation 16:5-6 And I heard the fourth angel saying: You are just, who are, and who were, holy, because you have judged these things; for they poured out the blood of the saints and of the prophets, and you have given them blood to drink, which they are worthy of.

By the fourth angel we can understand the angel of that people, who, seeing by the just judgment of God that the wicked people have been condemned, praises the most just sentence of almighty God. And because they poured the blood of the saints and of the prophets, they drank blood; because for the temporal death which they inflicted upon the righteous, they received eternal death.

Revelation 16:7 And I also heard the altar saying: Lord God almighty, true and just are your judgments.

By “the altar” we can understand the holy men who lived at that time, who praised the just judgment of God because He brought most just condemnation upon the wicked people, just as we read that Daniel and the three youths did. (Dan. 3)

Revelation 16:8-9 And the fourth poured out his bowl upon the sun, and it was granted to it to scorch men with heat and with fire. And men were scorched with great heat, and they blasphemed the name of God who has power over these plagues; and they did not repent so as to give Him glory.

The fourth angel signifies Christ and His apostles, and the other preachers as well, just as we said that the fourth angel in the earlier vision had signified them; (Rev. 8:12) but there the people of the Jews are represented by the sun, and by the third part of the sun and the moon and the stars the believers who from that people came to faith in Christ are indicated. Therefore the fourth angel poured out his bowl upon the sun, because Christ and His apostles openly foretold that the wicked Jews would be destroyed by the Romans. (Luke 19:41–44, 21:20–24) For just as any servant receives a sword from his master, not that he himself might be slain by it, but that he might slay the guilty, so also the Romans received the outpouring of the bowl in which the wrath of God was contained, not in order that they themselves should perish in it, but that they should pour it out upon the Jews to avenge the blood of Christ; where it also follows: Because it was granted to it to afflict men with heat and with fire. We can understand by “heat” the siege, and by “fire” famine and the sword, by which almost all the Jews were destroyed. It was therefore granted to the sun to afflict men with heat and with fire, because it was granted by God to the Romans that, after tormenting the wicked Jews with various calamities, they should kill some of them and tear others up from their own land. And men were scorched with great heat. Whoever wishes to know how great that heat was with which the Romans afflicted the Jews, let him read Josephus, and he will find that no nation in all the kingdoms of the earth perished more cruelly (Josephus, The Jewish War, 1.4). And they blasphemed the name of God who has power over these plagues. So wicked was the nation of the Jews that, although they knew they were suffering unheard‑of evils for their crimes, not only did they not cease from their blasphemies against Christ, but in their afflictions they spewed forth even far worse blasphemies against God.
