Where it also follows: And they did not repent so as to give Him glory; for they would have given glory to God if, repenting of their sins, they had believed in Christ and kept His commandments.

Revelation 16:10-11 And the fifth poured out his bowl upon the throne of the beast, and his kingdom was made dark; and they chewed their tongues for pain, and they blasphemed the God of heaven because of their pains and their wounds, and they did not do penance for their deeds.

The fifth angel signifies the orthodox fathers who fought against the heretics, just as we said that the one who, in the earlier vision, sounded the fifth trumpet signified them. (Rev. 9:1-12) And as there the heretics were represented by the locusts, so here the heretics are represented by the throne of the beast; and the beast signifies the Devil. Therefore, with good reason the heretics are called the throne of the beast, because the Devil dwells in their hearts. So the fifth angel poured out his bowl upon the throne of the beast, because holy men, by uncovering the errors of the heretics, strove to make clear what punishment was awaiting for them. And his kingdom was made dark, because to those to whom the teaching of the heretics had seemed bright, it was shown through the teaching of the holy Fathers to be worthless and dark. They chewed their tongues for pain, because each condemned the errors of the others; for neither did Arius follow Eunomius or Sabellius or any of the others, nor did they follow him; but each, wishing to establish his own heresy, condemned the errors of the rest. They are also said to have blasphemed God because of their pains and their wounds; for the heretics grieved over the fact that they were always refuted by catholic men; and their wounds were the individual errors of each. Indeed, the heretics blasphemed God because of their pains, because the more they were overcome by catholic men, the greater blasphemies they devised in order to refute them; nonetheless, their own errors, which they professed, drove them on to blaspheme God. They are also said not to have done penance; for you will scarcely find any among the inventors of heresies who has done penance for his errors.

Revelation 16:12 And the sixth poured out his bowl upon the great river Euphrates, and dried up its water, so that a way might be prepared for the kings from the rising of the sun.

This sixth angel has the same meaning as the one who seemed to have sounded the sixth trumpet; (Rev. 9:13) for he signifies the holy martyrs. And those who are there represented by the horsemen, by whom a third part of men is described as having been killed, are in this passage themselves represented by the great river Euphrates, that is, the persecutors of the Church of God. Therefore the angel poured out his bowl upon the river Euphrates, because the condemnation that was about to come upon the persecutors of the Church was foretold to those persecutors without any hesitation. By the water, indeed, the persecution itself is signified; but he dried up its water, because the holy martyrs persevered in preaching the word of God until every persecution came to an end, some of the persecutors being plunged into hell [infernum], and some submitting themselves to the Christian faith. By the kings we can understand the multitude of the nations that have flocked to the faith of Christ. Therefore a way was prepared for the kings when the water was dried up, because, when the persecutions had ended, free opportunity was given to all nations to come to the faith of Christ. But how are they said to have come from the rising of the sun, that is, from Christ, who is signified by the sun, when they did not come from Christ but to Christ, having abandoned the Devil? Unless perhaps we were to understand the Devil by the sun, so that they came from him to Christ; but it is better to understand Christ by the sun, because of that prophetic saying: “But for you who fear my name, there shall arise the sun of righteousness with healing in his wings” (Mal. 4:2). From the rising of the sun, then, that is, from Christ, they come, because it is by his inspiring and urging that they come to him; it is his gift both to will and to accomplish what is good, as he himself said: “Without me you can do nothing” (Jn. 15:5). From Christ, therefore, come all who come to Christ, because he grants that they may come, and he himself receives those who come.

Revelation 16:13-14a And I saw from the mouth of the dragon and from the mouth of the beast and from the mouth of the false prophet three unclean spirits coming out in the form of frogs; for they are spirits of demons working signs.

After describing the calling of the Gentiles, how they came to the faith of Christ, he makes mention of Antichrist, who is to come near the end of the world. Now the three unclean spirits signifies the disciples of Antichrist, who are to preach him throughout the whole world: and although they will be men, they are called unclean spirits and spirits of demons, because demons will dwell in them and will speak through their mouths; they were seen to have come out of the mouth of Antichrist and out of the mouth of his false prophet, because through their teaching they will become sons of the Devil; they were also seen to have come out of the mouth of the dragon, because through the mouth of Antichrist the Devil will speak. Nonetheless they are rightly likened to frogs, which are unclean creeping animals that live in the mud; for just as frogs remain in filthy waters, so too the disciples of Antichrist will easily deceive those who have not feared to be defiled by various vices and crimes. For the croaking of frogs, being hoarse and disgusting, signifies their most wicked preaching, full of blasphemies.

Revelation 16:14b And they will go forth to the kings of the whole earth to gather them together for battle on the great day of God Almighty.

By the kings of the earth are signified not only kings, but also peoples; now the disciples of Antichrist will gather them together for battle for the great day of God Almighty, not that they would dare to fight against God on the day of judgment, by whom they will be judged so terribly, but they will gather them for battle to persecute the Church, so that on the great day of God Almighty they may be condemned to everlasting punishment. And because, when the persecution of Antichrist is ended, the Lord will come shortly to judgment, he adds:

Revelation 16:15a And behold, I come like a thief.

The Day of the Lord, as the Apostle says, will come like a thief in the night; for when they shall say, “Peace and security,” then sudden destruction will come upon them. (1 Thess. 5:2–3)

Revelation 16:15b Blessed is he who keeps watch and keeps his garments, so that he may not walk naked and they see his shame.

The wicked, who rest in the pleasures of their flesh as if in a certain softness of a bed, and who are unwilling to foresee the evils that follow, are not inappropriately said to be asleep. The Apostle warns them, saying: “Awake, you who sleep, and rise from the dead, and Christ will enlighten you” (Eph. 5:14). But the righteous, who strive to foresee the evils that are to come, who extinguish bodily pleasures in themselves, who arm themselves with the weapons of virtues so that they may be able to fight unceasingly against visible and invisible enemies, are fittingly said to keep watch, and therefore they are blessed. Moreover, the garments of our souls are good works: the keeping of the garments is the keeping of the commandments of God. We therefore keep our garments if we fulfill by our deeds what God has commanded. But he is naked who is devoid of good works. The wicked will walk naked when, on the day of judgment, they are led forth empty of good works; and therefore their shame will be seen, because their crimes will be revealed both to angels and to human beings.

Revelation 16:16 And he will gather them together into the place which in Hebrew is called Armageddon.

Armageddon is interpreted as “rounded mountain” or “rising again to former things”; and both interpretations can be applied to the Day of Judgment. For it is rightly called a rounded mountain, because at that time the whole world will be destroyed by balls of fire; and likewise it is rightly called a rising again to former things, because by rising again all human beings will be restored to their former state.

Revelation 16:17a And the seventh poured out his bowl into the air.

By this seventh angel the holy preachers who are to be in the times of Antichrist are signified; just as we have said that he also is meant who above (Rev. 11:15) is read to have sounded the seventh trumpet. By the air, moreover, vain men are signified. Therefore the angel poured out his bowl into the air, because the holy preachers will declare to vain and wicked men that they are to be condemned to everlasting punishment.

Revelation 16:17b And a great voice went out from the temple from the throne, saying.

The great voice is the voice of the holy preachers; by the temple the Church is understood. So a voice went out from the temple, because from the Church the voice of holy preaching proceeds; which is also said to have gone out from the throne, because the Church of God is the throne of God, and, sitting in it, he rests. But what this voice says he makes clear by adding:

Revelation 16:17c It is done.

That is, the end of the world is at hand, in which all things that have been foretold by the Lord and by the saints will be fulfilled.

Revelation 16:18 And there were flashes of lightning and voices and thunders, and a great earthquake occurred, such as had never been since human beings were upon the earth, such an earthquake, so great.

By the flashes of lightning are signified the miracles that God will perform through his saints; for we read above that Elijah and Enoch will work many signs. (Rev. 11:5–6) By the voices is meant the preaching of the saints; and by the thunders the terror of eternal fire is expressed. But the earthquake often signifies the movement of hearts. For some will be moved from evil to good, like the Jews who will come to believe in Christ; others will be moved from good to evil, who will abandon the faith of Christ and follow the errors of Antichrist.

Revelation 16:19a And the great city was divided into three parts, and the cities of the nations fell.

The great city is the city of the Devil, that is, the whole multitude of the wicked: by the three parts into which it is divided, the Jews who refused to believe in Christ, and the pagans, and the false Christians are indicated. But when he says: And the cities of the nations fell, this seems to me to refer to the day of judgment. The cities of the nations will then fall when, by that fire which will go before the face of the Lord as he comes to judgment, all nations will be destroyed, as the Psalmist says: A fire shall go before him and shall burn up his enemies round about (Ps. 96:3).

Revelation 16:19b And great Babylon came into remembrance before God, to give her the cup of the wine of the indignation of His wrath.

How is Babylon said to have come into remembrance before God, with whom nothing can be added to or taken away from His remembrance; in whose sight the present, the past, and the future alike stand? But he has put “remembrance” in place of “time.” Babylon therefore will come into remembrance before God when that time comes in which He has determined to judge the world and to render to each according to his work. (Rom. 2:6) Now the wine of the indignation of the wrath of God is eternal punishment; because, when the judgment is finished, all the wicked will be judged together with the author of sin, the Devil.

Revelation 16:20 And every island fled, and the mountains were not found.

The mountains and the islands have the same meaning as the cities of the nations, which a little above are said to have fallen; for by the islands the nations are indicated, and by the mountains their rulers. They are said to flee when they will be destroyed by fire on the day of judgment.

Revelation 16:21a And great hail, like a talent, fell from heaven upon men.

In the Gospel it is written that a certain householder, setting out on a journey, gave to one of his servants five talents, to another two, and to another one. But the first and the second, by trading with the talents which they had received from the lord, doubled them; the third dug in the ground and hid his lord’s money (Matt. 25:15). Whatever temporal goods therefore, or whatever skills we have received from the Lord, will be reckoned to us as the reception of talents: the righteous, therefore, who are signified by the first and second servant, double their talents, since they spend the temporal goods which they receive from the Lord in good works, and also urge whomever they can toward doing good; but the wicked, who are signified by the third servant, hide their talents in the earth, because from the temporal goods which they have received from the Lord they nourish vices rather than virtues. By the hail, which crushes and destroys the things that spring from the earth, is signified the vengeance of almighty God, by which he will crush the wicked and sink them into hell [infernum]; which is said to be like a talent, because he will punish each one according to the quality of his talent; and the more someone has sinned, the greater torments he will be forced to endure. But what follows is very obscure:

Revelation 16:21b And men blasphemed God because of the plague of the hail; for it was made exceedingly great.

For who in that judgment, so strict and so just, will dare to blaspheme God? But they are said to blaspheme, because the blasphemies of the wicked, which they now hide in their heart, will be revealed to all.

Chapter 17

Revelation 17:1a And one of the seven angels, who had the seven bowls, came and spoke with me, saying:

Just as above, under the sounding of the trumpet of the seventh angel, briefly grasping the day of judgment, he returned to the things he had omitted; and, describing the holy Church under the appearance of a woman who was clothed with the sun and had the moon under her feet, (Rev. 12) he adds the sufferings which she has endured from the Devil, and which she is going to endure from Antichrist at the end of the world. So also here, under the pouring out of the bowl of the seventh angel, briefly touching on the day of judgment, he returns to what he left out, and, describing under the appearance of a harlot woman the city of the Devil, he makes clear in what follows what punishments she is going to suffer for her crimes. But the angel who has the bowl signifies the holy preachers; and John holds the figure of all the faithful; therefore it is added:

Revelation 17:1b-2 Come, I will show you the condemnation of the great harlot who sits upon many waters, with whom the kings of the earth have committed fornication, and all who dwell on the earth have been made drunk with the wine of her prostitution.

This harlot in some places specifically represents Rome, which at that time was persecuting the Church of God; but in some passages she more generally signifies the city of the Devil, that is, the whole body of the wicked. Therefore the angel shows John the condemnation of the great harlot, because holy preachers, both by words and by writings, show the faithful what punishments the wicked suffer for their crimes, so that, terrified by their punishments, people may abstain from sins. She is said to sit upon many waters, because the city of the Devil is built from the multitude of nations, which are signified by the waters. The kings of the earth are said to have committed fornication with her, because they have increased rather than destroyed the crimes of Babylon. And all who dwell on the earth have been made drunk with the wine of her prostitution. The wine of prostitution is the various errors, the various crimes, of the wicked city; for just as a harlot prostitutes herself to all who wish to commit fornication with her, so that she is defiled by their impurity, so also all the wicked are subjected to demons, when, obeying their perverse suggestions, they cast themselves into every kind of crime, just as the prophet speaks to Jerusalem, saying: “You have made your body like the ground, and like a road for those who pass by” (Isa. 51:23). By “inhabitants of the earth” he calls all the wicked; and the drunkenness is the nastiness of Babylon, because just as a drunkard is filled with wine until he becomes alienated from his own mind, so too the wicked so fill themselves with all wickedness that, being alienated from their own mind, they cast away from themselves the fear of God and all goodness, as blessed Job says, that they “drink iniquity like water” (Job 15:16).

Revelation 17:3 And he carried me away in the spirit into the wilderness, and I saw a woman sitting upon a scarlet beast, full of names of blasphemy, having seven heads and ten horns.

By “wilderness” the whole multitude of the wicked is indicated, because they have abandoned God, and therefore have been abandoned by God. Therefore the harlot woman is found in the wilderness, because from the multitude of the unrighteous the city of the Devil is made up. The beast, however, as he himself explains in what follows, signifies the Devil; scarlet has the color of blood, and by blood death is often signified. Therefore the Devil is “bloodstained,” because he is the author of death and of all destruction. This beast is said to be full of names of blasphemy, because the Devil himself is the author of all blasphemies. But what the seven heads and the ten horns are, the angel explains in what follows.

Revelation 17:4 And the woman was clothed in purple and scarlet, and adorned with gold and precious stone and pearls, having a golden cup in her hand, full of abominations and the uncleanness of her fornication.

Purple is dyed with blood, and scarlet has the color of blood, and they are royal garments, through which worldly power is signified; but through blood death is often signified. Therefore purple and scarlet have the appearance of blood, because worldly power is deadly and brings eternal danger to those who embrace it more than heavenly glory. We can also understand by bloodstained garments the works of the wicked, for which they will be condemned to everlasting death. And when it then says: “And adorned with gold”; by gold wisdom is often signified. The woman therefore was adorned with gold, because she possessed the wisdom of this world, which is foolishness before God. By precious stone and pearls, moreover, worldly eloquence is signified; by the golden cup, which she is said to have held in her hand, the teaching of philosophers and poets is signified; which is said to be full of abominations and the uncleanness of her fornication, because the teaching of the philosophers and poets was full of lies and of every error created to deceive the human race.

Revelation 17:5 And on her forehead a name was written: Mystery, great Babylon, the mother of fornications and of the abominations of the earth.

By the forehead and the face a person is recognized; likewise the ungodly are understood from their works, as Solomon says: A child is known by his pursuits (Prov. 20:11). Therefore by the forehead and the face the works of the ungodly are indicated, because from their works it is understood that they are children of the Devil and citizens of the ungodly city.

Revelation 17:6a And I saw the woman drunk with the blood of the saints, and with the blood of the martyrs of Jesus.

Has Babylon drunk the blood of the saints? But she was drunk, that is, completely separated from all spiritual understanding; she was drunk, because the vengeance for the blood of the saints had made her so damnable, and so distant from the mercy of God, that she was not worthy to be able in any way to take thought for her own salvation.

Revelation 17:6b And I marveled when I saw her, with great amazement.

The faithful are amazed when they see the wicked so far removed from the fear of God that, without any trembling, they pile many evils upon the innocent, and plunge themselves into every vice, knowing that God leaves no crime unpunished, unless it has first been wiped away by repentance and the other remedies by which sins are healed.

Revelation 17:7 And the angel said to me: Why do you marvel? I will tell you the mystery of the woman, and of the beast that carries her, which has seven heads and ten horns.

The beast in this place has the same meaning as we said above that the dragon had: for it signifies the Devil. What the seven heads and the ten horns are, he himself, as we said, explains in what follows.

Revelation 17:8a The beast that you saw was, and is not, and is about to come up from the abyss, and will go to destruction.

The beast, that is, the Devil, existed before the coming of Christ, possessing humanity; he is not now, that is, he does not possess humanity as before, because through Christ he has been driven out from the hearts of the chosen. But he is about to come up from the abyss, because in the times of Antichrist, Satan will be released from his prison, and will come out and deceive all nations, as the following passages show more fully; and he will go to destruction, because together with all the wicked whom he has deceived, and with all his helpers, he will be sentenced to eternal punishment.

Revelation 17:8b And the inhabitants of the earth will marvel, whose names are not written in the book of life from the foundation of the world, when they see the beast which was, and is not.

By inhabitants of the earth we must understand those who will be lovers of the earth more than of God, whom Antichrist will easily deceive. Therefore the wicked will marvel, whose names are not written in the book of life, that is, who are not predestined to eternal life: when they see the beast, that is, the Devil working signs through Antichrist, and in Antichrist they worship the Devil.

Revelation 17:9a And here is the meaning, for the one who has wisdom.

That is, in what follows there is great obscurity, and the understanding must be sharpened greatly to grasp it. And let us ask the Lord to open this unclear matter to us.

Revelation 17:9b-10 The seven heads are seven mountains upon which the woman sits; and they are seven kings. Five have fallen, one is, the other has not yet come; and when he comes, he must remain for a short time.

We said above that by the seven heads of the dragon those wicked ones are indicated through whom the Devil deceived humanity, (Rev. 12:3-4) whom we also divided into seven parts, just like the rest of the multitude of the wicked. And because this beast of which we speak has the same meaning as we said the dragon has; namely, they signify the Devil: the seven heads of the beast have the same meaning as the seven heads of the dragon which we said it has. These are also called mountains, because those who deceived others were higher in crimes than those who were deceived by them, just as it says in what follows about Babylon: Because her sins have reached as far as heaven (Rev. 18:5). They are likewise called seven kings, because they provided leadership to the others as they went to destruction. Upon these mountains therefore the harlot is said to sit, because from them the city of the Devil has been shaped in all errors and crimes. For just as the apostles are called the foundations of the Church, so equally the inventors of errors are the foundations of the city of the Devil. From these heads, or mountains, or kings, five have fallen, one was, the other had not yet come. The first head of the Devil had fallen, because those who lived before the flood, through whom those who were called sons of God were deceived, had gone down into the sewer of hell [inferni] (Gen. 6:2). Likewise, the second, who lived after the flood up to the Law, had sunk and were held in that same pit of destruction. The third also had fallen, those who persuaded the people of God, to whom the Law was given, to worship idols. Likewise, the false prophets, who are placed in the fourth place, had already fallen into the ruin of their destruction, because they had been deceivers of the people of God. But the wicked Jews, who killed the Lord and persecuted his apostles, who are placed in the fifth place, at the time when this vision was shown to John, had already been devastated by the Romans. Five heads of the beast had therefore already fallen, which are called mountains and kings, because all who came before, and the wicked Jews who are placed in the fifth place, were already paying the penalties of their destruction. One was, because the pagans, that is, those who persecuted the Church of God, and who are placed in the sixth place, at that time were persecuting the Church of God; for which reason this John was being held in exile on the island of Patmos. The other had not yet come, because Antichrist, who is placed in the seventh place, had not yet come; and when he comes, he must remain for a short time, because the Lord Jesus will kill him with the breath of his mouth and will destroy him by the brightness of his coming, as he himself says in the Gospel: And unless the Lord had shortened those days, that is, the days of the persecution of Antichrist, no flesh would be saved (Mark 13:20); by “all flesh” indicating only the elect.

Revelation 17:11 And the beast which was, and is not, is itself also the eighth, and is of the seven; and it goes into destruction.

And the beast, that is, the Devil, who before the coming of Christ held dominion over the human race, and now does not hold it so subject as he did then, is that same eighth beast. For there are seven parts of all the wicked, but the eighth part of them is the Devil with the other demons. It is “of the seven,” because the demons exist from the company of the wicked; and just as they were the authors of their destruction, so they will also be their companions in torments. Therefore it also follows: And it goes into destruction. The things that follow, up to the place where the Angel is seen to have descended from heaven, properly refer to the destruction of the Roman Empire.

Revelation 17:12 And the ten horns that you saw are ten kings, who have not yet received a kingdom; but they will receive authority as kings for one hour after the beast.

Four beasts were shown to Daniel in a vision (Dan. 7:3), through which four kingdoms were represented: moreover the fourth beast, which was strong and terrible, through which the Romans are indicated, is described as having had ten horns, through which the kingdoms that destroyed the Roman Empire are signified, as the holy Jerome15 explains, following the claim of certain authorities. Therefore the ten horns in this passage have the same meaning that those are said to have had; indeed, they signify the kingdoms by which the Roman Empire was destroyed. For they first took away a part of Asia by themselves: later the Saracens subdued the whole of it: the Vandals claimed Africa for themselves, the Goths Spain, the Lombards Italy, the Burgundians Gaul, the Franks Germany, the Huns Pannonia; moreover the Alans and the Suebi laid waste many places that were subject to their rule. Therefore these kingdoms, at the time when this vision was shown to John, had not yet received authority: but they received authority as kings for one hour, because the power of each of these peoples lasted only a short time. They received the kingdom after the beast, because after the kingdom of the Devil was destroyed through Christ, these peoples received godless power.

Revelation 17:13 They will have one plan; they have handed over their strength and their power to the beast.

They had one plan, that is, one will in evil. They handed over their strength and their power to the beast, because their strength and power were demonic.

Revelation 17:14a They will fight with the Lamb, and the Lamb will conquer them.

They fought with the Lamb, who persecuted the Church of God and killed the people of God; but the Lamb will conquer them, because we know that these nations, except for a few, have now accepted the yoke of the faith of Christ.

Revelation 17:14b For he is King of kings and Lord of lords, and those who are with him are called, chosen, and faithful.

Those who are kings and rulers are themselves the chosen and the faithful. Christ, therefore, is King of kings and Lord of lords, because he is the King and Lord of all the saints; and those who are with him are called, chosen, and faithful, that is, the saints, who are called to this end, that they may remain with him forever, are rightly called chosen and faithful. For even the wicked are called, as the Lord says: Many are called, but few are chosen (Matt. 20:16). But the wicked are called to this end, that they may be without excuse; the chosen, however, that they may endure with him forever.

Revelation 17:15 The waters that you saw, where the prostitute sits, are peoples and tribes and languages.

By the waters are signified all the nations that the Romans had subjected to themselves.16

Revelation 17:16a And the ten horns that you saw on the beast: these will hate the harlot.

He calls Rome the harlot, because, in defending false gods, she persecuted God, her Creator. Therefore the ten horns hated the harlot when the nations signified by the ten horns took up arms to destroy the Roman Empire.

Revelation 17:16b And they will make her desolate and naked.

They made her desolate and naked when they took away all the nations from her power.

Revelation 17:16c And they devoured her flesh.

Since delights nourish the flesh, we can understand by “flesh” the riches of all the nations. They devoured her flesh when they took the wealth of all the nations, which was being brought to Rome, and turned it to their own use.

Revelation 17:16d And they will burn her with fire.

We know that many cities, villages, and fortresses that were subject to the Roman Empire were burned by these nations.

Revelation 17:17 For God indeed put it into their hearts to do what is pleasing to him: to give their kingdom to the beast, until the words of God are fulfilled.

God put it into their hearts, that is, he allowed them to do what is pleasing to him and what has been foretold by his prophets and apostles, who predicted that the power of the Roman Empire would be destroyed by these nations. And they gave their kingdom to the beast until the words of God were fulfilled; because, by the permission of the most just God, they followed the suggestions of the Devil in carrying out crimes until the words of God were fulfilled, that is, until the things foretold by God and his saints are brought to completion.

Revelation 17:18 And the woman whom you saw is the great city, which has power over all the kings of the earth.

This is clear; for we know that the Romans at that time ruled over all the kings of the earth. But someone will say: How are the Romans indicated by the woman who is a prostitute, when in those times, in which these peoples brought many disasters upon humanity, the Romans were Christians? To this we reply that by the woman who is a prostitute are not understood the elect who were from that same people and who despised the glory of the world out of love for heavenly glory, but the wicked, whether they were pagans or Christians. Therefore, the power of those who with wicked authority oppressed humanity was destroyed by those peoples.

Chapter 18

Revelation 18:1 After these things I saw another angel coming down from heaven, having great power, and the earth was illuminated by his glory.

The things that follow refer in general to the ungodly city, that is, to all the wicked. And in order to show more fully the overthrow of Babylon, he returns to its first ruin, by which it was overthrown through Christ. For this angel signifies Christ, who consented to come down from heaven to earth, so that he might lift humans up to heavenly things. He is said to have great power because he has the same power as the Father, since in all things he is equal to the Father. The earth was illuminated by his glory, because through the teaching of his faith humanity has been snatched from the darkness of ignorance and unbelief.

Revelation 18:2a And he cried out in a loud voice, saying:

The voice of Christ is the teaching of the Gospel. But what this voice cried out he makes clear by adding:

Revelation 18:2b Babylon the great has fallen, has fallen.

To have said something, for God, is to have done it; for he said that Babylon had fallen, because he himself brought about its fall. It is said to have fallen twice, because it fell the first time when, through the teaching of the Gospel, it lost a very great multitude of the elect, who, cut off from it through faith in Christ, became citizens of the holy city, that is, of the Church. It will fall a second time, when on the last day it will be punished with everlasting death, which in this book is called the second death.

Revelation 18:2c And it became a dwelling place of demons.

Were demons not dwelling in Babylon before the coming of Christ? They certainly were, but their dwelling was unknown. However, when the multitude of the elect was converted to the faith, Babylon became known as the dwelling place of demons, because those who accepted the faith of Christ truly came to know that the dwelling of demons had always been in Babylon.

Revelation 18:2d And a prison of every unclean spirit, and a prison of every unclean and hateful bird.

Those who are demons are the same as the unclean spirits and the unclean birds. They are called “birds” because, like the winds, they ceaselessly move through this air; and they are called the guardians of Babylon for this reason: just as the angels guard God’s elect so that they do not sin so gravely as to perish, so also the demons watch over the wicked, lest they ever come to repentance from their sins and be saved.

Revelation 18:3 Because all the nations have drunk of the wine of her wrath, and the kings of the earth have committed fornication with her, and the merchants of the earth have become rich by the power of her luxuries.

What the wine of wrath is, and what the fornication of the kings with Babylon is, however, we have briefly said above. By the merchants of the earth we can understand the lovers of this world, who hand over their souls to destruction for the gaining of money. Of merchants of this sort Jesus, son of Sirach, speaks, saying: For this man has made his soul for sale; for in his lifetime he cast forth his innermost being (Sirach 10:10). He has made his soul for sale, who did not fear to hand it over to demons for the gaining of money; and he calls the luxuries the strength of Babylon, because just as the elect work with all their strength to acquire heavenly riches, so also the wicked strive with all their efforts to heap up for themselves riches that will perish. Of such people the prophet says: They have labored to act wickedly (Jer. 9:5).

Revelation 18:4a And I heard another voice from heaven saying.

By heaven in this place the Church is signified; the voice from heaven is the voice of preachers proceeding from the Church. But what does this voice say?

Revelation 18:4b Go out from her, my people.

Through his preachers the Lord urges the elect to go out from Babylon, not in body, but in mind. How, however, they ought to go out from Babylon he teaches in what follows, saying:

Revelation 18:4c And do not be partakers in her sins;

For they suitably go out from Babylon who make themselves strangers to her evil works, just as the Psalmist speaks about himself, saying: I have not sat in the council of vanity, and with those who act wickedly I have not entered (Ps. 25:4).

Revelation 18:4d And do not receive her plagues.

What are the plagues of Babylon, if not the vices that kill souls? We can also understand by the plagues of Babylon the punishments that have been prepared for the wicked, so that the meaning is: If you abstain from the crimes of Babylon, you will be strangers to the punishments that she will suffer eternally.

Revelation 18:5 For her sins have reached all the way to heaven, and God has remembered her iniquities.

The Almighty God always beholds the iniquities of Babylon; but when He does not exact vengeance, He appears not to see what He sees. By this, therefore, what he says points to that time in which all the wicked will be punished with eternal torment.

Revelation 18:6a Render to her as she herself has rendered to you.

The chosen are commanded to repay Babylon as she has repaid them, either because the saints of God will judge the wicked, as the Lord says in the Gospel: “In the renewal of all things, when the Son of Man sits on the throne of his majesty, you also will sit on twelve thrones, judging the twelve tribes of Israel” (Matt. 19:28); or because they themselves are members of him to whom the power of judging has been handed over by the Father. Therefore the righteous will repay the wicked with the evils they suffered from them, when Christ, who is their head and of whom they are members, condemns them to everlasting punishment.

Revelation 18:6b And repay her double, twice over, according to her works.

Babylon persecuted God’s chosen in a twofold way, when she inflicted many evils on their bodies and on their souls. She herself will receive punishments in a twofold way, because bodies will be tormented together with souls; and her “double” will itself be doubled, because for the temporal and brief punishments with which she afflicted the righteous, she will receive eternal punishments.

Revelation 18:6c In the cup that she mixed for you, mix for her double.

By the cup we can understand the tribulations that the saints suffered from the wicked.

Revelation 18:7a As much as she has glorified herself and lived in luxury, give her that much torment and sorrow.

As much as she has glorified herself, that is, raised herself up in pride and entangled herself in every vice, by that much she will also receive greater torments.

Revelation 18:7b For she says in her heart: “I sit as queen, I am no widow, and I shall not see mourning.”

Babylon in a certain way calls herself queen when she exalts herself in the prosperity of this world. She says she is not a widow, because she is joined to temporal goods and nourishes her own luxury from them, and trusts that she cannot be separated from them. She says she will not see mourning, because the wicked, although they know that they are to be punished for their crimes, pretend not to see what they see, and drive the memory of death from their mind, so that sorrowful reflection may not disturb the joy of their heart.

Revelation 18:8a Therefore in one day her plagues will come: death, and mourning, and famine.

On the day of judgment, everlasting death will come upon her, which in this book is called the second death. Mourning will come upon her, as the Lord says in the Gospel: There will be weeping and gnashing of teeth (Matt. 8:12). Famine will come upon her, that is, the famine of rest; for they will long to find rest from the punishments they endure, but they will by no means attain to that rest.

Revelation 18:8b And it will be burned with fire;

That is, with eternal fire.

Revelation 18:8c-10 For the Lord is mighty, who will judge her. And the kings of the earth who committed fornication with her and lived in luxury with her will weep and mourn over her when they see the smoke of her burning, standing far off because of fear of her torments, saying: “Alas, alas, that city Babylon, that mighty city; for in one hour your judgment has come!”

What the fornication of the kings with Babylon is, we explained above; (Rev. 17:2) but it is very strange that they are said to stand far off, when they themselves are to be plunged into the same punishments as the other wicked, who are signified through Babylon. Therefore they will stand far off in mind and will, not in body; shuddering at their own crimes, for which they will be eternally tortured. They will stand far off in their desire, wanting something different, that is, rest; and undergoing something different, that is, eternal torments.

Revelation 18:11 And the merchants of the earth will weep and mourn over her, because no one will buy their merchandise any more.

Since above we said that by the merchants of the earth are meant the lovers of this world, (Rev. 18:3) in this place we can understand by merchants those who sell the good that they seem to do. For there are many to whom knowledge of secular laws has been given, who carry the cases of the poor in courts of law; but because they do this not out of love for God, but for the reception of rewards, they do not receive payment from God. This misery has indeed grown so strong that you can hardly find anyone among those who have influence with rulers who will utter any word to support the need of a poor neighbor without receiving a reward. We can also, by the merchants of the earth, understand those who seek praise from men for the good that they do. And by the same, we can understand those who sell ecclesiastical ranks: something that divine Scripture strongly condemns. And although many bishops may seem free from this crime, yet their ministers are often defiled by it; but it is to the teachers that whatever is committed by the disciples is referred. There is also another very wicked crime, committed by those who are called archdeacons; for they accept payment from adulterous priests and, by keeping silent, consent to the evil which they could correct by the authority they have received from the bishops. There are also others from among such priests who, fearing that they will be condemned for their crimes, are more diligent in the services of archdeacons than the others; and if anyone wishes to speak any contrary word against them, they are defended by these archdeacons; and they pretend not to know what they do know, and therefore fall under that prophetic sentence which says: “Woe to those who call evil good, and good evil; who put darkness for light, and light for darkness” (Isa. 5:20)! To you, then, I speak, who are such a person: behold, you have sold the soul of a priest to the Devil. For who persuaded the priest to commit fornication, if not the Devil? And the one who persuaded him to fornicate is the same who persuaded him to give you payment, so that he might be able to commit this crime without fear. Through the hand of the priest, therefore, the Devil has given you the price of his soul’s destruction. Behold, you have received the price, and you have delivered the soul of your brother, redeemed by the blood of Christ, back to the same Devil from whom it was redeemed. In the Law it is written that if anyone sells a man who is his brother, he shall be put to death (Exod. 21:16). If, then, he is guilty who sells a man to another man, how much more guilty are you, who have sold a man redeemed by the blood of Christ, not to a man but, what is far more damaging, to the Devil! Such merchants will therefore weep and mourn in hell [inferno], because no one will buy their merchandise anymore; for they will lose what they seemed to hold and will fall into the evil which they neglected to foresee.

Revelation 18:12-14 The merchandise of gold, and silver, and precious stone, and pearl, and fine linen, and purple, and silk, and scarlet, and every kind of thyine wood, and all vessels of ivory, and all vessels of precious stone, and of bronze, and of iron, and of marble, and cinnamon, and perfumes, and ointment, and incense, and wine, and oil, and fine flour, and wheat, and beasts of burden, and sheep, and horses, and chariots, and slaves, and human souls: and your fruits, the desires of your soul, have departed from you, and all things rich and splendid have perished from you, and merchants of these things will find them no more.

In all the things that are listed, the greed of Babylon and its luxury are condemned. For in the judgment the Lord will not say to the wicked: “Go, accursed, into eternal fire, because you were murderers, proud, fornicators, adulterers;” (Matt. 25:41; Rev. 21:8; 1 Cor. 6:9-10) but he will say: “I was hungry, and you did not give me to eat; I was thirsty, and you did not give me to drink;” (Matt. 25:42) and the other works of mercy, from which greed holds back the wicked, so that they are not merciful toward the poor. For greed is indeed the nurse of luxury; and the nourishments of luxury are described in this place, by which it is fed by greed. And if we are willing to look closely, we will find that all the things which in this passage are listed to show the greed and luxury of Babylon belong to the five senses of the body. For there are five bodily senses, that is, sight, hearing, taste, smell, and touch, which God gave to humans and animals, to animals only for the preservation of their life, but to humans both for preserving life and so that through them reason might be able to lead the soul that is entrusted to it along the way of righteousness. Therefore the righteous, who have tried to walk by these five bodily senses according to the straight rule of reason, have gained for themselves the friendship of almighty God; but the wicked, who, turning the good of reason into evil, have forced these bodily senses to serve luxury and the other vices, have likewise experienced the wrath of the just Judge. Therefore, if you wish to connect with these the things that are written below, namely, the sound of harpists and musicians, and flute-players and trumpets, you will find that all the riches of Babylon described here belong, as we said above, to the five bodily senses. For gold, and silver, and precious stones, and pearls belong to sight; fine linen, and purple, and scarlet, and the rest, to touch; cinnamon, and perfumes, and ointments, and incense, to smell; wine, oil, fine flour, wheat, and the rest, to taste; the sound of harpists, and musicians, and flute-players belongs to hearing. The condemnation therefore of Babylon, as this Scripture shows, consists especially in riches; because riches are the nourishment of vices, and from them greed, which can now be called the queen of the world, feeds luxury, its daughter.

Revelation 18:15-17 Those who have become rich will stand far away from her because of fear of her torments, weeping and mourning, and saying: “Alas, alas, that great city, that was clothed in fine linen and purple and scarlet, and adorned with gold and precious stone and pearls; for in one hour such great riches have been brought to nothing!”

That the rich of Babylon are said to stand far away is to be understood according to the sense given above, which we explained about the kings.

Revelation 18:18-19 And every shipmaster and all who sail on the lake, and the sailors who work on the sea, stood far off and cried out when they saw the place of her burning, saying: “What city is like this great city?” And they cast dust on their heads, weeping and mourning, and saying: “Alas, alas, that great city, in which all who have ships on the sea became rich by her prices; for in a single hour she has been laid waste!”

By the sea we can understand this present age; by the shipmasters, in fact, we can understand wicked rulers; and by those who sail from place to place, and by the sailors who work on the sea, we can understand those who carry out various tasks in the world for the plundering of the poor and for the daily increase of their sins. These, then, will weep and mourn when they see the place of Babylon’s burning; for they themselves will be Babylon, and they themselves will mourn and weep. Moreover, they will see the burnings of Babylon because, by enduring their own burnings, they will see what, as people set in the world, they neglected to perceive with the mind so that they might correct themselves. These people are also said to have cast ashes on their heads; for often the mind is signified by the head, and sins by ashes. Therefore they will place ashes upon their heads, because the memory of sins will never depart from their minds.

Revelation 18:20 Rejoice over her, heaven, and holy apostles and prophets; for God has carried out your judgment on her.

By “heaven” we can understand the angels; by “apostles,” the fathers of the New Testament; and by “prophets,” the fathers of the Old Testament. They will therefore rejoice when every band of the wicked has been destroyed together with the demons themselves, the authors of iniquities. They will also rejoice because then all wickedness will come to an end, and everlasting justice will be brought forth.

Revelation 18:21 And one mighty angel took up a stone like a great millstone and cast it into the sea, saying: With this violence shall great Babylon be thrown down, and shall be found no more.

This angel signifies Christ, who is said to be mighty, because the human mind cannot comprehend how great the strength of Christ is. By the great millstone every multitude of the wicked is represented; and the sea signifies hell [infernum], into which all the wicked will be plunged. Therefore the angel cast the millstone into the sea, because our Lord Jesus Christ, on the day of judgment, by his just judgment will plunge the whole multitude of the wicked into hell [infernum].

Revelation 18:22a And the sound of harpists, and musicians, and flute players and trumpets will no longer be heard in you.

This was mentioned above.

Revelation 18:22b-23a And every craftsman, and every skill will no longer be found in you, and the sound of the mill will no longer be heard in you, and the light of the lamp will no longer shine in you, and the voice of bridegroom and bride will no longer be heard in you anymore.

We must ask why it counts crafts, and the sound of the mill, and the light of the lamp, and the voice of bridegroom and bride among the crimes of Babylon; since all these things can be practiced without guilt, and without them human life cannot exist. Therefore all these things must be understood spiritually. We can, then, understand by “crafts” the wisdom of this age, which is foolishness before God (1 Cor. 3:19), which wicked craftsmen and sons of Babylon learn for this purpose, that they may be able to advance in the wickedness of Babylon; for they are wise to do evil, but they did not know how to do good (Jer. 4:22). Now a mill, whatever it receives beneath it, crushes and grinds: we can therefore take the mill to mean wicked judges. Therefore the mill sends forth a sound when wicked judges bring forth unjust judgments to crush the innocent. By the light of the lamp, the glory of this age is indicated: of this lamp Scripture says, “The lamp of the wicked will be extinguished” (Prov. 24:20). Indeed, the lamp of the wicked is extinguished when, taken away from this life, they lose their temporal joy and suffer eternal sorrow. Nevertheless, we can understand by the bridegroom the lovers of this world, and by the bride the earth; because just as love binds bridegroom and bride so that they become one flesh, so also the love of the world joins its lovers to the earth in such a way that they become one with it, from which, if it were possible, they would never wish to be separated forever. Such craftsmen, then, will no longer be heard in Babylon; because with it they will be condemned forever.

Revelation 18:23b For your merchants were the princes of the earth.

The Church has her merchants, and Babylon has hers: the merchants of the Church store up their treasures in heaven, where thieves do not dig through and do not steal (Matt. 6:19); but the merchants of Babylon store up their treasures on earth, where thieves dig through and steal.

Revelation 18:23c And in your sorceries all nations went astray.

The sorceries of Babylon are the various errors by which humanity has been deceived.

Revelation 18:24 And in it was found the blood of the prophets and of the saints, and of all who were slain upon the earth.

The blood of the saints was found in it, that is, the crime of the killing of the saints, just as the Lord says to the wicked Jews, with the understanding of all the wicked: “All the righteous blood that has been shed upon the earth shall come upon you” (Matt. 23:35).

Chapter 19

Revelation 19:1-8 And I heard as it were a great voice of many trumpets in heaven, saying: Alleluia, salvation and glory and power belong to our God, because his judgments are true and just; for he has judged the great harlot who corrupted the earth with her prostitution, and has avenged the blood of his saints at her hand. And again they said: Alleluia; and her smoke goes up for ever and ever. And the twenty-four elders fell on their faces, and the four living creatures, and they worshiped God who sits on the throne, saying: Amen, alleluia. And a great voice came out from the throne, saying: Give praise to our God, all you his saints, and you who fear him, small and great. And I heard, as it were, the voice of a great trumpet, and as the voice of many waters, and as the voice of mighty thunders, saying: Alleluia, for the Lord our God the almighty has reigned: let us rejoice and exult and give him glory; for the marriage of the Lamb has come, and his bride has prepared herself. And it was granted her to clothe herself with shining, pure fine linen; for the fine linen is the righteous deeds of the saints.

I have set out the whole passage, so that, with its detail examined from every side, it may be more easily investigated. For it is read that “alleluia” is chanted five times by seven voices: first and second by the voice of many trumpets; third by the twenty‑four elders and the four living creatures; fourth by a voice that went forth from the throne, where no longer “alleluia” itself, but its interpretation is chanted, that is: “Say praise to our God, all you his saints”; for “alleluia” indeed sounds as “Praise the Lord.” But fifth it is chanted once by three voices, that is, by the voice of the great trumpet, and by the voice of many waters, and by the voice of mighty thunders. The Scripture of this book often shows that the whole multitude of the elect is to be divided into seven parts, if its meaning is examined with greater detail. Therefore the righteous who were before the Flood belong to the first part; those who were after the Flood until the Law was given belong to the second, as we have already shown often; and they had no other law except the natural one, that is, the love of God and of neighbor. For each person knows that he ought to fear and, above all, to love him by whom he was created and by whom life and the supports of life are given to him; and that he ought not to do to anyone what he does not want to suffer from another. No one who is of sound mind is allowed to be ignorant of this law. By the many trumpets, therefore, are signified the elect who were before the Flood and those who were after the Flood up to that time when the Law was given. They have uttered a voice, because by the teaching of their preaching they recalled, as many as they could, from their errors. They have chanted “alleluia,” because, according to the knowledge of the natural law, they strove by good works to please God. They have said “alleluia” a second time, because those who were after the Flood, living according to the same law, followed their Creator by living righteously. But we must ask why, at the second proclamation of “alleluia,” it is said that the smoke of Babylon has gone up forever and ever. Reputation can be signified by “smoke.” We know that in the second part Noah and his sons are placed first, in whose times, by the waters of the Flood, humanity was destroyed. Afterwards, however, Sodom and Gomorrah and the other cities that clung to them were consumed by heavenly fire. Therefore the smoke of Babylon goes up forever and ever, because this report will never depart from the mouth of the faithful.

But in truth, by the twenty‑four elders, the fathers who lived under the Law are signified. To them are joined the four living creatures, through which the New Testament is prefigured; because in the Old Testament, if it is understood spiritually, the New is contained. They chanted “Alleluia,” because, observing the legal commandments, they strove by good works to please God. By the throne, however, the prophets are signified, in that they were the seat of God, and through them he arranged his judgments. A voice went out from the throne, because from the prophets holy preaching proceeded. They sang praise to God, because they strove to serve him with all their strength. The great trumpet signifies Christ: the voice of the trumpet is the preaching of the Gospel; and by the voice of many waters is signified the multitude of nations confessing Christ; by the voice of great thunder are understood the elect who are to be born at the end of the world, and rightly by thunder, because they will announce the terror of the day of judgment. Therefore three voices once chanted “Alleluia,” because they have preserved, and will preserve until the end, the faith of the Holy Trinity and the doctrine of the Gospel, which proceeded from the great trumpet, that is, from Christ. Two parts, that is, the first and the second, belong to the law which we have said is natural; the two which follow, that is, the third and the fourth, to the law which was given through Moses; but the three which remain, that is, the fifth and the sixth and the seventh, to the law of the Gospel, so that they show that those men ought to remain in the faith of the Holy Trinity until the end of the world.

But let us hear what these three parts sing: Alleluia, for the Lord our God the Almighty has reigned; that is, let us praise the Lord; because, with the kingdom of the Devil, our destroyer, having been overthrown, he consents to reign in us, he who is our Lord and Savior. Let us rejoice and exult, and give glory to him; for the marriage of the Lamb has come, and his wife has prepared herself. Therefore let us rejoice, therefore let us exult, because by the mercy of our Creator he has consented so to join to himself his Church, which is we ourselves, as a bride is joined to her bridegroom; so that we may be with him one flesh, as Scripture says: The two shall be in one flesh (Gen. 2:24; Eph. 5:32); which the Apostle taught must be understood of Christ and the Church. His wife has prepared herself, that is, the Church, which is called the wife of the Lamb, has adorned herself with various virtues. And it was given to her that she should clothe herself in shining, white linen. What linen signifies, he himself explains: For linen is the justifications of the saints.

Revelation 19:9a And he said to me: Write: blessed are those who are called to the marriage supper of the Lamb.

The meal of the chosen is now, when in the Church they are nourished with the banquets of the Gospel; but the supper will be then, when after the resurrection they will enjoy eternal glory in heavenly blessedness. And because the meal which is now celebrated receives both the good and the bad, not all are blessed who seem to recline at it; but only those are blessed who will be received at that supper which is to be after the end of the world.

Revelation 19:9b-10 And he said to me: These words of God are true. And I fell at his feet to worship him. And he said to me: See that you do not do this; I am a fellow servant with you, and with your brothers who have the testimony of Jesus; worship God.

The redemption by the blood of Christ has granted this to the faithful, that they should be made sons of God and companions of the angels; and therefore the angel is afraid to be worshiped by a man, when above himself he worshiped a man who is God. For this reason he calls himself the fellow servant of John and of the other faithful. The testimony of Jesus is the spirit of prophecy; moreover, it is absolutely certain that the fathers of the Old Testament, who foretold the coming of Christ, his passion, and his resurrection, possessed the spirit of prophecy, and therefore they are called prophets. Therefore, whoever bears witness to Christ has the spirit of prophecy.

Revelation 19:11a And I saw heaven opened, and behold, a white horse, and the one who sat upon it was called faithful and true.

We must in no way suppose that this chapter refers to the coming of Christ in which he is to come for judgment; but rather to the elect who are to be born at the end of the world. For when the end of the world is at hand, the Lord will come in his saints, so that through them he may fight against Antichrist and his ministers. And as he will be preached by those saints, so also his form is described in this place; for by “heaven” the Church is signified, by the white horse the man whom the Son of God assumed, upon whom he was also seen to sit. He is also said to be called faithful and true. These two names, that is, faithful and true, have one sense in this place. Rightly is the Son of God called faithful, of whom the Psalmist says: “The Lord is faithful in all his words, and holy in all his works” (Ps. 144:13). He calls himself true in the Gospel, saying: “I am the way, and the truth, and the life” (Jn. 14:6). It continues:

Revelation 19:11b And with justice he judges and fights.

God will not contend against the wicked with weapons on the day of judgment, but by his justice they will be overcome and crushed; for the Lord will bring all the sins and crimes of the wicked before their eyes, so that they may know that they are condemned by the most just judgment of God.

Revelation 19:12a His eyes like a flame of fire, and on his head many crowns.

By the eyes of the Lord the Spirit of God is signified, as in the third vision the author of this book explains: “The Lamb,” he says, “had seven eyes, which are the seven spirits of God, sent into all the earth.” (Rev. 5:6) And because flame burns and gives light, it is not unfitting that the Holy Spirit is said to be burning, since he both enlightens the hearts of the faithful and inflames them with love for God. And on his head many crowns. By the many crowns the multitude of the saints is expressed.

Revelation 19:12b Having a name written which no one knows except himself.

Since above he says that he was called Faithful and True, and shortly after that he was called by the name Word of God, what does it mean that he says no one knows his name except himself? Indeed the saints of God both know and do not know the name of Christ, which is Son of God. For they know in part and in part they do not know; for in all creatures which beget and are begotten, no son can be of the same age as their father; because it is necessary that the one who begets precede, and the one who is begotten follow after. But such is not the nature of the Son of God; for just as the Father is without beginning, so also is the Son. And although we cannot comprehend how this can be, yet in truth we believe and confess that the Son is in all things equal and same age as the Father. Of this his generation the prophet speaks, saying: “Who shall declare his generation?” (Isa. 53:8).

Revelation 19:13 And he was clothed with a garment sprinkled with blood, and his name was called the Word of God.

By the garment we can understand the body of Christ; by the blood, however, our sins for which he died, or even the very death that he endured for us. But because by dying he wiped out the sins of his elect, and by rising he trampled down death itself, as the Apostle Paul says: “Christ, rising from the dead, now no longer dies; death shall no longer have dominion over him” (Rom. 6:9), it seems better to me that by the garment of Christ we understand the multitude of the saints, who are his members; and by the drops of blood with which the garment is read to be sprinkled, the martyrs are signified, who did not hesitate to pour out their blood for love of Christ; and therefore in that heavenly blessedness they adorn with their splendor the garment of Christ, which consists of angels and holy men.

Revelation 19:14 And the armies that are in heaven were following him on white horses, clothed in fine white pure linen.

We said above that as Christ will be preached by the saints at the end of the world, so also his image is described in this place. Therefore, by “heaven” in this passage the Church is understood; by the “armies” who were seen to have come forth from heaven following Christ, the saints who will be born at the end of the world and will fight against the Antichrist are understood; by the horses, bodies; and by the riders of the horses, souls are indicated. They were seen to have white horses because they will present themselves as pure and shining, as far as human weakness allows, in the sight of God. What the fine linen is, he explains above (Rev. 19:8): “Fine linen,” he says, “is the righteous deeds of the saints.”

Revelation 19:15a And a sharp sword went out of his mouth.17

By the mouth of the Lord we can understand those same preachers, of whom we have spoken, who are to be born at the end of the world, through whose mouth God will speak, as he himself said in the Gospel: For it is not you who speak, but the Spirit of your Father who speaks in you (Matt. 10:20). And he himself says through the prophet: If you separate the precious from the vile, you shall be as my mouth (Jer. 15:19). By the sword, however, as we have said above, we must understand the divine Scripture, as the apostle Paul says: And the sword of the Spirit, which is the word of God (Eph. 6:17). This is said to be sharp, because divine Scripture is most sharp for cutting away vices.

Revelation 19:15b So that with it he may strike the nations.

Christ will strike the nations with his sword, when through the teaching of his holy ones he destroys the errors of foolish men.

Revelation 19:15c And he himself shall rule them with an iron rod; And they shall be broken like a potter’s vessel. (Ps. 2:9)18

By the iron rod we have said the Gospel is signified; therefore Christ will rule the nations with an iron rod, when through the teaching of his saints he converts the Jews to faith in him and protects the elect from the errors of Antichrist. And they shall be broken like a clay vessel. As long as anyone lies in sins, he is rightly called a clay vessel; but when through penance and tears he has turned to doing good works, the clay vessel is broken, and from a clay vessel he becomes a solid and firm vessel, and very strong against the blows of the temptations of demons.

Revelation 19:15d And he himself will tread the winepress of the wine of the fury of the wrath of God Almighty.

Isaiah, when he was speaking of the passion of the Lord, introduced the Lord saying: I have trodden the winepress alone, and of the nations there is no man with me (Isa. 63:3). But there, by the winepress the cross of the Lord is understood; here, however, not the cross, but the place of punishments into which the wicked are to be thrust is signified. For the winepress in this place has the same meaning which we said above the lake had, into which the clusters of the vineyard of the earth, by which the wicked are signified, are read to have been plunged. And the very treading of the winepress has the same meaning as there we said the treading of the lake had; for it signifies the final plunging of the wicked into hell [infernum]. But what the wine of the fury of the wrath of God Almighty is, we have said above (in chapter 14).

Revelation 19:16 And he had written on his garment and on his thigh: King of kings and Lord of lords.

By the garment, as we said above, the saints are meant; by the thigh, from which human lineage is said to proceed, the man whom Christ assumed is signified. Therefore he had written on his garment that he himself is King of kings, because in the works of the saints it is recognized that this is the true God, who has such servants. He also has written on his thigh that he himself is King of kings and Lord of lords; because in the works which he carried out while placed in the flesh, it has been known that this is the true Son of God. We can also understand by the thigh the fathers of the Old Testament, from whose lineage Christ took his origin: they both prefigured him and plainly foretold, with open words, that he would be the King of kings and Lord of lords.

Revelation 19:17a And I saw another angel standing in the sun, and he cried with a loud voice to all the birds that were flying in mid‑heaven.

This angel signifies the preachers who will be in the end of the world, who are also indicated a little above by the horsemen; by the sun, however, we must understand Christ. Therefore the angel stood in the sun, because the holy preachers will be so established in Christ that by no force of persecutions can they be torn away from his faith. By the birds, moreover, all the faithful who will exist at that time are signified: they will fly through heaven, because, though placed on earth, they will dwell in mind on heavenly things. Nonetheless, we can understand the Church by “heaven.” The saints fly through heaven because, having two wings, that is, the two commandments of love, they fly everywhere, by showing mercy to some and by calling others back from iniquities.

Revelation 19:17b-18 Come, be gathered to the great supper of God Almighty, that you may eat the flesh of kings, and the flesh of tribunes, and the flesh of the strong, and the flesh of horses, and of those who sit on them, and the flesh of the free and of the slaves, of the small and of the great.

Those who are indicated by kings and princes, by free and slave, and by the rest, are the horses themselves and their riders, that is, the princes of the earth and the peoples who will be subjected to them. But what is the supper of God, if not the extermination of the wicked? The righteous will eat the flesh of the wicked when they receive vengeance for the evils which the wicked have inflicted on them. And because the righteous, seeing the punishment of the wicked, advance in good works, as the Psalmist says: The righteous will rejoice when he sees vengeance; he will wash his hands in the blood of the sinner (Ps. 57:11).

Revelation 19:19 And I saw the beast, and the kings of the earth, and their armies gathered together to make war with him who sat upon the horse, and with his army.

Here it shows that this does not refer to the coming of the Lord according to what has been said above, but rather to that time when he will do battle through his saints against Antichrist, as we have already said. For the beasts, and the kings of the earth, and their armies will be gathered together to fight against Christ and his saints, when Antichrist and the countless multitude of nations who will be deceived by him rise up to persecute the Church of God, in order to extinguish utterly the name of Christ from the world.

Revelation 19:20-21 And the beast was seized, and the false prophet, who performed signs in its presence, by which he deceived those who had received the mark of the beast and who had worshiped its image: the two of them were cast alive into the lake of fire burning with sulfur, and the rest were killed by the sword of the one sitting on the horse, which went forth from his mouth, and all the birds were filled with their flesh.

The beast and its false prophet will be seized when that is fulfilled which the Apostle Paul says: “Whom the Lord Jesus will kill with the breath of his mouth and will destroy with the brightness of his coming” (2 Thess. 2:8). If Antichrist is to be killed by the breath of the Lord’s mouth, how are he and his false prophet to be cast alive into the lake of fire? There is a life of the righteous, and there is a life of the wicked. Of the life of the righteous the prophet says that “the righteous will live by his faith” (Hab. 2:4); but of the wicked widow Paul says that “though she lives, she is dead” (1 Tim. 5:6). Indeed, the righteous do not inappropriately call the life of the wicked “death”; likewise the wicked regard the life of the righteous as “death,” as they will say in the judgment: “These are the ones we once held in ridicule and as a pattern of reproach; we foolish men thought their life was madness, and their end without honor” (Wisdom 5:4). Therefore Antichrist and his false prophet are cast alive into the lake of fire, that is, alive in their wickedness, persevering in it, namely, until the end of their life. But when it says that the rest were killed by the sword of the one sitting on the horse, it has put this killing for their shame; for the saints, whose teaching is represented by the sword of the Lord’s mouth, after Antichrist has been slain, will mock the unbelievers because they had considered Antichrist, a most wicked man and worse than all other men, to be immortal and a god, whereas it will be clear that he has been destroyed by a most wretched death; and they will be confused and waste away in their iniquities. Therefore all the birds were filled with their flesh, because when Antichrist is overthrown, all the elect will rejoice and exult beyond telling, because by divine mercy they will have been freed from such an evil enemy, and all those who persecuted them will have vanished like smoke.

Chapter 20

Revelation 20:1 And I saw an angel coming down from heaven, having the key of the abyss and a great chain in his hand.

Wishing to recount the damnation of the Devil, he goes back to the coming of Christ. For there are three damnations of the Devil: he was damned first when, because of his iniquity, he was cast down from heavenly blessedness; his second damnation was the one about which the author of this book now begins to speak, when, through the death of Christ, humanity was snatched from his power, and he himself, as the present Scripture shows, was bound in the abyss; his third damnation will be when he is plunged into hell [infernum] to be tormented forever with all the wicked. Therefore this angel signifies Christ; his descent from heaven is his incarnation. He himself holds the key of the abyss, that is, of hell [inferni], because he draws back from its entrance whom he wills, and whom he wills he justly allows to fall into its damnation. By the great chain, moreover, his power is signified.

Revelation 20:2 And he seized the dragon, the ancient serpent, who is called the Devil and Satan, and bound him for a thousand years.

Christ seized the ancient serpent, that is, the Devil, when by his death he destroyed his dominion. By the thousand years, however, we should understand the whole period from Christ’s coming until the end of the world. Christ therefore bound the Devil not with physical chains, but with the chains of his immense power: casting him out of the hearts of his chosen ones, he keeps him, by his divine might, from deceiving them.

Revelation 20:3 And he cast him into the abyss, shut it, and sealed it over him, so that he might deceive the nations no longer until the thousand years were completed. After this, he must be released for a short time.

We can understand the binding of the Devil in three ways. We may understand it simply to mean that, by the power of almighty God, he is held bound in the abyss until the appointed time. We may also understand the abyss as the hearts of the wicked: excluded from the hearts of the elect, he is confined and held bound within the narrow confines of the hearts of the wicked. We may understand it in this third way as well. The Devil desires nothing other than to deceive people; and while, through the protection of almighty God, who never ceases to guard and defend his elect, he is kept from deceiving them, his most wicked will is held bound as if by chains. By the nations whom he is not permitted to deceive, we must understand the elect alone. The short time during which he is said to be released after the thousand years is the time of Antichrist. For then, because of people’s sins, the Devil will be granted the power to wage war against humanity with all his might.

Revelation 20:4a And I saw thrones, and they sat upon them, and judgment was given to them.

Since he has already related how divine mercy daily delivers His chosen from the most savage enemy, the Devil, it follows that the glory to which their souls are daily brought should now be described. By the thrones, then, the heavenly homeland is signified; by those seated upon them, the souls of the saints. That judgment was given to the saints whose souls rest in heavenly blessedness may be observed when we see demons driven out of possessed bodies at their tombs, perjuries often exposed, and many miracles performed.

Revelation 20:4b And the souls of those slain for the word of God and the testimony of Jesus, and those who did not worship the beast and its image, nor receive its mark on their foreheads or hands, lived and reigned with Christ for a thousand years.

By the souls of those slain for the word of God and the testimony of Jesus, we may understand the martyrs who were killed at various times. By those who did not worship the beast and its image, I think we should understand those who are to be killed by Antichrist. These, then, lived and reigned with Christ for a thousand years, because although they were killed in body, they nevertheless live with Christ in heavenly blessedness in soul. But we must ask how the elect who are to be born as the end of the world approaches are said to reign with Christ for a thousand years, since the thousand-year period refers not to the future, which is without end, but rather to the present, which will be brought to its completion at the end of the world, and during which the faith of the Gospel flourishes throughout the whole world, directing the chosen daily to achieve eternal life. I think this should be resolved as follows. The period from Christ’s Ascension until the end of the world, which is signified by a thousand years, has been given to all the chosen, so that during it they may enjoy heavenly glory with the Lord without their bodies. We know that all the chosen are members of Christ, and that many members form one body; and if one member rejoices, all the members rejoice with it. Therefore the joy of the saints who came before will also belong to those who are to be born at the end of the world, because they will form one body with them and, through the others, possess what they themselves lack.

Revelation 20:5a But the rest did not come to life until the thousand years were completed.

The rest, that is, the wicked, did not come to life, because after death they are not brought to the life allowed in heaven to the souls of the saints; instead, they are plunged into hell [infernum], which in this book is called the first death.

Revelation 20:5b-6a This is the first resurrection. Blessed is the one who has a share in the first resurrection; over such people the second death has no power.

We must ask what the first resurrection is, for he would not speak of a first resurrection unless there were also a second, nor of a second death unless there were also a first. Therefore we first rise from the death of the soul through baptism.
